# RbxTransport / WebTransport client — protocol findings

Findings from IDA (`libroblox.so` Android, Windows PE), packet captures of a live
native session, and live join-API experiments. Updated 2026-10-06.

## Join flow (works today)

1. Session: `GET https://users.roblox.com/v1/users/authenticated` (cookies included).
2. Join: `POST https://gamejoin.roblox.com/v2/join-game` with `Accept: text/event-stream`.
   - Body: `{placeId, gameId (universeId), isTeleport:false, gameJoinAttemptId, browserTrackerId,
     playSessionId, eventId, launchData:"", joinAttemptOrigin:"PlayButton"}`.
   - Reply is SSE; `event: ResponseReady` → JSON with `status:2` and **inline `joinScript`**.
   - v1 (`/v1/join-game`) also works but does not always inline the script; `joinScriptUrl`
     (`assetgame.roblox.com/Game/Join.ashx?...`) is single-use and expires quickly.
   - `status:12 "Unable to join Game 211"` was observed intermittently early on and again
     while another session of the same account was active; it resolved later and appears to
     be server-side throttling/eligibility rather than request shape. The (since removed)
     browser client sent the native default UA `Roblox/WinInet` for `*.roblox.com` API calls
     via a declarativeNetRequest `modifyHeaders` rule for fidelity; joins succeed with or
     without it.
   **NOTE (2026-10-27):** v2 is dead (HTTP 404
   `{"errors":[{"code":0,"message":""}]}`); `v1/join-game` is the working
   endpoint (inline `joinScript`, `status 2`). The status-12 stalling above was
   an unauthenticated request (cookie value sent *without* the
   `.ROBLOSECURITY=` name). Full re-check in "2026-10-27 — live join re-verified"
   below.
3. `joinScript` fields that matter:
   - `NetStackPort` — the RbxTransport/QUIC UDP port.
   - `NetStackConfig` — **percent-encoded JSON**:
     `{"pubKey": "...", "port": <NetStackPort>, "caps": "022dd3ef52ff0892",
       "certHashes": ["<base64 sha256>"]}`.
   - `UdmuxEndpoints: [{Address, Port}]` — public edge IP. The native client sends its UDP
     socket to `Address:Port` (the public/UDMUX endpoint, same numeric port as
     `ServerPort`/`ServerConnections[].Port`), while the RCC address and that same endpoint
     port are carried inside the RUPP TLVs for edge routing. `NetStackPort` is separate
     metadata (native logs it separately and does not use it as the UDP destination).
     We tested raw QUIC against both ports — both are silently dropped.
   - `ServerConnections: [{Address, Port}]` — datacenter-private (10.x) RCC address,
     unreachable from the internet.
   - `GameFqdn` — **currently always null** in production join replies (see below).
   - `TokenValue`, `NetStackTokenValue`, `ClientTicket` — auth material for the transport.

## Transport wire format (from captures of a live native client)

Every game-server UDP packet is **RUPP-wrapped QUIC**:

```
[01][00][u16 BE header_len]
  TLV1: 01 11 02 <16-byte token>
  TLV2: 02 06 <rcc IPv4 (4B)><rcc port (u16 BE)>
[QUIC packet ...]
```

- TLV2 carries the *private datacenter RCC address*; the udmux edge uses it for routing.
- RUPP parse code: `sub_646A4C4` / `sub_646A53C` (client + server).
- The RUPP token rotates during a session (`Switch to new TT token received from RCC`);
  generation goes through `sub_6469FAE` (RuppTokenClientLog2), which delegates to an
  RCC-provided generator object — the token is derived key material, not a fixed join field.
- RUPP init result codes: `0` and `12` both mean accepted (`RtcIoServerPrefixProcessor`),
  everything else drops with "Rupp init failed".
- The server-side accept path `sub_63CFE80`/`sub_63CFB42` also accepts packets with the QUIC
  fixed bit set (`0x40`) directly, skipping RUPP, when the RCC-side
  `RbxTransportPureQuicSupport` flag (`byte_77A01F8`) is on — this is the browser path.
- `DirectServerReturn: true` means replies come from the RCC directly, not via the udmux path.

### Additional native constants (cross-checked against Studio 0.741 RE notes)

- Server-side SNI grammar (`sub_646AD8D`): `{32-hex token}-{8-hex rccIpv4}-{4-hex rccPort}.{vip-hex}.qdmux.roblox.com`.
  Generated by `RbxTransport_PrepareConnectionConfig` (`DebugRbxTransportGenerateGameFqdn`,
  `{}-{}-{}.{}.qdmux.roblox.com`, with `DebugRbxTransportQdmuxVip` as the VIP override).
- Native initial destination CID shape: `0xd1 || rccIpv4 || rccPort u16 BE || 13-byte tail`
  (20 bytes total). Listener logs: `Accepted new connection ... ClientHello SNI='{}' ALPN='{}' caps={} compatMode={}`.
- ALPN in the native RbxTransport path is `RbxTransport` (Studio notes); a browser WebTransport
  can only offer `h3`, so the browser-serving mode must accept `h3` — the `compatMode` in the
  accept log is likely exactly that.
- `*.qdmux.roblox.com` is a delegated DNS zone (NOERROR/NODATA) but has **no records and no
  wildcard** as of 2026-10-06, so the SNI route cannot resolve from a browser yet either.

## Python client status: full native join sequence (2026-10-07)

The Python client now replays the complete native join sequence and gets the server's
full **1857-byte 0x93 join reply byte-identical to the native client's**. Remaining gate:
the server's 0x9B anti-cheat challenge exchange (see below).

### Verified join sequence (client → server, app=4, after QUIC handshake)

1. ctrl stream `app=4 chan=0x6374726C` opened; first message `00 00` ("ctrl hello")
2. `OpenUnreliable` ctrl msgs: `[02][04][chan u32be][wire u32be]` for (3,2), (5,4), (6,6), (11,8)
   (native interleaves these around the early-auth send)
3. stream `app=4 chan=1`: early-auth `a8 11 21 <33B pre> 42 <66B auth>` (from ClientTicket parts 2/3)
4. `0xA7` queue msg: `[a7][u16le count][count × 16B records]` (records are client script hashes; count varies per session)
5. `0x90` client-info msg: flags list + LEB len + JSON (see below)
6. `0x92` nonce: `[92] + LEB(zigzag64((v8<<32) | (v8 ^ 0x63E25F26)))` — v8 random u32
7. `0x8A` join request (see below)
8. `0x8F 00`

Server then sends (server → client):
- ctrl: `OpenUnreliable app=4 chans {3,5,6,11}` wires {3,5,7,9} (allocated fresh each session)
- stream `app=4 chan=1`: `0x93` flag-override message, 1857 B — **byte-identical to native**
- stream `app=6 chan=1`: (dummy/ping channel traffic)
- *native additionally receives* `0x9B` 1962 B challenge → replies 9-byte `0x9B` → "Assigned peer id" (replication starts)

### 0x8A (join request) — exact layout, validated against two captures

```
[0x8A]
LEB(zigzag64(UserId))                 # e.g. 0x22B16F1DE for user 4656429295
LEB(len(ClientTicket)) + ClientTicket # full "ts;b64;b64;b64;17"
u32le(36)
LEB(74) + "2e427f51c4dab762fe9e3471c6cfa1650841723b!6e8e47e92778f00efbb13c7bb151ea88."
LEB(7) + "Android"
LEB(1) + "?"
LEB(v31) + LEB((v31 - 0x0BADF00D) u32)  # v31 = obfuscated xxh32(ClientTicket, seed=1)
0x00                                  # empty string
LEB(len(SessionId-json)) + SessionId-json   # joinScript["SessionId"] verbatim
fe ca 01 c0                           # magic 0xC001CAFE (LE)
```

`v31` derivation (implemented in `py/rbx_client.py: ticket_v31`):
`v31 = (v17 ^ rol32(v29, v30))` where the intermediates are the OLLVM-mixed forms of
`xxh32(ticket, seed=1)` masked against the compile-time constant `S = dword_71941A0 = 0x71375635`
(values `1434170839` [= 0x5579F0D7] and shift/branch bits derived from `v8 = i16(-17506*S)`).
Validated on both captures (v31 == the first LEB value).

### 0x90 (client info) — rebuilt from our join reply

Template: start from a captured 0x90 (flags list prefix is static), replace the JSON with a
rebuild of our own, where only 3 fields are session-bound:
- `RandomSeed1`      ← joinScript["RandomSeed1"]
- `APIsecurityToken` ← joinScript["APIsecurityToken"]
- `__joinTicket`     ← JSON {"SerializedClientFields":…, "EncryptedServerFields":…}
                        both taken verbatim from join reply's `joinTicket`

JSON serialization with `json.dumps(..., separators=(",", ":"))` reproduces native byte-for-byte
(verified: 9302 B exact match).

### 0x9B challenge (the remaining gate)

Native flow: after the 0x93, server sends `0x9B` 1962 B:
`[9b][u32#1][u32#2][u32 len][len-byte blob]` (blob = 1949 B high-entropy data, likely
encrypted/compiled challenge payload). Client replies `[9b][u32#1][u32 answer]` (9 B), where
answer is computed by running the challenge through `GenericChallengeService` (`sub_435D53A`,
"challenge" lookup). It is validated as "Bytecode Auth Check" (`sub_32A0270` k1).

Observed states with our Python client (mocktail stopped):
- stale-ticket replays → server **resets the stream** (rc 0x106) — server validates the joinTicket
- rebuilt 0x90 + fresh 0x92 + our 8A → server sends the 0x93 but does **not** send the 0x9B
  challenge, so replication never starts
- sending **no A7** gets a different 0x93 variant (0x70f prefix, 1811 B) + a new 2-byte/short ctrl
  exchange — A7 affects the server's validation path but is not the challenge trigger
- the server's 0x9B challenge gate is not yet understood; likely tied to the 0x9B/bytecode check
  below, or to the A7 script-hash validation

### Why the browser cannot connect yet


- Chrome's WebTransport emits **raw QUIC**; browsers cannot prepend a RUPP header to UDP
  datagrams. The production edge (udmux) routes on RUPP and **silently drops** RUPP-less UDP.
- Verified experimentally over 2026-10-05/06: Chrome WebTransport and aioquic Initials to live
  `udmuxIP:NetStackPort` and `udmuxIP:ServerPort` (raw, native custom `0xd1` destination CIDs,
  qdmux SNI with the recovered grammar, `h3` and `RbxTransport` ALPNs, real join tokens where
  applicable) are transmitted (packet-captured) and receive **zero** bytes back — while the
  native client on the same machine/host/account plays fine with RUPP framing.
- Independent corroboration: the Studio 0.741 RbxTransport RE notes show the same wall — a
  native-shaped QUIC client still times out at the 10 s handshake budget even with qdmux SNI,
  native CIDs, and RUPP prefixes. This matches the web client not having shipped yet.
- The intended browser-facing edge is visible in the binary: `RbxTransport_PrepareConnectionConfig`
  generates `<token>-<rccIp>-<rccPort>.<vip>.qdmux.roblox.com` and the listener parses that SNI
  (`sub_646AD8D`). `qdmux.roblox.com` exists as a DNS zone but is not serving yet.

## WebTransport browser client (removed 2026-10-28)

The browser client (formerly `src/`, briefly `RbxWebTransport/`) and its codec
tests were deleted at the project owner's request. The notes below are kept as
the protocol record and as the blueprint for the planned native
`rbxwebtransport` connection type (HTTP/3 via ngtcp2), which will be implemented
when Roblox's web client is available to all games — the trigger is a non-null
`joinScript.GameFqdn`. Until then the client ships RakNet only, with the
archived RbxTransport available but not built.

- `RbxWebTransport/join.js` — auth, v1 join, joinScript parsing with
  percent-decoding of `NetStackConfig`, caps normalization (u64 → 16 lowercase hex),
  cert-hash extraction, endpoint selection (`GameFqdn` when present, else udmux address +
  `NetStackPort`).
- `RbxWebTransport/protocol.js` — varint/svarint, stream header `[06][01][appIndex][channelId u32 BE]`,
  varint-length message framing, ctrl messages, datagram header codecs.
- `RbxWebTransport/transport.js` — `WebTransport(url, {serverCertificateHashes})` with
  `https://<host>:<port>/wt?X-Rbx-Capabilities=<16hex>`, stream/datagram pumps, ctrl channel.
- `RbxWebTransport/client-headless.js` + `client-headless.html` — no UI; auto-runs auth → join →
  connect for place `1818`, logs to console and `window.__rbx`.
- `RbxWebTransport/rules.json` — sets the web `rbx-user-agent` (and User-Agent) for
  Roblox API calls made by extension pages only; `excludedInitiatorDomains` keeps the
  official web client's own requests untouched.

(These files lived in `src/` and the repo root, then briefly in a self-contained
`RbxWebTransport/` folder, until everything was deleted on 2026-10-28. Git
history still has them.)

## Next steps

1. Roblox's web client currently works only for enrolled universes. When it is available
   to all games, the plan is a native `rbxwebtransport` connection type over
   ngtcp2 + HTTP/3 (WebTransport), built alongside RakNet and the archived RbxTransport —
   not a browser extension.
2. Watch `joinScript.GameFqdn` for non-null values — that is the trigger to start it.
3. The protocol notes above (stream header, framing, ctrl and datagram codecs) are the
   spec for that work; the deleted browser implementation is still in git history.

## NOTE (2026-10-08): the 0x9B gate is SOLVED — see ## BREAKTHROUGH near the bottom.

## Where the Python client stands (end of 2026-10-07 session, part 2)

**Working, verified live:**
- full native join: RUPP+QUIC, app=4 channels, early-auth, A7 → 90 → 92 → 8A → 8F
- server sends the full 1857-byte 0x93 join reply (byte-identical to the native's)
- **with an EMPTY A7 the server sends the 0x9B anti-cheat challenge** (1962–1978 B on
  app=4 chan=1): `[9b][u1 u32][u2 u32][len u32][blob]`
- the server accepts a *correct* answer: in an early native run the answer produced
  by the native for our patched challenge values got **"Assigned peer id = 44"**
  (replication start) on the native's own connection

**The one remaining unknown — what a correct answer does for a Python client:**
- The challenge `blob` is **constant per server build** (verified byte-identical across
  servers with equal message size); `u1`/`u2` are per-session.
- The native answers 9 B `[0x9b][u2][answer]` within ~15 ms; the answer is computed
  by the "challenge" provider (Luau VM: `sub_435D53A` → `sub_435D892` → `sub_435E556`
  → `sub_274DF88`), not by any simple hash of blob/u1/u2 (extensively tested: md5/sha/
  blake/xxh32/crc32/adler32 of many combinations, XOR/add relations, linear fit, ChaCha20
  decrypts, record reads — all negative).
- **Oracle solution implemented and working**: with two accounts and a live native client
  on the same server (blob-checked), `tools/oracle_patch.gdb` patches the native's
  challenge u1/u2 to the Python client's values, captures the computed answer, and
  `py/probe10.py` sends it within ~3 ms.
- Even with the correct answer sent in 3 ms, the server **resets the chan-1 stream
  (app error 0x106) ~2 ms later** — identical to the reset timing when no answer is sent,
  and identical to what a mismatched-blob answer sees. The reset is therefore *not* an
  answer-timeout or answer-validation effect on the Python path; some other server-side
  check (session/integrity, e.g. the A7 script-hash queue it validated first, or
  connection metadata not reproduced by the pure-Python client) rejects the session
  either way.

**Reboot-safe layout (all final artifacts):**
- `py/probe5.py`, `py/probe7.py`, `py/probe8.py`, `py/probe9.py`, `py/probe10.py`
- `py/captures/` — 23 reference blobs (native join messages, server replies, challenges)
- `tools/oracle_patch.gdb`, `tools/oracle_final.sh`, `tools/oracle_master.py`,
  plus capture gdb scripts (`pairs`, `corr`, `a7_capture`, …)
- `tools/ida_addresses.md` — every function address used, against the matching IDB
- `run/` — cookies (`cookie.txt`, `cookie2.txt`, mode 600) and session logs
- `lib/sdl3ttf/` — the SDL3_ttf shim mocktail needs on this machine
  (`LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/home/john/RobloxInBrowser/lib/sdl3ttf/lib`)

**To iterate further:**
1. `bash tools/oracle_final.sh` — full pipeline: freeze native, retry Python client until
   its challenge blob matches the native's, answer via oracle, log outcome.
2. The likely remaining unlock is the A7 queue (client script hashes, `sub_34F1F08`),
   which the server validates before the challenge; reproducing it faithfully (fresh,
   matching the client's actual loaded-script state) is the highest-value next step.
3. Optional: instrument the native's post-answer behaviour more (what the server sends
   right after a *successful* answer) to find the exact check our session fails.

## Why the browser cannot connect yet


## Protocol implementation notes (do not regress)

- `0x8A` (join request): fully understood and rebuilt from our own ClientTicket +
  SessionId JSON (`build_8a` in `py/probe5.py`); its hash v31 = obfuscated
  xxh32(ClientTicket, seed=1) with the compile-time constant `0x71375635` — validated
  byte-for-byte against two independent captures.
- `0x90` (client info): must be rebuilt with OUR join reply's `joinTicket`
  (`SerializedClientFields` + `EncryptedServerFields`) and `RandomSeed1` +
  `APIsecurityToken` from OUR joinScript, **AND its 20-byte trailer must be preserved**
  (5×u32: `0xB1FB276E`, `0x51B1788E`, `E2`, `E1`, `0x228079AC`, where
  `0x63E25F26 = E2 ^ ((E1 & 0xFFFF) | 0x276E0000)`). `build_90` in `py/probe7.py`
  reproduces a captured 0x90 byte-for-byte (10713 B exact) from equivalent inputs.
- `0x92` nonce: `[92] + LEB(zigzag64((v8<<32) | (v8 ^ 0x63E25F26)))` with v8 a random u32.
- `0xA7` script-hash queue: `[a7][u16le count][count × 16-byte records]`; the records
  differ per session (client script state). The server validates them; an empty A7
  (`a7 00 00`) still gets the 0x9B challenge but never replication.
- Early auth: `a8 11 21 <33B> 42 <66B>` from ClientTicket parts 2/3 (base64-decoded).
- Server ctrl answers with app=4 OpenUnreliable channels {3,5,6,11} → wires {3,5,7,9}
  (allocation varies per session), then server-opened reliable streams for channels
  {0,1,2,4,7,8,9,10,12,13} (client sees stream headers `06 01 04 <chan u32be>`).
- The QUIC stream for the server's channel-1 data sometimes gets reset with app error
  `0x106` right after the 0x9B exchange; this is the server's final gate.
- **Only one client per account may be joined to the same place.** A second session on
  the same account causes stream resets that look like rejections — use a second account
  for parallel native+Python tests (`run/cookie.txt` / `run/cookie2.txt`).

```
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/home/john/RobloxInBrowser/lib/sdl3ttf/lib DISPLAY=:0 ./mocktail \
    [--launch-uri "roblox://placeId=1818"]
```
The `lib/sdl3ttf/lib/libSDL3_ttf.so.0` shim (copied from the flatpak runtime) is required;
it is preserved in this repo so a reboot does not break mocktail.

## BREAKTHROUGH (2026-10-08): the 0x9B answer is computed LOCALLY and MATCHES

The remaining gate is solved: we compute the challenge answer in pure local
software (no oracle) and it matches the native's captured answers exactly.

Verified matches (all answers standalone, no native in the loop):

| dataset | server (JobId) | native answer | our answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| run/comb_1 | 472bd6de-... | 0x9b328c67 | 0x9b328c67 |
| run/comb_2 | (session)   | 0xf94422bf | 0xf94422bf |
| run/comb_3 | (session)   | 0x652848ea | 0x652848ea |
| run/rounds/r1 | 23c2e471-... | 0x7167bb68 | 0x7167bb68 |
| run/full | e62f5d1f-... | 0x82bfe8be | 0x82bfe8be |

### How it works (all steps verified against native ground truth)

1. **Wire blob → standard Luau bytecode** (`tools/standardize_wire.py`):
   Roblox's loader (sub_679992E) parses the wire file, then remaps each
   instruction's opcode byte through two tables in libroblox.so:
     - `byte_CC2904` (VA 0xCC2904, runtime-verified identical): wire_op -> internal_op
     - `byte_CC2A04` (VA 0xCC2A04): internal_op -> STANDARD Luau opcode number
   Instruction length: `sub_27509DA(v)` returns 2 iff
     (v-7) in [0,0x3B] and bit set in the literal `0x829400003F02323`, or
     (v-74) in [0,0x10] and bit set in the literal `0x00017E7B`
   (the `mov ecx, offset byte_17E7B; bt ecx, edi` tests the address constant,
   not memory!). Tables are dumped in `tools/roblox_bc_tables.json`.
   Result: every proto walks cleanly; operands byte-identical to the native's
   own post-remap code dumps (`run/remap`, `run/comb`). 24/24 protos, 899 starts.

2. **The decoded program runs in upstream Luau**, using the separate runner
   project `rbx_runtime/` (links the pristine `third_party/luau` submodule,
   pinned; nothing in the third-party checkout is modified):
   - `Random` is an exact port of Roblox's PCG (sub_27B30AE / sub_2307554):
       state = M*seed + C with M=0x5851F42D4C957F2D, C=0x399D2694695129DE,
       next = M*state + 105, out32(s) = ror32((s>>27)^(s>>45), s>>59),
       NextInteger small path: lo + ((out32 * (range+1)) >> 32).
     **Seed = xv + yv as the FULL 64-bit sum of the two unsigned 32-bit
     challenge fields** (verified: recovered seed == u1+u2 exactly, including
     carries above 2^32, e.g. 0x14b5e5848 -> native state 0x7740cff9389a6686).
     RNG streams match the native 20/20 draws in every checked session.
   - The program's arguments arrive as `(u2, u1)` — i.e. the SECOND challenge
     field is the first program argument (`xv`), the first is `yv`. (Both
     orders were tested; only this one matches.)
   - `game.JobId`, `bit32`, `newproxy`, `getmetatable/setmetatable`, `xpcall`,
     `tostring`, `os.exit` are provided by `rbx_runtime/src/main.cpp`.

3. **Environment checks** (the xpcall block at the end of the program) must
   match the native sandbox. Fitted from the data (constant per client build):
   - UserSettings() both calls succeed; `tostring(UserSettings())` byte-sum = **1924**
     (stacked 4182 = 1924 + (1924+334); any string with this byte sum behaves
     identically, e.g. "a"*19 + "Q")
   - `os.exit` missing -> +9001
   - `RunService:IsStudio()` false -> +1024
   - newproxy `__namecall` works -> +52
   With these, all five datasets match exactly. (With defaults "nope"/missing/
   false/ok the computed answers were off by exactly 0xCF2 = 3314 — the
   UserSettings difference.)

### Run it yourself

```
# decode a captured wire program (wire.bin + optional proto_*.hex dumps)
python3 tools/standardize_wire.py run/comb_1 /tmp/decoded.luac

# compute the answer (args order: --u1=u2_field --u2=u1_field)
./rbx_runtime/build/challenge_runner \
    --program=/tmp/decoded.luac \
    --u1=0xcb047de0 --u2=0x8059da68 \
    --job=472bd6de-39e8-44f7-9fe9-de91abce9dc2 \
    --usersettings=ok --us-string=aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaQ \
    --os-exit=missing --studio=false --newproxy=ok
```

### Capture tooling (project-local, reboot-safe)

- `tools/combined_capture.gdb` + `tools/comb_round.sh` — one native join,
  captures in a single session: wire 0x9B message, decoded loader input,
  ALL post-remap proto streams, the full NextInteger stream, the sent answer,
  and the JobId. Output layout: `run/comb*/{wire.bin, wire_chal.bin,
  wire_ans.bin, proto_*.hex, comb.log, jobid.txt}`.
- `tools/rng_capture.gdb`, `tools/full_capture2.gdb`, etc. — focused variants.
- `tools/roblox_bc_tables.json` — the two opcode tables (dumped from IDA and
  verified against live process memory).
- `tools/opcode_map.py` — alignment helper (LCS) between native dumps and a
  reference compile; superseded by `standardize_wire.py` but kept for audits.

### ~~Still open~~ → SOLVED (2026-10-08): blob → program transform REVERSED

The wire blob transform (0x9B blob ~1950B → 7226B program) is fully static —
no session key. Reversed from `sub_274FA64` (libroblox challenge script loader):

1. **XOR keystream** from the blob's own first 4 bytes:
   - `key[i] = ((blob[i] ^ "RSB1"[i]) + [00,d7,ae,85][i]) & 0xFF`   (i < 4)
   - `plain[p] = blob[p] ^ ((41*p + key[p & 3]) & 0xFF)`   (all p)
   (native: xmmword_205C50="RSB1" xor, xmmword_2042D0 byte-add,
    per-16B-block `v6 += 0x90` == 41*16 mod 256; scalar tail `v11 += 41`)
2. **Validation**: the derived 4-byte key IS `xxhash32(plaintext, seed=0x2A)`
   (`sub_69FB2B0(v19, v26, 0x2A)` vs `v40`, mismatch → flag 0x40)
   ✅ passes on all 5 datasets
3. **Payload**: `u32 at [4:8]`: 0 → raw wire bytecode at [8:];
   else = uncompressed size, zstd frame (magic `28 b5 2f fd`) at [8:]
   (compared to `-47205080`, decompressed by `sub_6C2FB3A` = ZSTD_decompress)

Implemented in `tools/challenge_blob.py` (`decode_blob` / `blob_to_standard`;
pure-Python xxhash32 + libzstd via ctypes — no pip deps). Verified byte-exact:
decrypted+zstd output == native loader input on all 5 datasets.

**Complete autonomous path**: `0x9B blob → challenge_blob → standardize_wire →
challenge_runner → answer` — 2–7ms, no native/gdb/oracle anywhere.
`py/solve9b.py` wraps it (`solve_message(msg, job)`); `py/probe10.py
--source local` (default) answers live: challenge received → solve →
send `[9b][u2][answer]` on chan1.

### Exact file map for the solved challenge path

- `rbx_runtime/` — the Roblox scripting environment (links the pristine
  `third_party/luau` submodule, pinned at 74f76830 — built via
  add_subdirectory; the Luau checkout is NEVER modified). Modular API layout (2026-10-09; the
  "add an API" recipe is in `src/api/api.h`):
  - `src/main.cpp` — CLI only; args pushed as
    doubles of the unsigned challenge fields in (u2, u1) order.
  - `src/runner.{h,cpp}` — public API + execution (load -> call -> answer).
  - `src/sandbox.{h,cpp}` — installs the environment (openlibs + registry).
  - `src/api/registry.cpp` — ordered module list (THE file to edit to add an API).
  - `src/api/game.cpp` — game.JobId + GetService dispatch via rbx.Services;
    `api/runservice.cpp` RunService.IsStudio; `api/user_settings.cpp`
    UserSettings; `api/os.cpp` os.exit; `api/newproxy.cpp` newproxy
    (bit32 comes from luaL_openlibs).
  - `src/api/random.cpp/.h` — exact Roblox PCG Random (Random.new + NextInteger
    + NextNumber + Clone).
  - `challenge/challenge_src.lua` — decompiled reference source (no longer
    required for the answer; kept for auditing/type reference).
  - build: `cmake -B build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release && cmake --build build -j`
- `/home/john/luau-v13/` — pristine v13-era worktree (for verification work).
- `tools/standardize_wire.py` — wire->standard decoder (THE key tool).
- `tools/roblox_bc_tables.json` — opcode tables from libroblox.so.
- `tools/comb_round.sh` + `tools/combined_capture.gdb` — one-shot capture of
  everything needed for a new dataset (wire, program, dumps, RNG, answer, job).
- `py/local_solve.py` — earlier source-based solver (superseded, kept).
- `run/comb_1..3`, `run/rounds/r1..4`, `run/full` — verified datasets with
  matching native answers (regression corpus).

### Reproduce a full verification (5 matched datasets)

```
for d in run/comb_1 run/comb_2 run/comb_3; do
  python3 tools/standardize_wire.py $d /tmp/$d.luac
  u1=$(python3 -c "d=open('$d/wire_chal.bin','rb').read();print(int.from_bytes(d[1:5],'little'))")
  u2=$(python3 -c "d=open('$d/wire_chal.bin','rb').read();print(int.from_bytes(d[5:9],'little'))")
  ans=$(python3 -c "d=open('$d/wire_ans.bin','rb').read();print(int.from_bytes(d[5:9],'little'))")
  job=$(cat $d/jobid.txt)
  ./rbx_runtime/build/challenge_runner --program=/tmp/$d.luac \
     --u1=$(printf 0x%x $u2) --u2=$(printf 0x%x $u1) --job=$job \
     --usersettings=ok --us-string=aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaQ \
     --os-exit=missing --studio=false --newproxy=ok
  echo "expected: $(printf 0x%x $ans)"
done
```

### LIVE BREAKTHROUGH (2026-10-08 pm): challenge triggered + answered on a real server

**The trigger was the A7 message.** Format: `[a7][count u16le][count x 16B script hashes]`.
- `a7 00 00` (count=0, "empty A7") — the server treats this as "no scripts
  attested -> nothing to verify" and NEVER sends the 0x9B challenge. Every
  Python-client run with empty A7 (6+ runs, 45-75s each) got the join reply
  then idle/no-challenge. (The 10-07 finding "empty A7 triggers the challenge"
  was wrong/stale — no log ever backed it.)
- A7 with count>0 (real hashes) — the server issues the challenge ~0.4-0.6s
  after the join reply, every time. The native never sends count=0 in any
  capture (count=1/2/5/6 observed); captured real A7s: 99B count=6
  (`py/captures/msg_0006_a4_c1.bin`), 83B count=5 (`run/sessioncap/`).
- `--a7 skip` (no A7 at all) also challenges (native acct2cap did this) but
  A7-real is the native-faithful default now.

**First live answered challenge** (`run/live_a7real_1.log`):
- CHALLENGE u1=0x846db0cf u2=0xf056f3aa blob=1959B -> LOCAL ANSWER 0x5ecb15e4
  (decoded+standardized+ran in 29ms) -> sent `[9b][u2][answer]` on chan1.
- This live blob decoded cleanly (checksum ok, 24 protos / 899 starts) but its
  program is a per-server-instance variant (1015 bytes differ from comb_1 and
  from the sessioncap native's own program) — the program is constant per
  SERVER INSTANCE, not per build; the per-blob decode path handles this.
- The server did NOT kick (wrong answers kick) but also sent no replication.

**Ground truth from the real client** (`run/sessioncap/index.txt`, gdb capture
of send(0x631BD58)+recv(0x632127A)+challenge-dispatch(0x32AC2D6)+loader(0x679992E)):
- native order: ctrl hello, openU c3/c5/c6/c11, [app=6 dummy up: ctrl, openU
  c2/c3, ping 77B, sync 25B], early-auth, A7(count=N), 90, 92, 8A, 8F;
  challenge arrives ~0.77s after 8F; answer 14ms later; THEN chan11 route
  declarations `a6` (~66ms after answer, ids +4 each, session-specific base);
  server's first post-answer data ~+170ms; replicator created ~+200ms;
  "Assigned peer id" ~+1.1s; physics replication flows.
- probe10 now mirrors this: all four openU before early-auth, dummy support
  (`--dummy full`), A7-real, and chan11 `a6` route declarations after the
  answer (`--routes on`).

### Next steps after compaction

1. ~~Wire solver into py/probe10.py~~ DONE (`solve9b.solve_message` +
   `solve_local` in probe10; `--source local` default).
2. Verify the server accepts the Python client's answer (no 0x106 reset,
   replication starts). The answer value is proven correct; this validates
   the remaining session-level checks.
3. Live test command:
   `python3 py/probe10.py --seconds 75 --place 1818 --a7 empty --source local`
   → log `run/live_9b_*.log` (expects: CHALLENGE → LOCAL ANSWER → peer assigned).

## Native game ClientHello + QUIC fingerprint (2026-10-08, gdb captures)

Authoritative captures: `run/native_clienthello_tx001.bin` (byte-exact 325 B
game ClientHello), `run/quicdump/udp/tx001-008.bin` (Initials, decrypt with
`tools/quic_initial_parse.py`), `run/quicdump_settings/` (ngtcp2 params/settings
gdb dumps), `run/extff00/` (caps ext getter/payload/parse round).

**RUPP wrap on the wire**: `[01][00][u16 BE header_len]` + TLV1 `01 11 01
<16B token>` + TLV2 `02 06 <rcc IPv4><rcc port BE>` + QUIC; header_len=31
counts the 4-byte prefix (the working client uses subtype **01**, not 02).
Inner Initial is 1200 B → 1231 B on wire via `sendmsg` (GSO batches = N×1200).

**QUIC/TLS**:
- ClientHello ciphers: **only 0x1302, 0x1303** (no 0x1301).
- 14 extensions in exact order: `0xff00` (8 B caps), `0x0039` QUIC transport
  params (88 B), `0x0000` SNI = **literal IP** `128.116.2.33`, `0x000b`
  ec_point_formats (`03000102`), `0x000a` supported_groups (**x25519 only**),
  `0x0023` session_ticket (empty), `0x0010` ALPN = **`rbx-rtcio`**, `0x0016`
  encrypt_then_mac, `0x0017` extended_master_secret, `0x0014`
  server_certificate_type (`0102`), `0x000d` signature_algorithms (42 B),
  `0x002b` supported_versions (`0304`), `0x002d` psk_ke_modes (`0101`),
  `0x0033` key_share (x25519 + 32 B). No GREASE anywhere; sid_len=0.
- QUIC: server sends RETRY; client adopts server DCIDs (initial keys stay
  post-Retry CID); initial PN = random 31-bit; CRYPTO frames carry raw TLS
  handshake (no record layer).
- Transport params (88 B) = **stock `ngtcp2_transport_params_encode()`** —
  skips zero/default params (streams_bidi=0, max_udp=65527, ack_delay_exp=3,
  max_ack_delay=25ms, active_cid_limit=2, disable_active_migration all
  omitted); wire order initial_scid → 3 stream windows (1410065407) →
  max_data (141006550700) → streams_uni (1024) → idle (20000 ms) →
  datagram (65535) → version_info (v1/v1). Full runtime settings dump:
  windows 1410065407, cid_limit=2, cc=BBRv2, initial_rtt=333333333,
  max_tx_udp_payload=1452, random31 pn, streams_uni=1024, idle 20s,
  handshake 10s (see `run/quicdump_settings/` + `tools/ngtcp2_off3.txt`).

## TLS custom extension 0xFF00 = CAPABILITIES exchange (2026-10-08, IDA+gdb)

**What it is**: an 8-byte little-endian **u64 capability bitmask** exchanged
during the TLS handshake (`RbxTransportQuicPeer` "custom TLS extension").
Server value = its `netStackConfig.caps` from the join reply (16-hex string of
the LE u64); e.g. server `022dd3ef52ff0892` ↔ ext bytes `92 08 ff 52 ef d3
2d 02`. Native client conn#0 sends `0x002dd3c750b70892` (bytes `92 08 b7 50
c7 d3 2d 00`) — a strict subset of the server's (server has extra bits);
conn#1 of the same session sends **8 zero bytes** → zeros are tolerated.

**Registration/flow (libroblox.so offsets, IDA instance)**:
- `OsslContext::vtable[6] = sub_6380F58` → `SSL_CTX_add_custom_ext(ctx,
  0xFF00, context=1152, add_cb=sub_638104F, free=0, add_arg=getter,
  {parse_cb=sub_638120C, parse_arg=setter})`. 1152 = 0x480 =
  `SSL_EXT_CLIENT_HELLO|SSL_EXT_TLS1_3_ENCRYPTED_EXTENSIONS` (send in CH,
  echo in EE).
- Client registers at `sub_63AB002` (getter=`sub_63A84E6`, setter=
  `sub_63A8522`), server at `sub_63ABA2C` (+extra `sub_63A9514`).
- add_cb calls the getter at **`0x63810A4 call r13`** (rax=ptr, rdx=len at
  `0x63810A7`); requires len<256 else alert 80.
- getter `sub_63A84E6`: reads tagged fn at `conn+0x4D8` / buffer at
  `conn+0x4C0`; the fn is `sub_638CEE2` → returns ptr to the **local caps
  u64** and logs `[FLog::RbxTransportQuicPeer] Sending custom TLS extension
  with capabilities: {}`.
- parse_cb `sub_638120C` **always returns 1 when configured** (app return
  ignored) → TLS-level acceptance never depends on content; logs `Received
  custom TLS extension of size {}`. setter `sub_63A8522` appends the bytes
  to a buffer at `conn+0x500`.
- receive side `sub_638CFB6`: caps valid **only if exactly 8 bytes** (else
  0), logs `Received remote peer capabilities: {}` then
  `Combined peer capabilities: {}` after `*combined &= remote`.
- negotiation (per `sub_63108C8`): common = local & remote; ext absent +
  `RbxTransportFailClosedCapabilityNegotiation` (byte_76EBDE8) OFF →
  common = local (optimistic); ON → 0. Server caps can also come from the
  join reply (`RbxTransportTakeServerCapabilitiesFromJoin`). Downstream:
  `Assigning capabilities {} to Replicator`,
  `pushProtocolNegotiatedCapabilities`, `Peer has capabilities: 0x%llx,
  common capabilities: 0x%llx`.
- C++ client: register custom ext 0xFF00 first (add first ⇒ appears first in
  ClientHello, matching native order), send 8-byte LE caps
  `0x002dd3c750b70892` (byte-identical to native conn#0; alternative if
  rejected: zeros, proven tolerated by native conn#1), parse cb stores/ignores.

gdb evidence: `run/extff00/index.txt` (GETTER=0x63A84E6, ctx vtable =
QuicClientConnection `0x70E88B0`, PAYLOAD#0/#1, PARSE#0/#1 server echo
`9208ff52efd32d02` for both conns), tooling `tools/extff00.gdb` +
`tools/extff00_round.sh`.


## C++ transport (ngtcp2+OpenSSL) handshake live + ClientHello byte-parity (2026-10-08)

Milestone M2 of the Python→C++ port: `client/src/quic_client.{h,cpp}` +
`rupp.h` (RUPP wrap/strip, 31B header, subtype-1 token) linked against
ngtcp2 (submodule sources, auto-built by CMake into the build tree:
libngtcp2.a + libngtcp2_crypto_ossl.a — the submodule checkout itself is
never touched) and system OpenSSL 3.5.7.

**Live result** (`./client/build/hs1818 run/join_cpp*.json`): handshake
completes and confirms (HANDSHAKE_DONE) against the real udmux for place
1818, ALPN `rbx-rtcio`, cipher `TLS_AES_256_GCM_SHA384` (1302), connection
stable (no reset, no rupp drops), and the **0xFF00 capabilities exchange
works end-to-end**: server echoes exactly 8 bytes = `022dd3ef52ff0892` =
its own `netStackConfig.caps` from the join reply.

**ClientHello vs `run/native_clienthello_tx001.bin`: CH MATCH** via
`tools/ch_diff.py run/cpp_tx/tx001.bin` (masked: random, key_share key,
TP initial_scid, SNI IP literal — each per-session/per-join):
- legacy 0303, sid_len 0, compression 1, ciphers **[1302, 1303]**
  (`TLS_AES_256_GCM_SHA384:TLS_CHACHA20_POLY1305_SHA256` — note 0x1303 is
  CHACHA20, not AES-128), all 14 extensions in native's exact order
  `ff00 39 0 0b 0a 23 10 16 17 14 0d 2b 2d 33`, every body byte-identical.
- `SSL_CTX_set1_server_cert_type(ssl, "\x02", 1)` produces the native
  `0014` body `0102` exactly.
- OpenSSL 3.5.7 sends RFC8879 `compress_certificate` (0x001b) by default;
  native (3.5.0) doesn't → disable with
  `SSL_OP_NO_TX_CERTIFICATE_COMPRESSION|SSL_OP_NO_RX_CERTIFICATE_COMPRESSION`.
- sigalgs default list of 3.5.7 == native 3.5.0's 20-pair list; stock
  `ngtcp2_transport_params_encode()` emits the native 88B TP (order
  initial_scid → 3 windows → max_data → streams_uni → idle(ms) → datagram →
  version_info; version_info is filled by ngtcp2_conn_client_new itself).

Settings/TP mirror `run/quicdump_settings/index.txt` (windows 1410065407 /
141006550700, streams_uni 1024, idle 20s, handshake 10s, datagram 65535,
BBR=2, initial_rtt 333333333, max_tx_udp 1452, random31 initial PN).
`settings.initial_ts` was 0 in the native dump but ngtcp2 derives
idle/handshake deadlines from it, so we set it to our CLOCK_MONOTONIC base
and pass the same clock to read_pkt/write_pkt (timestamps never hit the
wire; ack delays are relative).

## C++ client complete: local solve + LIVE session accepted on Crossroads (2026-10-08)

Milestone M3 of the Python→C++ port — the full session layer, and the first
client (any language) to get a Roblox server to accept a locally-computed
0x9B answer and start replication.

### Components

- `client/src/join.{h,cpp}` — libcurl join: gamejoin **v2 SSE** (ResponseReady
  records, same parse as `rbx_client.parse_sse`) → v1 JSON → joinScriptUrl
  GET fallback; `$RBX_SAVE_JOIN` keeps `{joinScript, reply}` (the reply's
  joinTicket is required by the 0x90 rebuild).
- `client/src/session.{h,cpp}` — byte-level port of `py/probe10.py`:
  ctrl hello + OpenUnreliable c3/c5/c6/c11, early-auth (A8 from ClientTicket
  parts 2/3), A7-real, 0x90 (template rebuild), 0x92, 0x8A,
  0x8F on chan1; frame-walk challenge detection; in-process `solveMessage`;
  9-byte answer; chan11 route declarations +60ms; DummySession (app=6 second
  conn, same RUPP token: ctrl hello, openU c2/c3, PING 77B/1s on chan1,
  SYNC 25B/5s on chan3).
- `client/test/play_main.cpp` — `rbxplay` CLI (join → connect → burst →
  solve → answer → pump; probe10-style `== summary ==` line; maintainer
  env (`RBX_DUMP_MSGS` for the built messages, `RBX_CORRUPT=1` flipping
  0xDEADBEEF into the answer as the control).
- QuicClient transport hardening for sessions: queue-based stream TX
  (`txq_` + `drainStream`, so flow-control blocks never lose the 10.7KB 0x90),
  ngtcp2 `keep_alive` 1s (replaces probe10's QUIC PING loop), app events
  queued (`evq_`) and delivered **outside** ngtcp2 (callbacks fire inside
  `ngtcp2_conn_read_pkt` — app code calling stream APIs there would be
  reentrant), `recv_datagram` callback, Python-semantics b64decode.

### Parity proofs

- `tools/msg_parity.py run/join_full.json run/cpp_msgs` → **MSG PARITY OK**:
  earlyauth **103B**, 0x8A **1765B**, 0x90 **10612B** byte-identical to
  probe5/probe7 builders (which were validated against native captures);
  0x92 random-nonce structure OK. PING/SYNC/ROUTES constants verified
  byte-exact vs probe10 (SYNC had a 24B vs **25B** off-by-one — fixed; the
  wrong sync made the server occasionally DRAINING-close the dummy).
- regressions: `tests/run_all.sh` runs all four suites (solver-regress py+cpp,
  handshake parity, message parity); individually:
  `python3 tools/regress9b.py` 5/5 and `./client/build/regress9b`
  5/5.

### THE RESULT — live acceptance

```
./client/build/rbxplay --seconds 45 --a7 real --dummy full \
    --cookie-file run/cookie2.txt
== summary == challenge=yes answered=yes peer=yes resets=0
              chan1_rx=993833 dummy_rx=3685
== solved=yes answer=0x3066e501 solve_ms=1 tx=969 rx=3464 err=
```

Timeline (run/live_cpp3.log, dummy-less): challenge at T, **solved in 1ms**,
answer sent, server begins chan1 replication **in the same millisecond** —
first frame is the 52140B initial world state (the "~50KB flood"), then
continuous replication (934KB over 60s), route declarations at T+60ms
(sessioncap s0019-s0022), zero resets, connection stable for the full run.
Accepted sessions so far: 5× (2 with `--dummy full` at ~1MB/45s, one
1.13MB, one 60s/934KB...); dummy survives the full run after the SYNC fix.

### Why Python never got accepted (the ~15ms window)

probe10's answer timeline on challenge=yes runs
(live_a7real_1.log): 0x93 at T, 0x9B challenge at T+362ms, **0x106
DisconnectSendPacketError stream reset at T+377ms**, Python answer at
T+393ms (29ms executor solve) — the server gave up **16ms** after sending
the challenge, i.e. before Python's answer could arrive. The in-process C++
solve is **1-2ms**, so the answer lands inside the window. This is the whole
game: the challenge→answer round trip must beat ~15ms.

### Corrupt-answer control (verified 2/2 — was the last open item)

`--corrupt` (answer ^= 0xDEADBEEF): the server still streams **exactly
63871B** (initial state) and then **CONNECTION_CLOSE → ERR_DRAINING at
+136/+137ms** (both runs byte-identical), instead of continuing replication.
Correct answer → open-ended stream (934KB-1.13MB runs). Proves the 0x9B
answer is exactly what is being validated. Caveat: the summary `peer=yes`
flag fires on any post-answer traffic, so it reads "yes" in corrupt runs too
— the discriminators are `err=` (ERR_DRAINING vs empty), resets, and
session lifetime.

### Challenge-trigger variance (updated)

- The 0x93 variant is NOT the discriminator: the `0x70f`/1811B variant was
  seen in both challenge-yes (live_a7real_1) and challenge-no (live_cpp1)
  runs — the older note tying it to "no A7" is stale.
- C++ runs: 8/12 challenged overall; **with `--dummy full`: 4/4**, without
  dummy: 4/8 (exactly 50%). Native always opens the dummy immediately —
  the dummy connection may well be (part of) the challenge trigger.
- One ERR_HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT after ~10 back-to-back joins (dummy4) —
  transient/rate-limit; a 20s wait + retry connects fine.
- Runs land on the same endpoint every time (udmux 128.116.2.33:61269,
  rcc 10.37.8.91, job 2718c55d-...), so the variance is server-side state
  or a race, not instance selection.

## 2026-10-09 — Repo restructure (modularity pass)

No protocol/behavior changes; every step gated on the 5-suite battery and a
final live acceptance re-run.

- `luau_runner/` renamed to **`rbx_runtime/`** — the folder is the Roblox
  scripting environment (Luau VM + Roblox globals + sandbox), not just a
  challenge-program runner.
- `runner.cpp` split into a **modular API registry** (see
  `rbx_runtime/src/api/api.h` for the "add an API" recipe):
  - `src/api/registry.cpp` — the ordered module list (order = behavior);
  - one `api/*.cpp` per surface: `game`, `RunService`, `UserSettings`,
    `os`, `newproxy`, `Random`;
  - `game:GetService` dispatches through a service table (`rbx.Services`),
    so new services (workspace, Players, ...) never edit `game.cpp`;
    unknown names still return a fresh empty table per call (identity-
    observable, preserved deliberately);
  - the process-global `g_opts` is gone — options are stored per-state in
    the Lua registry (`api::opts(L)`), so `run()` is re-entrant.
- **Luau is now the pinned `third_party/luau` submodule** (74f76830), built
  via add_subdirectory — no hardcoded /home/john/luau, no prebuilt-libs
  dance. Every third-party source dep is a submodule (ngtcp2, luau);
  SDL3_ttf stays vendored (prebuilt blob).
- All `py/`/`tools/` paths are repo-relative (`ROOT` from `__file__` /
  `BASH_SOURCE`); gdb captures honor `$RBX_ROOT`, mocktail honors
  `$MOCKTAIL`, `luau-compile` consumers honor `$LUAU_COMPILE`.
- New entry points: `README.md` (orientation + how to add an API) and
  `tests/run_all.sh` (one-command battery: solver-regress py+cpp, CH
  parity, msg parity).

Post-restructure live acceptance (Crossroads place 1818, acct2,
`rbxplay --seconds 45 --a7 real --dummy full`):
`challenge=yes answered=yes peer=yes resets=0`, `solve_ms=9`,
`chan1_rx=925475 dummy_rx=3685`, `err=` empty. Corrupt-answer control
unchanged from the earlier 2/2.

## 2026-10-11 — socket TOS parity (IDA) + CLI simplification

**Native sets no IP_TOS — so neither do we.** A carrier-throttling report
(DU and similar middleboxes cutting the session) prompted a check of what
the official client puts in the TOS/DSCP field. IDA on `libroblox.so`
(50 `setsockopt` call sites; transport-range functions `sub_63CE054`,
`sub_63D55A8`, `sub_63D7F20`, `sub_63DA2D0` decompiled): the native UDP
socket gets REUSEADDR/REUSEPORT, V6ONLY, TTL, MTU_DISCOVER/DONTFRAG,
PKTINFO/RECVERR, SNDBUF/RCVBUF — and never `IP_TOS` (level 0 / optname 1)
or `IPV6_TCLASS` (41/67). Zero TOS/DSCP/TCLASS strings exist anywhere in
the binary. TOS 0 on our socket (`client/src/quic_client.cpp:init`) is
therefore native-faithful, and there is deliberately no TOS knob: a
middlebox cutting TOS-0 UDP bursts cuts the real client identically. There
is deliberately no traffic-shape lever: session behavior is native-fixed
(real A7, full dummy — the `--a7`/`--dummy` flags were deleted; the
session burst order already mirrors the native sessioncap order, and
datagram emission is ngtcp2 congestion-controlled on both sides, so no
pacing divergence was introduced.

**CLI cut** (`rbxplay`, `hs1818`): the shell does shell jobs now.
`--cookie-file` → `--roblosecurity` (raw value; `$(cat file)` at the
call site; `$RBX_ROBLOSECURITY` fallback; `loadCookieFile` deleted),
`--seconds` gone (run until SIGINT/SIGTERM — `timeout 45s …` bounds a
run and still prints the summary), `--alpn` gone (hardcoded `rbx-rtcio`),
`--save-join`/`--dump-msgs`/`--tx-dump` → `$RBX_SAVE_JOIN`/
`$RBX_DUMP_MSGS`/`$RBX_TX_DUMP` (they only regenerate tracked fixtures),
`--corrupt`/`--no-routes` → `$RBX_CORRUPT=1`/`$RBX_NO_ROUTES=1`,
`--cap-dir` gone (resolved: `$RBX_CAP_DIR`, `./py/captures`,
`<exe>/../../py/captures` — the hardcoded relative path broke runs from
any other CWD with instant server-side ERR_DRAINING).
Kept: `--place-id`, `--roblosecurity`, `--join-json`, `--job-id`.
`--place-id` is always required: job-only joins come back status 12
"Cannot join game without placeId" (verified live 2026-10-11), so
`--job-id` only pins one running instance of the given place. Session
behavior (real A7, full dummy) is unconditional —
native does nothing else, so neither do we. Live command is now:
`./build/client/rbxplay --place-id 1818 --roblosecurity "$(cat run/cookie2.txt)"`.

---

## Replication capture + frame survey (2026-10-11)

**Capture.** `$RBX_REPL_DUMP` writes the full chan1 byte stream at exit
(`run/repl_1818.bin`, 917247B — pinned by size against the `chan1_rx`
the summary prints; gitignored, stays local). First artifact of its
kind: previously replication traffic was counted but never saved.

**Frame survey** (compactVarint walk over the capture — same framing as
the challenge finder): 3566 messages, **zero framing errors**, six IDs:

| ID | count | size | role (inferred) |
|----|-------|------|-----------------|
| 0x93 | 1 | 1807B | join reply (known layout) |
| 0x9B | 1 | 1980B | anti-cheat challenge (known layout) |
| 0x97 | 1 | 53164B | initial world snapshot (~52KB flood) |
| 0x81 | 1 | 528B | peer assignment (arrives with post-answer traffic) |
| 0x84 | 1 | 5B | unknown, tiny (ack/marker?) |
| 0x83 | 3561 | 7B–43KB, median 7 | continuous replication deltas (early flood, then heartbeats) |

**0x97 outer cracked.** Body = `[97][a3 cf][00 00][u32 LE size @5:9]`
`[zstd @9:]`, self-validating (field == 352276 == actual). Inner
(`run/join97_1818.raw`, tracked fixture): starts with an alphabetical
schema dictionary (class descriptors: `1c ff ff <len> <Class>` + property
lists with single-byte type tags), then ~630 variable-length instance
records (the universal Instance props — Archivable, Name, … — repeat
630×; `numExpectedDirectChildren` marks records). No instance *values*
parsed yet — schema positions known, field semantics next.

**Native ground truth (live gdb capture, same place).** A mocktail
session yielded `run/repl_native{97,81,83,83_06}.bin` (tracked fixtures):
- 0x97 is **byte-identical** to ours (53164B) — the snapshot is fully
  static across instances, so the frozen inner is a meaningful golden.
- 0x81 layout CONFIRMED: `[81][ena][x][isrR][isrW][isrLeg][v53?][v58]`
  `[peer:LEB128][v60?]` — native peer reads 31, matching its
  "Assigned peer id = 31" log line exactly; ours reads 696. (An early
  mis-model with one fewer read is ruled out by both captures.)
- New inner type 0x06 seen native-side only (1120B sample) — the type
  space exceeds any single capture; survey more sessions before freezing
  the enum.

**Native dispatch map (IDA libroblox.so, md5-verified == ~/rr binary).**
Stream frames switch on first byte in `sub_32AC2D6`: 0x83 → Replicator::
OnReceive (`sub_344324C`) → internal type 31 → deserialize queue;
0x97 → JoinDataItemV2 path (`sub_31F1008` → stats in `sub_3489D46` →
`processDeserializedJoinDataItem`); 0x81 → peer-init (`sub_32B111E`,
"Assigned peer id"). Control messages are BYTE-oriented (ReadByte,
LEB128 varint, IgnoreBytes, ReadU32LE — all with bounds-checked
`network_stream_exception`s), not bit-packed (see the confirmed 0x81
layout above). 0x83 bodies are NOT compressed (no zstd magic) — raw
item format, still to map.

**Job pinning verified live.** `--place-id 1818 --job-id <guid>` returns
that exact instance (`join: OK job=<same guid>`, session accepted,
replication flowing) — the kept flag works end to end. Job-only
(`placeId` 0) is rejected outright, which is why `--place-id` is
mandatory.

**0x83 inner types** (second byte; median message 7B, range 7B–43KB):
0x23 heartbeat-ish items dominate (2034×, all 7B: `[23][u24 timestamp?][0000]`,
server-clock-ish values climbing through the session); 0x16 (37×) =
compressed batches `[16][flags][u32 comp][u32 decomp][zstd][1 trailing
byte]` — both lengths assert exactly (`comp` counts the frame only;
naive one-shot decode fails "Src size is incorrect" on the trailer);
0x02 (101×) = string items (avatar-fetch URLs with placeId/userId,
`KeyboardBinding`/`GamepadBinding`, `ff`-separated TLV-ish); 0x1b/0x07/
0x0d/others = small binary items (u32-ish IDs + nested 0x23s).
Native cross-check: a live-gdb-captured native 0x1b frame
(`run/repl_native83.bin`, 1024B) shows the identical
`[1b][variant][000000][items]` shape — frame layout independently
confirmed on both ends.

Next: 2016 cross-reference (`App/v8datamodel` schema concepts onto
0x97/0x83 — background only; the NextGen replicator postdates it),
continued IDA pin of the item readers, then a pure-function decoder
(`replicate_parse`, fuzzable like every other parser) with golden
vectors from frozen captures — before any DataModel apply. A live
gdb capture (native JoinData struct dump) is armed and pending.

## JoinData record codec solved (2026-10-12, IDA + live native log)

**Verbose-log breakthrough.** Forcing `DFLog::ReplicationDataCaching`
verbose (2 bytes at file off `0x75E3F78` → 6,6 via gdb, before the
snapshot lands) makes the native print every instance boundary:
`Expecting %d instances` (9 blobs: 132+131+861+859+2+20+19+2+2 = 2028,
all inside 70ms), then per instance `Reading @ offset` / `Done ...
%s offset=%X ... numOfChildren=0, indexInParent=0`. numChildren/index
are ALWAYS 0 (vestigial field, never written — tree comes from parent
refs). Script: `tools/repl_loglevel.gdb`.

**Record grammar** (`sub_348C62C` + `sub_3493C2E` + `sub_336800E`,
all IDA-verified, all byte-oriented):
`[varint lo][u32 id][u16 class][props][varint plo][u32 parent]`,
props = `([u8 delta][value])* [0xFF]`, delta indexes the class member
list (range-checked only). lo==0/plo==0 = sentinel (no u32 follows;
parent −1 = graft to root). Observed lo/plo ∈ {1,4} (semantics
HYPOTHESIS: snapshot vs live). Ref values use the same
`[varint][u32]` (0 = null). Class id = schema order index.
Cross-check: our frozen batch bytes match the native log's boundaries
EXACTLY (7/7 leading Sound records: 63,52,54,51,52,55,55B).

**Records ride 0x83/0x16 batches, not 0x97.** The 0x97 inner holds
schema + two string tables + an opaque per-class/id tail; the ~2910
instance records arrive in 9 batch inners (012/022/024/026/028/030/
032/034/036). Other 0x16 batches are Type-35-style property updates
(37% printable ≈ random) and fail record-parse at offset 0, as they
should. The 0x97 tail (`[u32 630][u32 0][u32 7][u32 0]` + u32 arrays
1..10144) is still open (HYPOTHESIS: prealloc/id lists) — not needed
for spawning (all 2910 instances resolve from batches).

**Value shapes by schema tag** (writer `sub_33AD49C` + value reader
`sub_340A3E4` switch, 72 cases; only snapshot-occurring tags fully
pinned): 2/8 string, 3 empty, 6/46/47 16B (GUID/Tags/bytecode-hash),
7 enum varint, 9 u8, 10 zigzag, 11 f32, 12/48/55/62 f64/u64, 13 UDim,
14 UDim2 (f32,s32,f32,s32), 15 Ray, 18 u16 BrickColor, 19/22/36 Vec3/
Color3, 20 3B, 21/37 2xf32, 24 2xu16, 25 3xs16, 26 CFrameA (UNUSED in
schema — IDA-only), 27 CFrameB, 28/69 ref, 38 sequence (u32 N + N×20B
keypoints — the 8B first read misled once; 3614CAA nests a 12B
Vector3 read), 39 20B, 40 16B, 41 PhysicalProperties (1/21/25B),
49 Name (u8-len/shared/extended), 51 optional CFrame (1/13/19B),
52 16B, 54 FontFace, 61 Content, 65 13B, 66 3B, 67 16B, 68 4×varint.
CFrame table path ((kind−1)/6,(kind−1)%6 over ±axes, cross third
column — validated: 104/104 snapshot cases give det +1 signed
permutations) and quat path (33BEB0A literal port — validated:
all 28 kind==0 cases orthonormal det +1) both verified on live data.
Tags 29–33/35/50/53/56–60/63/64/70 never occur in snapshot and fail
loudly. Enum wire varint = raw item VALUE (KeyCode Space=32,
ButtonA=1002 match the dump). Shared Names resolve via the 0x97's
127-string table (348 uses: Handle, idle, Weight, …).

**Tree reconciliation (our job):** 2915 records → 2910 unique (5
retransmitted ids, first wins); 53 children of id 13 = the implicit
DataModel root (Workspace, Players, Lighting, …); 59 records graft
under 18 missing live-dynamic parents; 138 refs to missing ids go nil.
Wire→Luau aliases taken: exactly `size→Size`, `shape→Shape`
(case-insensitive; version-skew renames). Skips are all principled:
wire-only (Capabilities, numExpectedDirectChildren, …), unresolvable
(Tags guid, bytecode Source hash), undeclared skew, readonly.

**Code:** `client/src/replicate.{h,cpp}` (pure schema+record parsers),
`client/src/joindata.{h,cpp}` (Engine-only apply: spawn → props →
refs+parents, audit counters), goldens `tests/vectors/repl/frame{012,
022,024,026,028,030,032,034,036}.bin` + `unit-replicate` (schema +
record pins) + `unit-engine` (subset + full-tree apply pins),
`fuzz/fuzz_records.cpp` wired into `tools/fuzz_all.sh` (196k runs
clean). Schema header also yielded propbound/enum trailer details the
first C++ cut missed (two bound varints after classcount; 0x07 enum
trailer) — caught by golden mismatch, fixed, pinned.

### Headful viewer spike (raylib 6.0, SDL3 backend, mesh soup)
`rbxplay --headful [--shot PATH]` builds the world from the captured
chan1 stream after the session ends and renders every CFrame+Size
instance as a unit-cube box (orbit camera). `--headless` (default)
skips everything: zero session behavior change. Collect rule is pure
(`rbxclient::collectBoxes`, GL-free, pinned in `unit-engine` incl. the
Tool Handle CFrame/Size values); only `runViewer` touches GL.
Live 1818: 64 boxes (place state varies run to run).

Build note (2026-10-12, CI red then green): raylib's SDL backend
needs SDL3 dev, which Ubuntu 24.04 (CI runner) does not ship
(libsdl3-dev starts at 25.10+). The viewer therefore splits:
`view_collect.cpp` (pure, always built + golden) and `view_raylib.cpp`
(GL window, only with SDL3); without SDL3 `view_stub.cpp` makes
`--headful` fail loudly (rc 2, same CLI path). `-DRBX_VIEWER=OFF`
forces the stub. CI exercises the stub path; live runs cover the GL
path. No apt additions, no behavior change when SDL3 is present.

Two portability notes (both verified live, no guessing):
- raylib `TakeScreenshot(path)` prepends its internal basePath (exe
  dir), so absolute paths save as `<exe-dir>//tmp/...` and fail. The
  viewer uses `LoadImageFromScreen` + `ExportImage(shot_path)` instead:
  same capture, no path mangling, no OS headers.
- `resolve_cap_dir` used `/proc/self/exe` (Linux-only); now resolves
  exe-relative `py/captures` portably via `argv[0]` + `std::filesystem`.
  No `unistd.h` remains in the new viewer/CLI code.

## Recv items: 0x83 chains, spawn, events, ticks (2026-10-12, IDA + live)

**Chain model (corrects two earlier misreads).** A 0x83 message chains
items: `[83][type][body]* [00]`. The old "Type-35 kind/subchannel" theory
is withdrawn: the byte after each 0x23 clock u32 is the NEXT item's type
(or the 00 end marker) — 12B double-tick messages carry two +4 clocks,
and msg07's "19B records" are eight chained 0x07 invocations + a clock.
The old 0x07 owner/val/x split is withdrawn with it. Any 32B3BA4-level
type can chain (02/07/23 via the inner 3439982 dispatcher, 0d/1b/20/...
via outer handlers); 0x16 batches never chain (all 37 frozen messages
are pure `[83][16][frame][trailer]`). Coverage on the frozen session
(3566 msgs): 2431 pure walks, 1083 partial (stop pinned), 10 hard-fails
(all RemoteEvent.OnClientEvent, tuple-family), 37 batches deferred.

**Item grammars (all IDA-verified, all byte-exact on live bytes):**
- 0x23 tick (3511D26): `[23][u32 X]`, X +4 monotonic (2034/2034 ticks).
- 0x02 spawn (34956D2): `[ref lo+u32][u16 class][pad u8 iff
  state+15562 bit0==0][loop][loop][parent ref]`. Both loops are the
  snapshot `([u8 delta][value])* [0xFF]` grammar (3493C2E modes (2,1)
  then (1,0) — mode selects reader variants, never framing; call-site
  args confirmed in disassembly, snapshot passes (0,0)). Live flag is
  always 0xb7 (no pad); the pad branch is IDA-only, parameterized.
  Deltas are absolute member indexes (same evidence as snapshot).
- 0x07 event (34822C6): `[07][entity ref][u16 GLOBAL event][params]`.
  The event index runs over concatenated per-class lists in schema order
  (total 6937 == ev_bound exactly). Param shapes come from the schema's
  3B descriptors, byte[0] = value tag in snapshot numbering (native
  switches on descriptor+1 in 340A3E4). Validated: 8/8 msg07 invocations
  are FriendService.RemoteFriendStatusSignal#2351 (params [44,44,7],
  zigzag varints + enum), plus Sound playback events #5211/#5206.
- 0x83 walker stops pinned (Walk83.complete/stop_type/stop_off) at
  deferred types (0d/1b/20/05/01/03/10) — measured, never silent.

**Corrections to earlier claims:** tag 44 is ZIGZAG
(`(w>>1)^-(w&1)`, case 0x2C) — joindata's plain decode was wrong
(IntValue#4058575 Value: wire 30 -> 15, pinned). Tags 7/34 are plain
varints (verified). Native tag inventory: everything 0-70 except 4, 5,
45 (no case). Event-param tags 29-33 fail loudly: Tuple/ValueArray/
ValueTable/ValueMap element types live in compiled-in reflection, not on
the wire (schema has only [tag][ff][ff]; the API dump's Tuple is
`{"Category":"Group"}`, equally opaque). Tag 56 parses (bulk blob:
[varint comp][varint decomp][bytes], 340989C). Tag 57 fails loudly
(flag-variable frustum, client->server-only).

**Open anomaly (pinned, not solved):** message @57504 ends with a
LocalScript (#11969) whose parent ref is (scope 164917115, id
98643702) — unique in the session — followed by 3742B of structureless
binary (no clocks/spawns/zeros/zstd-Luau magic/ASCII). Native resolvers
tolerate deferred parents (319BE28/2495B8E, no throw), so the value is
carried verbatim; the tail's grammar is v2 (trace exotic guids
natively). The walker stops pinned at the 0x78 byte, never desyncs.

**Code:** `parseSpawnItem`/`parseEventItem`/`walk83Items` in
`client/src/replicate.{h,cpp}` (+ tag 56, tuple/57 loud-fails, event
storage in the schema); goldens `msg07/spawn_small/spawn_big/
spawn_weird/chain0730/ticks23/stop0d.bin` + 15-case `unit-replicate`;
tag-44 pin in `unit-engine`; streaming paths added to
`fuzz_records.cpp` + seeds (70k runs clean).

**v2 backlog (recv-complete remainder, handlers known):** 0x0d
(353EDF2, 526 stops), 0x01 (3481460, 246), 0x1b (34F07AE, 210), 0x05
(3486B9E, fixed 32B?), 0x03 (3404858), 0x10 (34899B8), 0x20 (35C3A7E,
43KB blob); flags=02/03/04 batch trailers (6B vs 1B) + the 712B
post-batch tail (NetAsset shapes in 348C62C); Tuple-family (needs a
compiled-in element-type source); spawn_weird tail; streaming apply
(spawn/despawn into the DataModel) + send path (untouched).

## Recv v2: 0x01/0x05/0x0d/0x1b items; 0x03 deferred (2026-10-12)

**Shipped (all IDA-verified + golden):** 0x01 = bare entity ref
(3481460, null dropped natively); 0x1b = u32-counted ref list (34F07AE);
0x05 = timing item (3486B9E: [u8][u64][3xu32][u8] + two state-bit-gated
u32s, flags=0x10 live); 0x0d = [u32 N][u32 comp][u32 decomp][zstd -> N
snapshot records][elements] (353C434 head via 34374B2 + 353ECD2 loop;
element = [u8]([u8 flag]+3xu32/3xu8 iff (u8&0xF7)==0), continue iff u8&8;
3617A08 readers confirmed u32/u8). Entry frames validated: N=1 (Part),
N=6 (mesh subtree), N=16 + 13262B opaque trail (count-based, not
EOF-exact — the native loop runs N times and ignores trailing).
Session coverage: 3457/3566 pure (97%), 57 partial, 10 hardfail
(OnClientEvent tag-29), 37 NetAsset batches deferred.

**0x03 triples DEFERRED (deep investigation, no clean model).** Single
triples (3404858: [ref][u16 prop][value]) chain with AND without
explicit 03 separators; greedy runs + strict validation either hardfail
(45: prop ids 13057-41689 exceed the dense range) or fragment (single +
dispatch diverges into garbage stops). The 1-based dense map (total
members == prop_bound == 10145, boundaries 1/10145 exact, 6 sane tags)
is CONTRADICTED by a 92B message whose prop-3796 value is 77B
Content-shaped (`01 4b` + 75B ASCII emote table, exact-EOF) — neither
dense-3795/DepthScale-f32 nor dense-3796/EmotesDataInternal-string fits.
The prop->member table is therefore NOT schema-order-dense (gappy,
permuted, or wider); without the native descriptor table, 0x03 values
are unboundable (same class of problem as tuples). v3 needs the table
layout (or per-triple tag inference). The 0x03 walker stays
stop-pinned; parsePropTriple removed (no dead code).

**Scope intel (parser-observed, all carried verbatim):** entity-ref los
are wild, not {1,4}: 1 x3058, 4 x774, 3 x2, plus ones of
6/12/22/33/56/88/96/99/101/104/107/117 and a ~1.08M cluster (27x).
The spawn_weird plo=164917115 is extreme but on-trend (deferred-parent
tolerance in 319BE28/2495B8E stands).

**Remaining stops:** 0x03 (53), 0x10 (34899B8, 2), 0x20 (35C3A7E blob,
1), 0x78 (spawn_weird tail, 1). Tag-33/Content-form-1 prefix shapes
(events/loop-A) are IDA-derived, not yet live-validated (no firing

## Join stalls: per-instance gating, fixed by fast bounded retry (2026-10-12)

**Symptom.** Some joins stall: handshake OK, EARLY-AUTH + A7 + full
dummy sent, server answers the 1154B join reply + 657B refactor
(chan1_rx stuck at exactly 1811B), then 50s of silence to
ERR_IDLE_CLOSE (`challenge=no peer=no`). Our TX is byte-identical in
stall vs success runs (same ctrl/openU/auth/A7/90/92/8A/8F/dummy down
to timestamps) — the server decides unilaterally after identical input.

**Cause: per-instance transient gating, not our bytes.** The join
balancer is sticky: three consecutive joins landed on the same instance
(128.116.5.33:49591, job 58136fc2) and all stalled; an immediate
re-join to the SAME instance then succeeded (challenge=yes, 70s clean
session). A wedged instance would never recover in 40s; an account/IP
ban would not clear between back-to-back joins. Verdict: the instance
gates admissions transiently (load/capacity), independent of our bytes.
The native hits this too when unlucky — it just joins rarely enough to
notice it less. Long backoffs (20-30min, previously used) were pure
waste; a 5s retry succeeds.

**Fix (rbxplay): bounded stall retry.** After the run loop, a stalled
attempt (joined, handshake OK, transport failed, no challenge seen, no
user interrupt, no RBX_CORRUPT/RBX_NO_ROUTES test mode) re-joins fresh
(new job -> new instance, or same instance recovered) up to
RBX_JOIN_RETRIES times (default 3, clamp 0..10), backing off 5/10/15s
(interruptible, SIGINT/SIGTERM respected). Handshake-never-completes
retries once the same way (5s). Join-HTTP/config failures, solve
failures, post-challenge deaths, and --join-json runs never retry.
Every retry is logged with job/instance/attempt counts; --job-id pins
log that retries hit the same instance. Verified live: stall detected,
re-joined in 5s to the same instance, full session (challenge=yes
answered=yes peer=yes). No behavior change on healthy joins (single
attempt, identical logs).

## Renderer framing: spawn-focus + robust radius (2026-10-12)

**Empty-frame root cause (live-diagnosed).** After streaming apply
landed, headful shots came out pure white despite 200+ boxes: the
camera orbited a radius-167432 sphere. No NaNs — live physics ships
finite-but-absurd CFrames (~1e5 studs) that the max-radius computation
dutifully framed. Fix is pure (`focusCamera`, GL-free, golden-tested):
spawn-pad ("SpawnLocation") centroid, else all boxes; coordinate-wise
median center; radius cuts before the first 10x distance jump (floor
keeps close neighbors); non-finite positions never frame; radius covers
kept half-extents, floor 1.0. The viewer logs `focus=(x,y,z) r R
spawns=N` permanently (an empty frame is now diagnosable from the log
alone). Live proof: focus=(0,3,10) r=259, ~40 parts clearly framed
(mesh soup, still box-only — meshes/colors are later phases).

## Send path: groundwork + plan (2026-10-12, not yet implemented)

**Status: 0% (recv-complete + framing + retry all shipped).** Our
session sends only join/auth/dummy traffic; movement, owned writes,
and FireServer are untouched.

**Groundwork done.** Writer-hunt in IDA: the stream-write primitive
69F81CE has 42 call sites, but they cluster in readers doing buffer
ops (340A3E4/34054B2) and the 34Fxxxx hits sampled are utilities
(34F3E74 = SharedString cache), not serializers. FireServer/luau API
strings are stripped (no hits). Stream inventory from a live session:
we open ctrl + chan1 upstream; the server opens chan 0/1/2/4/7
downstream — gameplay writes most plausibly ride chan1-upstream (same
channel as our auth/90/92/8A/8F) with new message types, but unproven.

**Safety rule (anti-cheat context):** the 0x9B challenge means the
server watches us. No blind writes: passive native-TX capture first,
grammar second, minimal sender last. Prefer the alt account
(run/cookie.txt) for first live-write tests, never the main.

**Plan (next milestone).** (1) gdb TX capture on a live mocktail
session (breakpoint the native upstream framer; dump payloads by
channel; idle session first for heartbeat shape, then driven input for
movement). (2) Map upstream item grammar against the known downstream
codec (symmetric where proven, never assumed). (3) Minimal sender:
stand-to-walk movement delta, verified by the server echoing position
(no rubber-band) + screenshot. (4) Owned writes, then FireServer, each
with live proof. Despawn/GC-by-silence and RemoteEvent Luau-fire
bridges ride along as needed.

## Join API status diagnostics (2026-10-12, live-verified)

- `gameId` is the UNIVERSE id (13058 for Crossroads 1818), never the job
  UUID. Passing a job UUID as `gameId` to `v1/join-game-instance` returns
  HTTP 200 `status 5 "Could not find requested game instance"` (verified
  live) — that is the not-found shape, distinct from `status 22` + null
  message (unreproduced; points at request shape or instance/account
  eligibility — e.g. PlayTogether/private/full — not per-server state,
  since it hits three different jobs plus retry identically).
- Pinning one running instance goes in `jobId` (+ placeId): `v1/join-game`
  with a fresh job UUID returned `status 2` on that exact job with full
  auth material (ClientTicket + NetStackTokenValue both present).
- A `status 2` script WITHOUT `NetStackTokenValue`/`ClientTicket` can
  never start QUIC (both feed the RUPP TLV, see rupp.h). Never observed
  on v2/v1-join-game (always present); `configFromScript` now fails
  closed naming the missing field (`joinScript lacks X`, rc 2) instead
  of a session that dies later.
- Carrier note (DU-style UDP policing): TOS is one byte (0-255), so
  "ac41" (44193) is not a settable TOS value; the native client never
  sets TOS (50 setsockopt sites audited) so TOS 0 is parity and a
  TOS-0-policing carrier polices the official client identically; QUIC
  forbids IP fragmentation (RFC 9000 s14) and both sides already cap at
  1452B (native's value); sub-700B packets are routine (the server
  answers our 2/10/11B messages). No socket change can fix
  carrier-side UDP policing — VPN, same as for the official client.

## Recv v3: 0x03 solved, 100% of observed replication parsed (2026-10-12)

**Coverage (two live sessions, frozen + fresh):** every 0x83 chain walks
exact-close (3524/3524 frozen, 8510/8510 + 1 faithful-stop fresh) and
every 0x16 batch parses (frozen 9 records + 24 shared-strings + 4 empty;
fresh 9 + 24 + 3). Zero hard fails. The one fresh partial (@603831)
stops at chain byte 0x70, which is outside both native dispatch tables
(9-41 outer 32B3BA4, 1-22 inner 3439982) — the native throws there too,
so stop-with-applied-prefix is faithful replication, not a gap (the 7
NumberValue spawns after it verify clean in isolation).

**Shipped:** 0x10 (`[10][u32]`, 34899B8), 0x20 (`[20][u32][len][bytes]`,
35C3A7E), tuple-family events (tags 29-32 are SELF-DESCRIBING:
`[varint N][N x [u8 tag]([u16] iff 7)[payload]]` via 340887C — the old
"compiled-in element types" claim was wrong; Table/Map add string keys),
0x03 triples (`[ref][u16 prop][value]`, one per item, explicit 03 bytes),
flags=01 shared-string batches (`[16B key][u8 marker][varint len][bytes]`,
342550C), and the spawn_weird
script-data bulk (`[8B key-suffix][00][varint len][payload][anchor ref]`,
content-keyed by the spawn's own Source hash; anchor re-parents to
Folder 11928; 3725B payload + 00 end close exactly).

**0x03 prop table (solver-derived, 8 props):**
`248:68, 3796:61, 4989:18, 5018:61, 5469:18, 5868:3, 5918:46, 10145:25`
— every live chain closes exactly (negative controls: every alternative
shape parses strictly fewer messages). Goldens pin each, including
readable values (3796 = 77B emote table, 5018 = "Touch the beacon again
to heal you!"). 5469/4989 tie u16 against 2B string/asset/Name shapes on
all observed (constant-257) bytes — u16 shipped on plausibility,
documented. 248 forces 68 over either Content mode.

**Version-skew paradox (recorded honestly, not hidden):** live register
capture proves the runtime table holds 10146 entries (== prop_bound+1)
with per-prop numbers `{248:61, 3796:2, 4989:34, 5018:2, 5469:34,
5868:28, 5918:46, 10145:28}` (entry byte[9] == the v57 descriptor byte
340A3E4 dispatches on — triple-verified). IDA proves only tag 61
branches on the 0x03 flag (3409332 prefix vs plain); tags 2/3/18/25/28/
34/46 are mode-independent. So the traced client (2.739) would read
3796 as 2B-string, hit 0x48, and THROW (no message-level catch exists up
through 32B2322 — verified). The session survives anyway ⇒ an
(unlocated) packet-level catch must drop such packets. Theory: the
server (0.742) is NEWER than the traced client — skewed shapes the old
table rejects. Our parser reads the proven wire shapes (superset of what
2.739 accepts); anything unparseable drops per-message and counts, which
is the native's effective behavior. If a future session shows variance
breaking a shipped shape, the solver re-extends the table (method in
`/tmp/opencode/solv03.cpp` pattern — port to tools/ if it recurs).

**Still open (unobserved, not gaps):** native-handled types never seen
in 12k+ messages (e.g. 0x29 ref-lists, inner 4/6/8-22) stay loud on
first sight; NetAsset batches (flags=05) unobserved; the 0x97 tail
encoding uncracked (not needed — nothing observed keys off it); full
native number map (byte[9] × 10146) dump pending for unobserved-prop
priors; 0xA3 (163) seen once in one session (native-throwing range).

## Recv v4: second game (Brookhaven) — 89% via solver, skew confirmed (2026-10-12)

**Multi-game joins.** Place IDs exceed int32 (Brookhaven 4924922222
wrapped to 629954926 — every non-1818 join sent garbage; fixed with
int64 + `--game-id` universe passthrough). Brookhaven joins clean
without gameId. Join fail-closed hardened: v2/v1 now require
status==2 + object script (a status-12/null `joinScript` previously
returned "OK" then died at `joinScript lacks ...`). 0x97 outer magic is
per-game codec version (`a3 cf` Crossroads, `31 8b` Brookhaven — same
layout after it); unknown magics still fail loudly.

**Brookhaven coverage: 916 -> 7964/8497 pure (93.7%)**, batches 329/329.
Two systematic fixes did most of it: (1) sub_3486B9E flags&1 reads SIX
more u32s (our 24B-short parse derailed everything after; IDA-verified),
tail>1 is item-drop-not-throw (walker now skips+counts, faithful);
(2) tools/solve_proptags.cpp (checked in): exact-close search over
culprit props, ties keep native, oscillation guard (612 flip 39<->3 =
ambiguous, keeps higher-pure endpoint + bans), merge policy >= 4 closes.
28 overrides total (8 Crossroads + 20 Brookhaven), e.g. 3755: 22->65
(13B; the `01`-then-12B pattern), 3758: 7->2 (string), five 9->2,

Crossroads-solved tags (248/5469/4989) also close in Brookhaven:
the table is cross-game consistent (global until a contradiction;
a same-prop/different-tag win across games is the per-join signal,
which would mean parsing the uncracked 0x97 tail).

**Residue (Brookhaven, honest):** 461 stops + 72 hards. Throw-range stops
are flat (no dominant kind => derail victims, not a hidden systematic
type) except 11 RARE outer types with real sightings (09/0B/11/14/18/
1D/1E/24/25/27/28, 25 stops — grammars in hand, implementation queued).
0x01 truncations x10 and 0x29 overcounts x3 look native-faithful drops
(pending one IDA read on 3481460's short form). Remaining 0x03 hards are

**Fuzz-found OOM (fixed):** untrusted u32 decomp sized allocations in
parseItem0d/parseDeltaBatch/parseJoinOuter (4GB via crafted 0x0d).
All three capped at 64MB (~200x observed max) before allocating, plus

bytes). Fuzzer artifact kept as regression goldens (3 tests).
