fine) — with the wrong token the server never sees them at all, which is why
the join previously looked like a bad 0x90 body.

### 8.6 The pre-join pad (1160 B)

Right after `0x13` the native sends a split (2 fragments, split id 0,
*unordered*, rel 0x50) message whose body is 1160 bytes of zeroes except
`0a 8c` at offset 7-8. Captured verbatim as `run/raknet_pre_join.bin` and
replayed as-is; it may be a capability/pad blob, and it is cheap to keep.

### 8.7 Verification

* `client/test/join_msg_test.cpp` (ctest `join_msg_raknet`, vectors in `run/`):
  17 checks, all passing — seal/unseal byte-exactness for the 0x90 body,
  `build8a` == the captured 0x8a message (1793 B), `build90` == the captured
  payload, the ticket hash, the derived 0x8a key, the 0x92 shape.
* live: full handshake + join from our client, server ACKs, replication stream.
* `tools/raknet_seal.py <capture-dir>` re-derives and re-checks the envelope
  from any harness capture (it parses every datagram, reassembles splits and
  compares seal() against the capture).

### 8.8 Retractions from §7

* §7.9's "the serializer for the join bodies is `sub_448BA24`" was wrong: that
  function is the *receive* handler ("Incoming packet error…") and the
  watchpoint had caught buffer reuse. The real builders are in §8.2/§8.3.
* §7.3's "the bodies are bit-packed, not wrapped" is superseded: they are
  bit-packed *and* sealed (§8.1). Our AEAD hooks never saw this because it is a
  different cipher path entirely (custom chained AES-128, not the datagram
  AEAD).

### 8.9 Remaining gaps (honest list)

1. The server retransmits its baseline (`msg 5/7/8`: `0x03`, `0x93`, `0x65`)
   about once a second even while streaming. Our ACKs match the native's
   captured 7-byte ACK format, so this is most likely the client-side "ready"
   traffic the native sends after the join (`0x97`, `0x9b` are the known
   builders) — needs a native *post-join TX* capture to pin. The session is
   alive and receiving in the meantime.
2. `0x92`'s varint: the captured value's low bit does not match what
   `sub_63D8F36`'s decompilation implies for the sign term, so `build92` is
   shape-exact (id + a 10-byte varint recovering the rand) but not value-exact.
   Harmless: the value is a fresh client RNG u32 by design.
3. `0x8f` sends an empty server id; the native's `sub_25BEB42` string source is
   not pinned.
4. The 0x90/0x8a templates are captures from the `mizg` account. The JSON merge
   rewrites every field the join script also has (identity fields included), so
   the payload stays coherent across sessions of the same account; a different
   account should recapture (`--90-template` / `--8a-template` override the
   paths). Building the flag list from scratch is still open.
5. `--save-join` writes the join script + reply for offline rebuilds; the
   template tail is read from the template, so a recapture keeps the derived
   0x8a key correct.

## 2026-10-27 (later) — RbxTransport restored beside RakNet (nothing lost)

Owner decision: RakNet stays the gameplay transport, but the archived
RbxTransport/QUIC stack (join → dummy session → 0x9B solve) is a separate
asset and must not rot out of the tree. It is restored **verbatim** from
`f28c1c8` — `client/src/quic_client.{h,cpp}`, `session.{h,cpp}`,
`send.{h,cpp}`, `client/test/hs_main.cpp`, `client/test/play_main.cpp`,
`tests/unit/unit_messages.cpp` are byte-identical to the archive commit.

Build glue is new, because the archive's CMake predates `rbx_join`:

- `rbx_transport` (ngtcp2 `ExternalProject` + `quic_client.cpp`), `hs1818`,
  `rbx_session` (`send.cpp` + `session.cpp`), `rbxplay` (+ `rbx_view`).
- `rbx_session` **links** `rbx_join` instead of recompiling `join.cpp`: one
  HTTPS join implementation — the v2-dropped, universe-id-fixed one — serves
  both transports, so the restored tree cannot drift from the live join
  code. `play_main.cpp` still calls `rbx::joinGame`/`loadJoin`; the
  signatures it needs are unchanged.
- `option(RBX_TRANSPORT … ON)` at the time; it is `OFF` since 2026-10-28.
  Leaving it off drops the stack and the ngtcp2 build entirely and leaves
  `rbx_raknet` exactly as it is.
  `unit_messages` is registered only `if(TARGET rbx_session)`.

Coexistence boundary: the two transports share exactly one file, `rupp.h`
(the framing both speak). No RakNet TU includes a RbxTransport header and no
RbxTransport TU includes a RakNet header — separate static libs, separate
executables, no shared symbols.

**Verified 2026-10-27.** Configure + build of all four restored targets
clean on the first try (the archive sources still compile against today's
headers). `unit_messages` 11/11 cases, 101 assertions (Python-reference
golden bytes). `ctest -R 'unit-messages|join_msg_raknet|unit-rupp|message-parity|handshake-parity'`
→ 5/5. Live `rbxplay --place-id 1818 --headless`: full native-parity QUIC
handshake (`dummy handshake=OK`, `[quic] handshake confirmed`), server
streams 3/7/11 open, 40 dummy RX, ~10 s of ping/sync, then the server drains
(`ERR_DRAINING`) — the same dead end that motivated RakNet, preserved rather
than re-broken. No 0x9B challenge arrived in this run.

## 2026-10-28 — one binary, two transports: `./roblox --connection-type`

The transports used to be separate executables (`raknet1818`, `rbxplay`), so
every flag and path had to be remembered per tool. Now
`client/apps/roblox_main.cpp` is the only `main`; `raknet_main.cpp` and
`play_main.cpp` expose `rbxclient::raknetMain` / `rbxclient::transportMain`
(`client/apps/entry.h`) and keep their own parsers and run loops untouched.

Selection:

- `--connection-type=RakNet` / `--connection-type=RbxTransport`
  (case-insensitive, `rbx-transport` accepted) forces one transport, with no
  fallback.
- Omitted (or `auto`): RbxTransport is probed first with a 20 s deadline.
  `transportMain(probe_ms)` returns 3 when no challenge was solved in time;
  a run that ends without a transport/solve failure is success. Anything else
  hands the same argv to RakNet. A SIGINT during the probe stops the process
  instead of falling back — `transportMain` reports `interrupted` separately
  from `g_stop`, which the headful path also sets when its window closes.
- A command line carrying RakNet-only options (`--seconds`, join templates,
  token/port knobs, …) skips the probe: the RbxTransport parser would only
  reject it as a usage error.

Build time: `RBX_RAKNET` (default `ON`) and `RBX_TRANSPORT` (default `OFF`)
decide what is linked into `roblox`; asking for neither is a configure error. The dispatcher is
compiled with `RBX_HAVE_RAKNET` / `RBX_HAVE_TRANSPORT`; a request for a
transport that is not in the binary exits 2 and names the switch. Display
flags (`--headless`, `--headful`, `--shot`) are accepted (and ignored) by
RakNet so the automatic selector can pass one command line to either.

**Verified 2026-10-28.** Default build: clean, `ctest` 17/17. RakNet-only
(`-DRBX_TRANSPORT=OFF`) and RbxTransport-only (`-DRBX_RAKNET=OFF`) both
configure, build and link `roblox`; `-DRBX_RAKNET=OFF -DRBX_TRANSPORT=OFF`
fails configure with the expected message. Dispatch exercised against the
saved `run/join_full.json`: forced RakNet reaches `Req1` (endpoint is stale,
so no Reply1), forced RbxTransport reaches the QUIC handshake, auto prints
the probe banner and falls back, `--seconds` skips the probe, and each
single-transport binary rejects the absent transport with exit 2.

**Layout (same day).** `client/src` was one flat directory mixing two
transports with the solver, engine and viewer, so it now mirrors the
subsystems: `common/` (RUPP, util, xxh32 — the only code both transports
share), `raknet/`, `quic/`, `join/`, `solve/`, `engine/`, `view/`. Entry
points moved out of the tree entirely into `client/apps/` (`roblox_main.cpp`
plus one main per transport), and client test executables into
`client/tests/`. Includes are rooted at `client/src/` and spell the group
(`#include "quic/session.h"`), which also removes the old ambiguity between
`session.h` (QUIC) and the RakNet session crypto. `rbx_runtime` already had
`api/` + `instance/`, so it was left as is.

The move broke exactly one thing: `tables-parity`, because the generator
wrote `client/src/tables.inc`. The generator, `gen_proptable.py`, the fuzz
harness source list and the README/FINDINGS paths were updated with the
move; `ctest` is 17/17 again and `roblox` still dispatches both transports.

**DRY pass (same day).** With the layout settled, the duplication it exposed
was removed rather than left as parallel copies:

- `client/src/common/cli.h` — one option parser. `raknet_main.cpp` and
  `play_main.cpp` each hand-rolled the same tokeniser (`next()` lambda, string
  compares, missing-value handling); now each declares a `cli::Spec` of
  switches/value options and the parser handles `--opt value` *and*
  `--opt=value` with consistent errors. `roblox_main.cpp` keeps its own loop:
  it must forward argv to the chosen transport untouched.
- `client/src/common/hex.h` — `hexDecode` / `hexDecodeInto` / `hexEncode` /
  `hexPrefix` / `hexU32` / `hexU64`. This replaced four pair-decoders
  (`raknet_main`'s `hexTo16`+`hexToBytes`, `send.cpp`'s `hx`,
  `session.cpp`'s `hx`) and three formatters (`hex_prefix` in hs_main,
  `hex64` in play_main, the last-datagram dump).
- `client/src/join/target.{h,cpp}` — one reading of a joinScript: Udmux edge,
  RCC address/port, NetStackPort, RUPP token, ClientTicket, and the
  `EphemeralEarlyPubKey` keyring walk plus the two early-auth blobs. That
  logic existed three times (raknet_main, play_main's `configFromScript`,
  hs_main's inline copy); policy stays with the caller (a missing token is
  fatal for QUIC, overridable by `--token` for RakNet).
- `client/src/quic/config.{h,cpp}` — joinScript + `RBX_TOS` / `RBX_MAX_UDP` /
  `RBX_DEST_PORT` / `RBX_RUPP_PORT` → `QuicClient::Config`, shared by
  `play_main` and `hs1818`. `hs1818` also loads joins through
  `rbx::loadJoin` now instead of its own JSON reader.
- `client/src/raknet/join_flow.{h,cpp}` — the native post-handshake sequence
  (pre-join pad, 0x90, 0x92, 0x8a, 0x8f) out of `main`, so the CLI is only
  arguments and config wiring.
- `join_msg.cpp` drops its private `xxh32` and calls `rbxclient::xxh32`
  (`common/xxh32.h`); the KAT still pins the resulting `ticketHash`.

Python: `tools/bc_common.py` holds the LEB128 `varint` that six bytecode
decoders carried verbatim (`decode_roblox_bc`, `decode_wire_bc`,
`opcode_map`, `standardize_wire`, `disasm_roblox_bc`, `patch_decoded_bc`,
plus `align_check`'s import), and `tools/zstd_lib.py` is the one libzstd
wrapper (`challenge_blob`, `repl_survey`). `py/rbx_client.py` imports
`frame` / `stream_header` from `py/msgbuild.py` (its local `frame` was a
strict subset and its `compact_varint` was dead code with a misleading name).

**Verified.** Build clean (`roblox`, `hs1818`, `join_msg_test`), `ctest`
17/17 including `join_msg_raknet` (17 KATs) and `message-parity`, every
touched Python module imports, and the unified binary still reports
`--place-id needs a value` / `unknown arg --bogus` and reaches `Req1` with
`--connection-type=RakNet --join-json run/join_full.json`.

## 2026-10-28 — RakNet and RbxTransport share the application layer (verified)

Question from the owner: do the two transports parse the same replication
packets, so RakNet can be piped through the same C++ API? Answer: **yes, at
the application-message layer — no, at the framing layer.** Both are now
proven and the shared path is implemented, not assumed.

### 1. What is shared

Every RakNet user packet, once the datagram AEAD is opened and RakNet's
reliability/split framing is removed, is **one complete application message
`[id][body]`** — the same id space and the same bodies the RbxTransport path
carries inside `app=4/chan=1` frames. Evidence:

* Our own join reuses the RbxTransport builders byte-for-byte: the RakNet
  `0x90` body and `0x8a` ticket are the templates pinned by
  `join_msg_test` (`run/raknet90_template.bin` / `raknet8a_*`), i.e. the
  message layer is transport-independent by construction.
* Live place-1818 run (2026-10-28, `mizg`): reconstructed server messages are
  `0x10` (104 B accepted/address list), `0x00`/`0x03` 1160 B keepalive pads,
  `0x51`/`0x52`/`0x53` 9 B pings, and `0x93` (1816 B). Every one of them is a
  self-contained app message; their ids and shapes are the ones the QUIC
  session already handles (`quic/session.h`).
* IDA (libroblox.so, 2.739.691): the RbxTransport side hands each channel
  message to a handler object — `sub_340C72C` calls a vtable slot with
  `(chan, data, len)` — and the native's RakNet side is a separate connection
  class (`RBX::Network::RakNetConnection` RTTI, `DFLog::RakNetConnectTrace`)
  that feeds the same network/replication layer. The transports differ in
  framing and reliability, not in what the bytes mean.

### 2. What is not shared (and now is)

QUIC prefixes each message with a compact-varint length; RakNet prefixes each
message with reliability headers, bit-lengths and a 10-byte split header.
`client/src/raknet/rx_stream.{h,cpp}` is the missing inverse: it walks a
decrypted datagram, drops ACKs, reassembles split messages by split id
(out-of-order fragments included, duplicate fragments dropped) and delivers
ordered messages by ordering index. `RaknetClient::setMessageSink` hands each
payload to the app.

Both transports now end in the same parser:
`LiveRuntime::feedMessage(payload, len)` (new) writes `compactVarint(len) +
payload` and calls the existing `feed()`, so `0x83` chains and `0x97` join
data go through `solve/replicate.h` + the Engine runtime exactly as the QUIC
path does. `raknet_main` installs that sink and reports `live:` stats.

### 3. The 0x93 message, decoded exactly (1816 B)

```
[93][u16 LE count=47] then count × [u16 LE nameLen][name][u16 LE valLen][value]
```
47 FFlag name/value pairs, consuming 1816/1816 bytes ("UserPlayerScriptsRefactor2=true",
"CLI198718Ver=3", "RCCChannelName=production", ...). This is a server flag
list, **not** replication; earlier notes that called `0x93` a snapshot were
reading split fragments as message ids.

### 4. ACKs: use the native's explicit-max form

Every captured native ACK is 10 bytes per range:
`c0 <count u16 BE> 00 <min u24 LE> <max u24 LE>` — including single-datagram
ranges. Our compact 7-byte "minEqualsMax" form is not what the native sends;
`flushAcks` now always writes min+max. The server's pads did **not** stop, and
the reason is that they are not retransmissions: their embedded timestamp
advances (`…64b2d4…` → `…64c65d…`) and each is a fresh split message. The
server is idling, not retrying.

### 5. RakNet is NOT finished — honest gap list

What works: offline handshake (Req1/Reply1/Req2/Reply2 + verified tag), data
AEAD both directions, `0x09`, `0x13`, `0x90`, `0x92`, `0x8a`, `0x8f`, ACKs,
duplicate suppression, full message reassembly (27 messages / 17 splits in a
6 s run), and the shared replication parser wired up.

Missing, in the order it blocks the join:
1. **`0x9b` is never sent.** The native sends a 9-byte `0x9b` after `0x8f`
   (captured: `9b 03 7e 65 d8 59 bf 66 6f`); the RbxTransport path builds the
   same message from the solved challenge, and the RakNet flow has no
   challenge solve at all.
2. **No `0x97` join data / `0x83` replication arrives.** The server answers the
   join with flags + pads + pings and then idles. Until (1) is done the world
   cannot start, so the RX de-framer has only been exercised on control
   messages — its replication path is unit-tested, not live-proven.
3. `0x92`'s value is shape-exact but not value-exact (fresh rand by design),
   and `0x8f`'s server id is empty (FINDINGS §8.9 items 2–3 still stand).
4. `0x90`/`0x8a` templates are `mizg` captures; another account needs a recap.

`RBX_RAKNET_DUMP=<dir>` writes every authenticated datagram (post-decrypt) as
`dNNNNNN.bin` for offline work — that is how the 0x93 layout above was pinned.

**Verified.** `ctest` 18/18, including the new `raknet_rx_stream` (17 checks:
split reassembly incl. out-of-order and duplicates, ordering gaps, ACK
skipping, truncated-datagram rejection). Live place-1818 run decodes the
server's messages and reports `app=27 split=17` with `live:` stats intact.

## 2026-10-28 — the official web client SHIPPED (0.745); `gameFqdn` is live

This supersedes the 2026-10-26 note that "the web client has not shipped yet" and
"`*.qdmux.roblox.com` is not serving". Both are stale: the browser client runs
(server `WebClientApp` 0.745.0.7451142, upload `version-f4a4dba57f874ad0`) and it
really opens WebTransport to a per-session `qdmux` edge.

**Browser requirement: WebGPU, not WebGL.** Headless Chrome/SwiftShader fails with
`Failed to create WebGPU Context Provider` → `[WasmPlayer] startup failed:
TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'getSupportedExtensions')`
and the engine never starts. Use a real GPU (`DISPLAY=:0 google-chrome`, headed).
`setup.rbxcdn.com` also 403s non-browser UAs (e.g. `Freebuff/... Electron/33`), and
after ~50 rapid fetches it 403s everything for a few minutes — one join per run only.

**The join call (captured from inside the engine's `stub.js` worker):**

```
POST https://gamejoin.roblox.com/v1/join-game
Content-Type: application/json
Requester: Client
Cache-Control: no-cache
IsFtsThumbAccepted: true
rbx-user-agent: Roblox/Web Unknown (8192MB; 1366x768; 96x96; 1366x768; ; Linux)
                Browser/Chrome/155.0.0.0 RobloxApp/0.745.0.7451142 (GlobalDist; RobloxDirectDownload)
Referer: https://play.roblox.com/

{"placeId":6597976680,"gameJoinAttemptId":"<uuid4>"}
```

- It is **game join v1** with a two-field body. Extra fields are not required;
  `rbx-user-agent: Roblox/Web …` is what makes the server treat it as the web client.
  Without it, the same request returns `status:12 "Unable to join Game 211"`.
- With it, an owned/enrolled place returns `status:2` and a 70-key join script whose
  **`gameFqdn = "{32-hex}-{8-hex}-{4-hex}.{vip-hex}.qdmux.roblox.com"`** (same grammar
  as `sub_646AD8D`). `ServerPort` / `UdmuxEndpoints[0].Port` carry the UDP port.
- `/v2/join-game` answers **404** for this exact contract, so v2 needs a different
  body/route; `finishJoinGamePostV2` and `/v1|/v2/join-game` both exist in the wasm.
- Native (WinInet) join scripts contain **no** `gameFqdn` — the field is web-only.

**The transport URL, captured live and matching the one the owner pasted:**

```
https://dc2e70d00ec71457a73d1dc984b30baa-0a1203da-fc09.80742022.qdmux.roblox.com:64521/wt
        ?X-Rbx-Capabilities=002dc3c750b30892&X-Rbx-Close-Reason=utf8-v1
→ Network.webTransportConnectionEstablished, then
  [FLog::NetworkClient] Transport selection: … canUseRbxTransport=true
  [FLog::RbxTransportDummyClient] Connected to server at 128.116.32.34:61636
  [FLog::Network] Assigned peer id = 5, Replicator created, gameLoaded
```

So `002dc3c750b30892` is the capability token from the native 0x8a `UniqueNu` family,
and the port in the URL is the server's UDP port (the RCC listens on it for QUIC too).

**Why the web client only joins "our own" games — server-side, not client-side.**
The same authenticated, correctly-shaped request for place 1818 answers
`status:12 "You are not allowed to play this game. (Playtesters Only)"`. The web
client is in a limited playtest: universes are enrolled server-side (own places are,
Crossroads is not). Nothing in the request body or headers changes that, and the
client surfaces it as `[LIFECYCLE] joinGameFailed state=12` / "Not authorized to
join this experience (Error Code: -1)". Bypassing it would require the universe to
be enrolled; the native/RakNet path is unaffected and still joins 1818.

**Tooling.** `tools/cdp/probe.js` (zero deps, `node --experimental-websocket`) launches
system Chrome with a stock UA, injects `~/.ROBLOSECURITY`, auto-attaches to the page
*and its `stub.js` workers* (the engine's HTTP is invisible from the page session
alone), and dumps console/network/WebTransport lifecycle to JSON + JSONL.

## 2026-10-28 — RakNet: join reply accepted, challenge still withheld (measured)

Live place-1818 run after the session/0x8a fixes now completes the whole native
order and gets the join reply:

```
0xA7 (opt-in only) -> 0x90 body 10931B / json 9415B (11 dgrams) -> 0x92 ->
0x8a ticket 1774B (2 dgrams) -> 0x8f
<= 0x93 join reply 1816B (rel 0x70, ord 1), then 0x51/0x52/0x53 pings, 0x00/0x03
   keepalives ... and no 0x9B, ever (45 s window, 42 app messages, 78 datagrams)
```

- **`0xA7` is a RbxTransport-only trigger.** The captured native *RakNet* session
  never sends one (its ordered messages are 0x13, 0x90, 0x92, 0x8a, 0x8f, 0x9b),
  and injecting it shifts every later ordering index. It is now `--a7 FILE`
  (default: not sent), not part of the sequence.
- **The challenge is genuinely absent from the wire**, not lost in the RX
  reassembler: `RBX_RAKNET_DUMP` (154 authenticated datagrams) contains no 0x9B
  message (the byte hits are ciphertext inside sealed 0x90/0x8a fragments).
- **Content diverges from the native**, which is the remaining suspect:
  | message | native | ours | delta |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `0x90` sealed body | 10785 B | 10931 B | **+146** |
  | `0x8a` sealed body | 1729 B | 1774 B | **+45** |
  | `0x13` address msg | 102 B | 99 B | **-3** |
- Native `0x13` layout, from `/tmp/rkc32/dgram_2.bin`:
  `13` + 11 x `04 <ip4> <port2>` (self, `04 80 ff fe fe 00 00`, then 9 x
  `04 ff ff ff ff 00 00`) + **24-byte tail** (`0000 0000 01ad20db 0000 0000
  0000 0a66 0006 5eda c21a 5abb` in that sample). Our builder sends self + the
  real server address + 12 dummies and no tail, so both the entry count/content
  and the tail are wrong.
- Next: unseal both native bodies (`tools/raknet_seal.py`) and diff the JSON
  field by field to find the 146 B excess; that is the smallest remaining gap
  and the server parses it before it will challenge.

### 2026-10-28 follow-up: the join bytes are faithful; the gate is not the payload

Unsealed both sides with `tools/raknet_seal.py` (`open_`) and diffed:

- **`0x90` body**: same key set as the native, same order; the only length delta is
  `__joinTicket` (+172 B) and it decomposes into account-only values
  (`PlayerSignals.timespent_sum…`, `ChosenDcPing`, `TextChatBucketsBitmask`). The
  embedded `SerializedClientFields` base64 also matches key-for-key (2804 vs 2856 B,
  delta is `SessionId` string length + our %-encoded key).
- **`0x8a` body**: identical shape (`LEB(zigzag(uid))`, `LEB(510)`, ticket, session
  doc, `C0 01 CA FE` trailer); plaintext 1774 vs native 1721, again account values.
- **Ruled out as the challenge trigger**, each by live test:
  - `0xA7` queue — sent (35 B, count 2): no 0x9B; and the native RakNet capture has none.
  - `0x13` content — replayed the native payload **verbatim** (102 B,
    `RBX_RAKNET_13=/tmp/native13.bin`): no 0x9B. Ours is 99 B with 14 entries instead of
    RakNet's 11 (external + 10 internal) + 24-byte extension, but that is not the gate.
  - `0x8a`/`0x8f` burst timing — 250 ms pump between them: no 0x9B.
- Our HTTPS egress and the session's `ClientIpAddress` agree (104.28.193.113), so a
  join-vs-UDP address mismatch is not it either.

Debug knobs added while doing this: `RBX_DUMP_JOIN90=<path>` (writes `<path>.json` +
`<path>.body`), `RBX_DUMP_JOIN8A=<path>`, `RBX_RAKNET_13=<file>` (replay a captured
ID_NEW_INCOMING_CONNECTION). All are read-only diagnostics.

**Remaining unexplored:** the server accepts the join (0x93) and then pings forever, so
whatever gates the anti-cheat is outside the six client messages we can compare — most
likely a RUPP/datagram-layer attribute (e.g. the control-datagram pattern the native
emits: `81000000…` guid announce, `c0` ACK cadence) or a per-session server-side
attestation. Next probe should replay the native's *first ten datagrams* structure
(ids + sizes + ACK cadence) rather than message content.

### Complete native RakNet TX timeline (2026-10-28, decoded datagram by datagram)

`tools/raknet_timeline.py` decodes both the native capture (`--native /tmp/rkc7`) and our
own plaintext TX dump (`RBX_RAKNET_TXDUMP=<dir>`, `--dump <dir>`) with one parser:

```
dgram 0   CONN_REQ(24B kind=2)
dgram 1   NEW_INCOMING(102B ord=0) + 9B ping
dgram 2-6 PAD 00 (1061+99) x2, interleaved with 51/52/53 (9B) and a 25B 0x03
ACK x5
dgram 7-11 PAD 03 (1061+99) x2 + 54/55/56 (25B) + 0x03
dgram 12-22 JOIN90 11 frags ord=1, **0x92 piggybacked on the last fragment datagram**
dgram 23-24 TICKET 2 frags ord=3, **0x8f piggybacked on the last fragment datagram**
ACK x2
dgram 25  **0x9B answer (9B, ord=5)** -> then replication
```

Facts that fall out of this and were not in the notes before:

- `0x92` and `0x8f` are **not** separate datagrams in the native; they ride the last
  fragment datagram of `0x90` / `0x8a` respectively. (Ours sends them alone. Harmless —
  proved by test — but it is a visible difference.)
- The native's ping echo (`0x51..0x56`, `0x03`) is sent **unreliable (kind 0)**; ours is
  reliable (kind 2).
- The 9-byte `0x9b` at ord 5 is an **answer**, not a request: sending it with zero
  fields, and sending the native's captured bytes verbatim, both change nothing — no
  challenge arrives and the server does not kick. So the RCC's decision to offer the
  anti-cheat challenge is not driven by anything the client sends after `0x8f`.
- `~/Rum/build/roblox` (the APK wrapper) cannot be run as an oracle on this machine:
  there is no `libroblox.so` or `assets/` next to it, only the `mocktail` host binary.
  The only native ground truth remains the captured sessions in `/tmp/rkc*`.

## 2026-10-28 — native RX capture: RUPP subtype semantics, and what is NOT the gate

Captured a live native session with **both directions**: `recvfrom` hooked in
`tools/gdb/rc_capture.gdb` (`wire_rx_*.bin`, 334 datagrams, RUPP header intact)
plus the 300 `wire_tx_*.bin` frames and both KDFs. All 334 RX datagrams decrypt
with the data key derived from `KDF#1` (BLAKE2b(shared||pub||peer)), which
re-confirms the data-key derivation.

### The RUPP header is a per-sender identity, not a handshake token

| side | subtype | token | when |
|---|---|---|---|
| client | 1 | join token (NetStackTokenValue) | Req1, Req2, **0x09 CONNECTION_REQUEST** |
| client | 2 | its own 16 random bytes | 0x13 onward, **rotated** every few seconds |
| server | 2 | its own 16 random bytes | every data datagram, also rotated |

Client tokens in one session: `1511cfad…` (85 datagrams), `abf40b45…` (57),
`3dc869f5…` (105), `52f7f102…` (50). Server tokens: `b4c84eb2…`, `8152bda8…`,
`50a102ca…`. **The sets are disjoint** — the token is never the peer's token
echoed back. Decrypting the whole server stream and searching it for the
client's tokens finds nothing, so the peer never learns them; it is a local
label. Consequence for this client: echoing Reply2's TLV1 token (what it used
to do) is wrong, and the token must not be switched before 0x09.

### What is ruled out (all measured, not reasoned)

* **The join body is not the gate.** Replaying the native's captured 0x90
  message verbatim (`RBX_JOIN90_RAW`, 10785 B, 11 fragments) still yields 0x93
  + keepalives and no 0x9B.
* **Join timing is not the gate.** Waiting for the server's 0x93 settings push
  before sending the join (the native's order; `netSettingsSeen()`) changes
  nothing.
* **TLV2 is validated.** `02 06 <ip> <port>` must be the join script's server
  address + NetStackPort, on *every* datagram including Req1. Replacing the
  port with the real UDP endpoint port makes the server stop answering Req1.
* **AEAD counters are continuous** from LE64("UniqueNu") across the subtype
  switch, and ACK datagrams consume counters too (native deltas 0,1,2,…).

### The remaining blocker, stated precisely

With subtype 1 the RCC answers everything (ACKs, the 1816 B 0x93 flag list,
keepalive pads/pings — 278 datagrams / 172 KB in a 12 s run) but never promotes
the session to the game phase. With subtype 2 + the ordering fix (0x09 under
subtype 1, then subtype 2) the server ACKs 0x09, sends `0x10 ACCEPTED` three
times, and then **stops ACKing entirely** — identically with its own token
(`RBX_SERVER_TOKEN=1`) and with ours. So the RCC accepts subtype-2 datagrams
only under a condition not yet reproduced; subtype 1 stays the default and the
subtype-2 path is `--token-sub 2`.

### New knobs / diagnostics in the client

`RBX_JOIN90_RAW=<file>` (replay a captured 0x90), `RBX_RAKNET_WIRE=<dir>`
(exact bytes handed to sendto), `RBX_SESSION_TOKEN=<32 hex>` (pin the token),
`RBX_SERVER_TOKEN=1` (echo the server's token), plus the 0x93-before-join wait
and the session-token/header log line.

## RUPP subtype 2: the acceptance condition (2026-10-28)

Question: under what condition does the RCC accept subtype-2 datagrams?

Setup: full two-way native capture decrypted with `tools/raknet_native_rx.py`
(334/334 RX datagrams; the data-layer counter is *hinted*, not `base+n` — the
first captured frame sits at `base+0x4aec`), compared datagram-for-datagram
with our client via `tools/raknet_timeline.py --dump`.

### What the native actually does

| datagram | subtype | token | message |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | join token | `0x09` CONNECTION_REQUEST (24B, identical to ours) |
| 1 | 2 | **new** 16B own token | `0x13` + 9B ping |
| 2..6 | 2 | same | MTU pads (0x00/0x03), 0x51..0x53 |

Tokens are **self-declared local labels** that each side rotates independently
every ~100 datagrams (client `1511cfad/abf40b45/3dc869f5/52f7f102`; server
`b4c84eb2/8152bda8/50a102ca`; disjoint sets, never echoed). The switch to
subtype 2 happens exactly at the `0x13`.

### Measured: the RCC drops every subtype-2 datagram from our path

Signal = how many times the RCC retransmits its `0x10 ACCEPTED` (a datagram it
retransmits until our ACK for it lands):

| our ACK | token | `0x10` copies |
|---|---|---|
| subtype 1 | join | **1** (accepted) |
| subtype 2 | own | 4 |
| subtype 2 | join | 4 |
| subtype 2 | server's | 4 |

So the token is irrelevant and the ACK body is byte-identical to the native's
(`c0 0001 00 000000 xxxxxx`): *any* subtype-2 datagram we send is dropped,
while client->server subtype 1 is processed and server->client subtype 2 is
fine. The native's subtype-2 traffic works because its captured session talks
to an RCC directly (`10.190.0.5:50105`); we reach the game only through the
udmux edge (`128.116.x.x:49xxx`), and the internal RCC is not routable from
this host (`ip route get 10.190.0.5` -> LAN gateway). TLV2 cannot be changed to
compensate: advertising the peer endpoint instead of `NetStackPort` makes the
edge stop answering `Req1` outright.

**Conclusion: keep subtype 1 for the whole session.** The native's switch is an
artifact of its direct RCC connection, not a protocol requirement we can meet
through the edge.

### Bugs this produced (all fixed in client/src/raknet/raknet.cpp)

1. **ACKs consumed datagram numbers.** The native's `0x13` is datagram 1; ours
   was 2 because an ACK took id 1. ACK frames carry no datagram id on the wire,
   so they must not advance it (`sealAndSend(..., data=false)`).
2. **ACKs were flushed once per second** (app loop: `pump(1000); sendAcks();`)
   while the RCC retransmit timer is ~250 ms. It resent its `0x93` and both pad
   fragments indefinitely and the reliable window never advanced. ACKing from
   inside the receive path took one 12 s run from 15 -> 145 ACKs and 535 -> 150
   datagrams, and stopped the `0x93` retransmissions.
3. **Pad echo loop.** We answered every 1160-byte `0x00` pad with another pad
   and the RCC answered that (measured 131 pad echoes / 384 kB in 10 s). The
   native's whole exchange is one `0x00` and one `0x03`; the reply is capped.

### Where it stands (subtype 1, 12 s run)

- `0x10 ACCEPTED`, `0x93` (1816B, before the join), pads and pings all arrive.
- Join sequence matches the native exactly: `0x13` ord=0, `0x90` ord=1 (11
  fragments), `0x92` ord=2, `0x8a` ord=3, `0x8f` ord=4 — all ACKed by the RCC.
- **The RCC never issues the `0x9B` challenge**, so `0x97` join data never comes.
- The native's next datagram after `0x8F` is a **9-byte `0x9B`**
  (`9b f1a6f265 2a33dc31`, ordered index 5) inside the same millisecond. Our
  client only ever sends `0x9B` in reply to a >=13-byte server challenge
  (`isChallenge`), so it never sends one. Whether that `0x9B` is spontaneous or
  answers a sub-millisecond challenge is the open question: replay it right
  after `0x8F` and watch for `0x97`.

---

## RakNet join SOLVED (2026-10-28) — replication reached

Root cause of "the RCC never challenges, so `0x97` never comes": **our own
pre-join MTU pad exchange.** Sending the four 1160-byte pads (0x00/0x00/0x03/
0x03) before the join makes the RCC answer `0x93` and then only pad at the
client forever; the `0x9B` challenge never arrives. With those pads off, the
challenge lands 1-2 datagrams after `0x93`, the solver answers it, `0x97`
arrives and the world builds. The exchange is now opt-in (`RBX_PRE_PAD=1`).

That was invisible for so long because two transport bugs sat on top of it:

4. **ACK encodes a *type tag*, not a min==max flag.** Native ACKs are
   `c0 <count u16 BE> { [01][id u24] | [00][lo u24][hi u24] }`; we wrote `00`
   for every entry and then seven bytes, so every range ACK was unparseable and
   the RCC ignored it (i.e. it kept retransmitting its baseline).
5. **ACKs must name the highest reliable *message number*** of the datagram,
   not its datagram id. The RCC resends the same pad message under a fresh
   datagram id each time, so acking ids never acknowledges its message and it
   retransmits forever (measured: 1224 identical 1160-byte pads in 8 s). Acking
   message numbers took the same window to 196 datagrams. Default now;
   `RBX_ACK_MSGNO=0` reverts.

Also fixed on the way:
- `0x97` codec magic rolls with server builds; the live one here is `ba d3`
  (`97 ba d3 00 00 1e 76 05 ...`, same `[00 00][u32][zstd]` layout).
- `hexU32` returned a shared `static char[]`, so `u1`/`u2` in one log line
  could print the same value.
- The `0x9B` after `0x8F` is a join step with two client-generated u32s, not a
  challenge answer (the native sends exactly one per session and never a second
  one after a challenge); it is now sent unconditionally at ord 5.

Measured with the default binary (`./roblox --connection-type=raknet
--place-id 1818 --roblosecurity <cookie>`), 8 s windows:

    raknet: app message 0x97 (54211B, rel 0x70, ord 3)
    join schema classes=638 members=10322 shared=127
    world spawned=3286 grafted=53 instances=3286 engine=on
    raknet: live: live_frames=16 schema=yes spawn=162 events=45 fired=45

5/6 sessions join and spawn 2600-3400 instances. A session that gets no `0x9B`
within 2.5 s is retried by resending the join sequencer (fresh ordering
indexes).

## RakNet join 6/6 (2026-10-28)

The remaining misses were not join-data problems. A failing session gets 3
server ACKs where a good one gets 5-7, with the same single `0x93` in both:
the RCC stops reading our reliable stream part-way through the 11-fragment
`0x90`, so it never reaches the `0x9B` challenge and the ordered stream stays
blocked behind a hole. Two changes close it:

1. **Pace split fragments** (`RBX_SPLIT_PACE_MS`, default 2 ms). Blasting the
   11 fragments back-to-back loses the session ~1 run in 3; a small pace
   matches the native's queue drain (~20 ms for the whole join).
2. **Reconnect on a missed challenge.** Re-sending the join in the same
   session cannot help (everything queues behind the blocked ordering index),
   so after 3 s without `0x9B` the client drops the connection, fetches a
   fresh join script, and redoes the handshake (`resetSession()`), up to 3
   attempts. This is what turns a 75-80% per-attempt rate into a reliable join.

Measured with `./roblox --connection-type=raknet --place-id 1818`, 6 s windows,
six consecutive runs: **6/6 joined, spawned 2962-3574 instances** (one needed
the fresh-session reconnect). Earlier batches: 12/12.

### RbxTransport status (same day)

`./roblox --connection-type=rbxtransport` completes the QUIC handshake, sends
the join burst (`0x90/0x92/0x8A/0x8F`), receives the 1820-byte `0x93` settings
push and then **nothing** — no `0x9B` challenge, connection idle-closes at 40 s
(`chan1_rx=1820`, `challenge=no`). This is not the RakNet fragment-loss bug:
QUIC is reliable and the whole burst lands in 2 ms.

Tried and rejected: sending the RakNet `0x9B` join step after `0x8F` on QUIC.
The RCC answers that with `ERR_DRAINING` (peer close) within 10 s instead of
going idle, so the step is not what the QUIC path is missing (reverted).

Open lead: the QUIC `build90` only patches `RandomSeed1`, `APIsecurityToken`
and `__joinTicket` into a captured template, while the RakNet builder merges
the *entire* join script into the template document. That leaves stale
UserId/PlaceId/GameId/... in the QUIC `0x90` (and explains its ~300 B smaller
body), which fits the server accepting settings but not proceeding to the
challenge.

Also tried and rejected: mirroring that merge into the QUIC builder (template
value-kinds preserved, same rule as RakNet). It made the `0x90` slightly
*smaller* (10597-10605 B vs 10616 B) because this template's captured values
are longer than the live ones, and the server still never challenged - one
attempt got `ERR_DRAINING` instead of idle close. The QUIC `0x90` shape needs
to be compared against a native QUIC/WASM session before changing it further;
blindly merging fields is not the fix.

## RbxTransport: why it stopped working (2026-10-28)

The client did not regress — the native RbxTransport path was switched off.
The native Rum logs on this machine show the flip:

    # 2026-10-07 (mocktail_*.out), when the C++ QUIC client worked
    Transport selection: useRbxTransportEnabled=true,
      rbxTransportHasValidPort=true, rbxTransportHasValidAddress=true,
      rbxTransportHasValidPubKey=true, canUseRbxTransport=true,
      selectedTransport=RbxTransport, rccPort=55875

    # 2026-10-26 (rum_raknet*.log), same account, same places
    Transport selection: useRbxTransportEnabled=false,
      selectedTransport=RakNet, rccPort=49338

The native client still opens an `RbxTransportDummyClient` connection to the
RCC over QUIC (`Connected to server at 128.116.2.33:59143`) but gameplay
goes over RakNet. That is exactly what our QUIC path sees: TLS + RUPP
handshake OK, ctrl/dummy channels open, the 1820-byte `0x93` settings push
arrives, and then no `0x9B` challenge — the transport is alive but the join
is not enabled. Rechecked with both old accounts (whose `0x90` templates
match byte-for-byte), a same-account synthetic `0x90`, the web capability
word (`002dc3c750b30892`, `RBX_CAPS`), A7 real/empty/skip, and places 1818
and 189707: no challenge in any combination.

Context: all of these runs were through Cloudflare WARP (connected), which
this machine needs to reach Roblox at all. WARP is not the discriminator for
the join gate: the RCC's `0x93` (1820 B) arrives intact through it, and RakNet
joins 20/20 through the same tunnel. A WARP-off A/B was not completed because
Roblox is unreachable here without it. A smaller QUIC payload cap
(`RBX_MAX_UDP=1200`, under WARP's 1280-byte tunnel MTU) also did not change
the result — useful anyway, since the default path MTU of 1300 does not fit
WARP's tunnel.

The join messages themselves are not the problem: our `0x90`, `0x8A` and
early-auth are byte-identical to the Python builders from the working
2026-10-07/08 sessions (`RBX_DUMP_MSGS` vs `py/msgbuild`). What changed is
the server-facing path:

- native (WinInet/Android) joins no longer enable RbxTransport, and
- the only live RbxTransport is the **web** client's WebTransport to a
  per-session `*.qdmux.roblox.com` edge (`/wt?X-Rbx-Capabilities=…`), which
  is playtest-enrolled only (place 1818 answers status 12 "Playtesters
  Only"). Reproducing it would mean HTTP/3 CONNECT WebTransport, not raw
  QUIC+RUPP — a different transport, not a fix to this one.

So `--connection-type=rbxtransport` now stops after `0x93` by design of the
server, not because of a client bug. `--connection-type=auto` detects that
case within ~3 s of the settings push (instead of waiting out the 20 s probe)
and hands the same join to RakNet:

    probe: 0x93 received but no challenge in 3s — the server did not enable
           RbxTransport for this join
    transport: RbxTransport did not take this run (rc=3); falling back to RakNet

## RakNet soak (2026-10-28, after the challenge-deferral fix)

20 consecutive live joins, place 1818, 6 s windows, default binary:

    === SOAK2: ok=20 first_try=15 reconnect=5 deferred=20 fail=0 of 20 ===

Every run received its `0x9B` during the join sequence and answered it after
`0x8f`/the `0x9b` join step (the deferral), and every run spawned a world
(1,300-3,600 instances). 5 sessions needed the fresh-session reconnect, and
all 5 succeeded on the retry. The pre-fix soak was 19/20: the miss was a
challenge that arrived mid-join and was answered before `0x8f`, which shifted
the ordered indices; the server accepted the answer but sent the `0x97`
without its record batches, leaving the world empty.

## RakNet template lookup is CWD-independent (2026-10-28)

Symptom: launching `./roblox` from `build/client` printed `0x90 template
run/raknet90_template.bin missing — join body held back` on every attempt, so
no `0x90` was ever sent, the RCC answered only `0x93` and pads, and the `0x9B`
challenge never arrived (three consecutive sessions, both accounts). The same
binary joined normally from the repo root, which is why the soak never saw it:
the template paths were plain relative paths, so they only worked from the
directory the templates are relative to.

Fix: `rbx::resolveDataPath` in `client/src/common/util.h` probes
`$BOBLOX_DATA_DIR/<name>`, the CWD and its ancestors, then the executable's
directory and its ancestors. `join_flow.cpp` resolves the `0x90`, `0x8A` and
pre-join templates through it. A path that exists is used unchanged, and a
genuinely missing one is still reported by name, so the diagnostic is intact.

Verified from `build/client`, place 1818, two consecutive runs: `0x90` join
body sent (10,919 B, 11 datagrams), `0x9B` challenge answered in 1 ms, `0x97`
received, `world spawned=2057` and `2375` instances with live frames. No WARP
change.

## RakNet joins the shared renderer; upstream 0xA6 wired (2026-10-28)

**Renderer parity.** `raknetMain` now uses the same `collectBoxes` /
`runViewerLive` path as RbxTransport: headful is the default, `--headless` is
the no-GL path, `--shot PATH` saves one frame. A live 1818 run produced a
1280x720 frame (sky, spawn parts, `view: players=7`), and a headless run of
the same binary still applies the same world. The viewer is linked into the
`roblox` target for RakNet too (`rbx_view` was previously only linked when
`RBX_TRANSPORT=ON`).

**Why the first attempt starved.** `RaknetClient::pump(1)` never called
`recvfrom`: the deadline is computed in whole milliseconds, so `1 ms` truncated
to zero and the loop exited immediately. The viewer therefore rendered while
the socket stayed unread (`boxes=0` for 20 s, only 807 datagrams). `pump(0)`
is now a true non-blocking drain (recv until `EAGAIN`, `MSG_DONTWAIT`), and the
render tick uses it. The same run then reached `schema=yes`, `snap_batches=9`,
`spawn=389` and saved the frame.

**Upstream input.** Added `RaknetClient::sendUnreliable` (RakNet kind 0:
`[81][u24 dgram][00][u16 bits][data]`, sealed under the data AEAD), the shape
the native uses for `0xA6` (58 packets, 15–37 B, in the RakNet capture).
`raknet_main` sends the four post-answer route declarations (verbatim native
bytes, same constants as `quic/send.cpp` kRoutes) 60 ms apart, then a 1 Hz
`A6 00` heartbeat.

**Measured limit.** Sessions survive the input (15–20 s runs, no reset, no
ERR_DRAINING seen on this transport), but the RCC still does not spawn our
Player/Character: `class_stats` shows `InputBinding/ModuleScript/Model/...`
and no `Player`, and `linked character` only appears for a remote player
(`Fafadog3`). This matches the QUIC conclusion: route declarations are
keepalives, and a character needs real ControlState content (`nActions >= 1`,
sub_34786F8/3478E96), which still requires a driven native TX capture (hold W)
to map the movement slots. Guessing the block on a live account was not done.

## RbxTransport is server-gated, and the native client agrees (2026-10-29)

The premise "native joins over RbxTransport, so ours is broken" does not hold
for place 1818. With `FFlagUseRbxTransportClient=True` in the Rum client's
fflags, the native log still says:

```
Transport selection: useRbxTransportEnabled=false, selectedTransport=RakNet, rccPort=49719
```

and the fresh join script for that join has `gameFqdn = null`. Native then
completes the join over **RakNet**, not RbxTransport. Its `RbxTransportDummyClient`
is a separate ping/time-sync side connection, not the game session.

Our side matches that gate: the QUIC handshake to `rbx-rtcio` succeeds, then
the server sends no `0x93`/`0x9B` and `chan1_rx=0` until ERR_DRAINING. Three
re-joins could never change it. `play_main` now reads `joinScript.gameFqdn`
before configuring the session and fails fast when it is null:

```
RbxTransport: not enabled for this join (joinScript.gameFqdn is null; ...)
```

Auto selection therefore falls through to RakNet immediately instead of
burning 20 s (and three joins) on a gate that is closed. Places that do carry a
non-null `gameFqdn` are the only ones where RbxTransport can be exercised; the
two enrolled ids available here do not join this account (status 0 / status 12
"unrated"), so no live end-to-end RbxTransport run was possible.

Also restored: `~/Rum/build/data/config/fflags.json` was only backed up, never
modified; the RakNet framer capture attempt failed because the native game
session was RakNet-only and XTEST cannot inject into the Wayland surface.

## RakNet sending: 0x84 marker ack, plus two parser fixes (2026-10-29)

**Marker ack.** The RCC's `0x84` SendMarker is answered with `[04][u32 marker
LE]`; `DeserializedMarkerItem::process` gates join progress on it. RbxTransport
had this (`buildMarkerAck`/`tryMarker`), the RakNet sink never sent it. It now
acks every 5-byte `0x84` as a reliable-ordered message and logs
`0x84 marker N acked`. Verified live on places 189707 and 142823291 (markers
694 and 339 acked). Whether it is what finally spawns our own Player could not
be confirmed, because 1818 started withholding the `0x9B` challenge after the
burst of joins used for these experiments (three sessions in a row, both
accounts), while other places still answer.

**0x97 codec magic rolled again.** Places 189707 / 142823291 send
`97 c1 8d 00 00 [u32][zstd]`; only 1818's `ba d3` (and older pairs) were
accepted, so the whole world was dropped with `bad 0x97 magic c1 8d`. Added
`c1 8d` to the accepted set; both places now pass `join schema classes=638
members=10322`.

**0x84 can precede the snapshot.** On 142823291 the server sends the marker at
ordering 5, then all the `0x83/0x16` batches — so finalizing on the marker
built an empty world, and finalizing only on a live chain never ran because
every chain was a batch. `LiveRuntime::pump` now finalizes a non-empty
snapshot after 1 s without a new batch. Live: 189707 reached
`world spawned=225 grafted=142 instances=225 engine=on` (that world is
script-heavy: ModuleScript 114 / Sound 90 / LocalScript 14 / Script 7), and
142823291 accumulated 14 batches without stalling. A bounded `0x83 chain
sub=...` census identifies non-0x16 chains instead of dropping them silently.

## Native join stream, decoded (2026-10-29) — and the join regression

The 1818 gate theory is dead: the native client joined 1818 at 11:58 today
(`selectedTransport=RakNet`, `Replicator created for player 128.116.46.33|54858`,
`Join snapshot timer: 0.944546`) while our client failed 8 runs in a row on
1818, 189707 and 142823291. So the server is not withholding the join from
this account, our own stream is still wrong somewhere.

Capture: `tools/gdb/rc_capture.gdb` (`/tmp/rkn_live`) writes the **plaintext**
of every outgoing datagram (`DGRAM#` in `rkc.log`) plus the pre-fragmentation
buffers (`copy_src_*.bin`, `seal_plain_*.bin`). Decoded with the new
`tools/raknet_tx_table.py`, the native's join is exactly:

    dg0  0x09                 reliable, 24B          connection request
    dg1  0x13                 ord 0, 102B            address list
    dg2  0x00 pad             seq 0, 1160B (2 frags, split id 0)
    dg4  0x90 join body       ord 1, 10911B (11 frags, split id 1)
    dg16 0x8a ticket          ord 3, 1793B (2 frags, split id 2)
    dg18 0x00 ping            unreliable, 9B
    dg19 0x00 pad             seq 0, 1160B          (answer to the RCC's pad)
    dg22 0x03 pad             seq 0, 1160B
    dg24 0x03 pad             seq 0, 1160B
    dg26 0x9b                 ord 5, 9B              `9b 1b057f51 11f1c46e`

Facts that contradict what this file used to say:

* **Ordering indexes are 0, 1, 3, 5.** Index 2 and 4 are never used by the
  native. Our client put `0x92` at 2 and `0x8f` at 4, and its 0x9b at 5, so
  every message after the join body sat at an index the native never uses.
* **There is no `0x92` and no `0x8f` in the native RakNet join at all.** Those
  are RbxTransport (QUIC) join-burst messages that leaked into this path.
* The only `0x9b` the native sends is 9 bytes at index 5; it never sends a
  second one. Whether that is a pre-emptive join step or the answer to a
  challenge cannot be told from TX alone (the capture has no RX hook), but its
  shape `[9b][u32][u32]` is the same as the answer form we send.
* The native sends **one** 1160-byte `0x00` pad before the join body (and the
  `0x03` pads only after the ticket). The four-pad burst that used to block the
  challenge was our own invention; `RBX_PRE_PAD=1` now sends just the one.
* The `0x13` is 11 addresses (`[04][ip4][port BE]`: the client's own address,
  `128.255.254.254:0`, then nine `255.255.255.255:0`) **plus a 24-byte tail**
  (`000000000021a85300000000000007b400065ef00c0726b6b6` in one capture) = 102B.
  Ours is the older 14-address form (99B). Replaying the captured native
  payload (`RBX_RAKNET_13=/tmp/native13.bin`) raises the RCC's ACK count from
  3-5 to 7-11 per 7 s window, so the shape matters to the RCC even though the
  join still does not complete with it.

**Join body: 53 keys, not 47.** Parsing the native's own plaintext `0x90`
(`/tmp/native90.pt`) gives 53 keys, including exactly the six that a previous
change deleted (`AgeBracket`, `Gender`, `IsOriginalUser`,
`OriginalAccountCreationTimestampMs`, `Os`, `Platform`). That deletion was
wrong and is reverted; `build90` must not prune fields. With the 53-key body
ours is 10952B vs the native's 10911B for the same account and place (the
remainder is template values, not schema).

What is in the tree now: 53-key body, native ordering with indexes 2 and 4
reserved, no `0x92`/`0x8f`/pre-emptive `0x9b` by default (`RBX_JOIN_EXTRA=1`
restores them for A/B), one optional pre-join `0x00` pad, and the subtype log
now reports the subtype actually in use. `ctest -R "join_msg_raknet|
raknet_rx_stream|solver-regress"` passes.

Measured after all of it: 0/8 joins (1818 x5, 189707 x2, 142823291 x1),
`srv_acks` 3-5 (7-11 with the replayed 0x13), so the RCC stops reading our
reliable stream early in the join window. The next thing to do is not another
guess: add an RX hook to `rc_capture.gdb` (recvfrom, same plaintext dump the
TX hook already does) and diff the native's *incoming* messages against ours
around 0x93/0x9b/0x97 — the TX side is now known byte for byte, the RX side is
still invisible.

## RX capture: what the RCC sends the native (2026-10-29)

`tools/gdb/rc_capture.gdb` now hooks `recvfrom`/`recv` (created disabled, enabled
in `arm()` so the launcher's auth preflight is untouched) and writes every
received UDP frame to `wire_rx_*.bin` plus an `RX#` log line. The frames are
sealed, so `tools/raknet_rx_decode.py` derives the data key from the capture's
own `KDF#` lines (BLAKE2b-512 of X25519(sec, peer) || pub || peer, rx = the low
32 bytes), opens each frame with the AES-256-GCM datagram AEAD, and decodes the
RakNet framing into app messages. Verified end to end on a live 1818 join:
**1066 of 1119 captured frames open**, including the 53,854-byte `0x97`.

Native RX for a join that works (1818, 12:24):

    rx1   0x10  ord0  104 B      ACCEPTED
    rx2   0x00        9 B        ping
    rx4   0x00  seq   1146 B     pad
    rx7   0x53        9 B        ping
    rx10  0x93  ord1  1802 B     network settings
    rx14  0x03  seq   1146 B     pad
    rx16  0x54        25 B       ping
    rx31  0x9b  ord2  1958 B     the challenge
    rx94  0x97  ord3  53854 B    join data, 51 fragments, `97 ba d3 00 00`
    rx95  0x81  ord4  528 B
    rx95  0x84  ord5  5 B        marker
    rx99  0x83  ord6  4024 B     replication (4 fragments)
    rx121 0x83  ord8  23211 B    replication (22 fragments)

So the order is: settings, challenge, join data, input binding, marker,
replication — and the `0x97` arrives 63 datagrams after the challenge, not
"after answering" in the next packet.

Ours, decoded the same way from `RBX_RAKNET_DUMP` (d*.bin = decrypted
datagrams, r*.bin = raw reads; only d*.bin are datagrams):

    dg8   0x93  ord1  1802 B     ...
    dg13  0x93  ord1  1802 B     retransmission
    dg14  0x93  ord1  1802 B     retransmission
    ...    (about ten 0x93 retransmissions in a 7 s window)

i.e. the RCC keeps retransmitting the settings push at us, which the native
does only twice, and the join never reaches `0x9b`/`0x97`. When a challenge does
arrive (earlier runs), we answer it and the RCC replies with a **5-byte `0x15`
at ordering index 3** where the native gets its `0x97` — that is the server
rejecting our challenge answer, or at least not accepting it.

Two concrete leads, in order:

1. Decode that `0x15` payload (it is 5 bytes; capturing it needs one line in
   the message sink) — an error code there names the rejected step directly.
2. Our `0x93` retransmission storm means the RCC does not count our ACKs as
   covering its message. Our ACKs are single-id entries (`c0 0001 01 <id>`) for
   datagram ids 0,1,2,..., while the native sends *ranges*
   (`c0 0001 00 <lo> <hi>`, e.g. `01 00 00 03 00 00` = 1..3) that advance past
   the join. Matching that batching is the next cheap experiment.

Split header shapes (both confirmed on the wire this time):

* ordered (`0x70`): client writes u32be count | u16be id | u32be index (10 B);
  the RCC writes u8 count | u16be id | u32be index (7 B) for the same flags.
* sequenced (`0x50`): u8 count | u16be id | u32be index (7 B), both directions.
* `bits` in the internal-packet header *includes* the split header, so the
  readable payload is `bits/8 - header`.

## RX/TX diff of a live 1818 join (2026-10-29, recvfrom hook)

`tools/gdb/rc_capture.gdb` now records both directions: `wire_rx_*.bin` (the
`recvfrom`/`recv` hook, added under `arm()` so the launcher's preflight is
untouched) and `wire_tx_*.bin` (the pre-seal datagram, `dgram_*`). A live native
join captured 1119 RX frames, of which `tools/raknet_rx_decode.py` opens 1066.

Native TX (decoded by aliasing the `wire_tx_*` files as `wire_rx_*`: the
datagram AEAD is the same, the tx key is just the other KDF half):

    tx   0x13  ord0       addresses (24-byte tail)
    tx   0x90  ord1  10852B  join body, split 11
    tx   0x8a  ord3   1772B  ticket, split 2
    tx   0x9b  ord5      9B   the challenge answer (u2 echoed + answer)
    tx  0xa6/0xa1/0x83     continuous input + replication-ack stream

Native RX: `0x93` settings at rx10, `0x9b` challenge at rx31 (1958B), the
`0x97` join data at rx94 (53854B, split 51). Ours: the `0x93` arrives and is
then retransmitted ~10x into a 7s window, and no `0x9b` follows.

### RETRACTED: the 0x90 body is plaintext JSON after all

The earlier note in this file claimed the native's `0x90` carries an opaque
9709-byte region after a 1125-byte feature table. **That was wrong** — it came
from decoding the captured `0x90` with `tools/raknet_rx_decode.py` while
aliasing `wire_tx_*` files as rx frames. For TX the internal-packet `bits`
field excludes the 7-byte sequenced split header, so every split fragment was
reassembled 7 bytes short (11 fragments x 7 = the 77-byte deficit, and the
"3-byte trailer" that made the body look 16-byte aligned). Decrypting that
truncated ciphertext desynchronises the block chain and produces the
high-entropy tail that was mistaken for encryption.

The ground truth is a dump of the native's *pre-seal* plaintext, taken by
hooking `sub_664D9B0` (the seal primitive) and reading `rsi`/`rdx`
(`tools/gdb/doc_dump3.gdb`). That body is 10918 bytes and is exactly our own
shape:

    0..1491    feature table (same names, same order as our template)
    1492..     plaintext JSON, 53 keys, `{"UserId":3733467888,"VerifiedAMP":10,…`
    tail       24 bytes

So there is no encryption to reproduce: the native seals one body with
`KEY90`, and the document inside it is the same JSON we build. Comparing the
native body with ours field by field gives 53 keys on both sides, an identical
key set and identical prefix; the only differences are account/session values:

    Gender                  native Male          ours Female
    AgeBracket              native Age13To15     ours Age16To17
    CharacterAppearanceId   native 3733467888    ours 2477467842
    OriginalAccountCreationTimestampMs  native 1546638083217  ours 1566424029267
    APIsecurityToken / RandomSeed1 / __joinTicket   per-session, expected

The `CharacterAppearanceId` mismatch was a real bug and is fixed: the join
script has no `CharacterAppearanceId`, so the merge left the *template's* user
id in place — an appearance id belonging to a different account. `build90` now
pins it to the join script's `UserId` when the script does not carry its own.

`Gender`, `AgeBracket` and `OriginalAccountCreationTimestampMs` are also
inherited from the template and are wrong for this account; the join script
and its SessionId document carry neither (`Age` and
`AgeBandsBucketsBitmask` are there, not these three). They come from the
account profile, which is the remaining lead for the `0x15` rejection below.

### Challenge rate is not purely client-side

With the RbxTransport-style join burst (`0x92`, `0x8f`, 9-byte `0x9b`, which
`RBX_JOIN_EXTRA=1` used to enable) six alternating runs against place 1818 got
the `0x9b` challenge 6/6 while the native-shaped stream got it 0/6 in the same
window. Twenty minutes and ~30 joins later, *both* configurations get 0/6, so
that pairing was at least partly an RCC-side rate/cooldown effect, not proof
that the burst is required. When the challenge does arrive we answer it and the
RCC replies with a 5-byte `0x15`:

    raknet: 0x9B CHALLENGE u1=0x88b54049 u2=0x46404c63 blob=1959B
    raknet: 0x9B answer 0xdc89b43b (solved in 0ms) sent in 1 datagram
    raknet: app message 0x15 (5B, rel 0x60, ord 3) 1500000101

i.e. answer accepted as a message, join refused. The solver itself is not the
problem: `unit_vectors` still matches every recorded native answer (90/90), and
our live blob decodes, while the native's captured 1959-byte blob fails
`decode_blob` at every offset (its `len` field says 1959 but the message carries
1945), so the offline extraction — not the solver — is what disagrees there.

The default join burst is now on (`RBX_JOIN_EXTRA=0` restores the native-shaped
stream) because it is the only configuration that has ever reached `0x9b`;
after a cool-down an alternating A/B gave 2/3 with the burst and 0/3 without.

### Capture-decoder caveat

`tools/raknet_rx_decode.py` is reliable for `wire_rx_*` (server to client,
where `bits` includes the split header) and **lossy for `wire_tx_*`**: aliasing
TX frames through it drops 7 bytes per split fragment. Decode TX from the
`seal_*.bin` dumps (`tools/gdb/doc_dump3.gdb`) instead, or fix the framer
before trusting a TX reassembly.

## The identity fields come from the player hydration service

`sub_569AC78` reads exactly `ageBracket`, `gender`, `platform`, `os`,
`isOriginalUser` and `originalAccountCreationTimestampMs` out of a JSON object.
Its caller `sub_5699DB0` is the request for
`https://apis.roblox.com/player-hydration-service/v1/players/signed`, with
`sub_569DA1C` reading the `playerInfo` + `signature` reply. The call works with
the account cookie and `Accept: application/json` (without the Accept header
the service answers 415), and the query parameter is `userId`:

    GET .../players/signed?userId=<id>
    {"playerInfo":{"lastPerformed":"…","userId":"…","ageBracket":"Age13To15",
     "gender":"Male","platform":"Computer","os":"Unknown","isOriginalUser":false,
     "originalAccountCreationTimestampMs":"1546638083217"},"signature":"…"}

Those values match the native's 0x90 body exactly. `rbx::fetchPlayerHydration`
(client/src/join/join.cpp) now fetches the same payload and merges it into the
join script under the document's key names, so `build90` stops inheriting the
template's values. After that the 0x90 body differs from the native's only in
`RandomSeed1`, `APIsecurityToken` and `__joinTicket` — the three values that
*must* differ per session.

## The join order was the actual 0x15 gate

With identity fixed the RCC still answered a solved challenge with
`0x15 00 00 01 01`. The remaining difference was the ordered stream: the
client's pre-emptive 9-byte `0x9b` "join step" occupied ordering index 5, which
is exactly where the native puts its *challenge answer* (`0x13@0, 0x90@1,
0x8a@3, answer@5`), so our answer went out at index 6. Making that step opt-in
(`RBX_JOIN_STEP_9B=1`) puts the answer back at 5.

Result, live against place 1818: **8/8 joins reach `0x97`**, no `0x15`, and the
world builds (`schema=yes`, 168-551 spawns, 328 entities, 40+ events fired per
run — `live: live_frames=15..21`).

## RakNet join: the 0x13 shape was gating the RCC's ACK stream (2026-10-30)

A fresh two-way native capture (`tools/gdb/rc_capture.sh`, 1818, 70 s,
`/tmp/nat_join`: 1200 RX frames opened, 104 TX datagrams) settled the join
sequence and produced three fixes.

**1. `0x13` (ID_NEW_INCOMING_CONNECTION) is 102 bytes, not 99.** The native
sends 11 addresses (`[04][ip4][port BE]`: its own socket, `128.255.254.254:0`,
then nine `255.255.255.255:0`) plus a 24-byte tail; our builder wrote 14
addresses and no tail. The RCC acknowledged our `0x09` and then **stopped
ACKing the very next datagram** (1 ACK per run, no challenge, no `0x97`). With
the native shape the ACK stream resumes (up to 52 ACKs in a later run), and
joins reached `0x97` again: 3643, 2018 and 337 instances across three runs.

**2. ACK bookkeeping.** The ACK value is the *datagram id* (the native client's
own ACK is `c0 0001 01 <u24>`), and a retransmitted datagram must be ACKed
again — the lost packet may have been our ACK. The previous default ACKed a
message number instead, and dropped duplicates without re-ACKing, which left
the RCC's reliable window jammed (a 45 s run sent 1468 datagrams; the server
re-sent the same 1093-byte fragments under fresh datagram ids).

**3. Root/services regression (the avatar blocker).** The join snapshot's
top-level services do not always carry the id-13 DataModel sentinel: on
189707/1818 all service records referenced ids that are never records (the
native client creates Players/Workspace/… locally). The 2026-10-29
"defer instead of graft" change therefore left **every** record detached —
`root->children()` was empty, `players-service=missing` even though
`Player:1 PlayerGui:1` had spawned, so `PlayerAdded` never fired and no
avatar could link. `applyJoinSnapshot` now (a) treats parent 13 as the
DataModel root and (b) flushes any still-deferred records to the root at
snapshot finalize, keeping the queue so a parent that later streams in still
re-parents its child (`flushDeferredToRoot`). `unit-engine` pins the contract
(`run/join97_1818.raw` → Workspace/Players/Lighting/… children of root).

**Post-join client stream, captured.** After the world, the native sends
`0x83/0x05` at ~1 Hz in two forms (50 B reliable-ordered + 45 B unreliable),
a 14-byte `0x83/0x07` opener, `0x83/0x06` records, and a dense `0x83/0x23`
clock stream. Our client only sent the ticks. It now replays the 0x05 ack
pair and the native's 0x07 opener (bytes verbatim from the capture) after
the world applies.

**Open:** after the snapshot the RCC goes silent to us even with that stream,
so the character spawn is still not observed. Joins are now rate-limited
(three consecutive runs stopped before `0x97`), so the root fix has not yet
been confirmed against a live world; the next run with a `0x97` should show
`grafted>0`, a `Players` service, and `player joined:`/`player character:`
lines.

### Native-vs-ours RakNet TX diff (2026-10-30, same job id)

Captured both sides with the plaintext hooks (`RC_HOOKS=wire,rx,dgram,kdf,seal`
for the native, `RBX_RAKNET_TXDUMP` for ours), decoded with
`tools/raknet_join_diff.py` (which now also accepts NAK datagrams). At one job
the ordered streams are:

```
native  /tmp/nat_diff2   13@0 | 90@1 | 92@2 | 8a@3 | 8f@4 | 9b@5(9B) | 83/04@6
ours                     13@0 | 90@1 | 92@2 | 8a@3 | 8f@4 | 83/04@5 | 9b@6
```

**The 9-byte `0x9b` at slot 5 is the challenge answer** (`9b` + `u2` + answer,
i.e. `buildResponse`), not a separate join step. Ours sat at 6 because the
318 B `0x83/0x04` hydration push was sent before the challenge arrived. The
join path now holds its `join_flow_open` window open for the `0x9B` (bounded
1 s), answers it at the native's slot, and only then pushes hydration.

**`0xA7` is part of the native's RakNet join.** An earlier capture missed it;
the full TX diff shows `a7 02 00 <2 x 16B>` at ordering slot 1, before `0x90`
(`/tmp/nat_now`, 35 B). Our client never sent one (`--a7` was opt-in) so every
later slot was shifted by one. The captured queue is now the default;
`--a7 FILE` overrides and `--a7 skip` omits it.

**`0x13` (102 B) field map**, from two native sessions:

```
0  13                        id
1  04                        address tag
2  7f 8b ca de               advertised address: 127.139.202.222 (constant),
6  <port u16 BE>             port is RANDOM per session (d2a2, f351, …)
8  04 80 ff fe fe 00 00      128.255.254.254:0
15 .. 77                     9 x 04 ff ff ff ff 00 00
78 00 00 00 00 00            five zero bytes
83 <A: 3B random>            6d025a / 0253ca across captures
86 00 x 6
92 <B: 2B random>            08b2 / 0cb8
94 00 06 5f                  constant
97 <C: 5B random>            13ef732fa4 / 14da4bf1f7
```

Ours wrote the real socket address (0.0.0.0) and a hardcoded tail from one old
capture; both are now rolled per session like the native.

**Pre-join probe**: native writes a 1160 B `0x00` (1061+99 split, u16 counter at
[7:9]) followed by `0x53`, `0x52`, `0x51` (9 B each, same counter). Ours was
1146 B with a single `0x51`.

**Still open:** after these changes the RCC accepts the transport, answers
`0x93`, ACKs our first datagrams (0, 1, 5) and then stops reading — no `0x9B`,
no `0x97`, on 1818 and 189707, for both accounts. The failure is job-sticky
(1818 now always resolves to job `19b2444b…` / `128.116.53.33:52108`), and the
native still challenges, so a body/stream difference remains in the join burst.

## 2026-10-30 late — the native's actual RakNet join ladder (decrypted, /tmp/nat_cmp)

Decrypting both directions of a live native 1818 session (`tools/raknet_native_rx.py`
on the `rc_capture.gdb` wire dumps) gives the reference ladder, and it is
*shorter* than what this client was sending:

```
msg  dgram  frame                                     notes
m0   dg0    0x09 CONNECTION_REQUEST     k2 24 B      subtype 1, join token
m1   dg1    0x13 NEW_INCOMING_CONNECTION k3 ord 0    subtype 2 from here on
m2-3 dg2-3  0x00 pad 1160 B   split(2, id0, 0..1)    k2, client-initiated
      —     0x53/0x52/0x51 9 B each                   same 24-bit clock
m4-5 dg7-8  0x00 pad 1160 B   split(2, id1, 0..1)
m6-16 dg12+ 0x90 JOIN          11 fragments, split id 2, **ordered index 1**
m17         0x92                                        ordered index 2
m18-19      0x8A ClientTicket  split id 3                ordered index 3
m20         0x8F (2 B)                                  ordered index 4
m25         0x9B (9 B)                                  ordered index 5
            0x83/0x04 hydration push, then 0xA6 inputs at ~13 Hz
```

Three things this settles:

1. **The native sends no `0xA7` before the join.** Our pre-join script-hash
   queue occupied ordered slot 1, which pushed 0x90 to slot 2. With that slot
   misaligned the RCC answers the join with nothing at all; with the queue
   removed (now the default, `--a7 FILE` restores it) 0x90 sits at slot 1 and
   the RCC answers with its `0x93` network-settings push every run.
2. **Pads and pings are generated, not echoed.** The native's reply pad carries
   its own 24-bit clock at `[6:9]`, its own `u32` clock at `[14:18]` and its own
   random tail; its 9-byte pings carry its own clock. Echoing the RCC's own
   bytes back (the previous behaviour) leaves the RCC probing with 1160-byte
   pads forever: 243 pads in 20 s, measured.
3. **The join document is 47 keys.** The six `AgeBracket`/`Gender`/`Os`/
   `Platform`/`IsOriginalUser`/`OriginalAccountCreationTimestampMs` fields we
   added belong to the separate `0x83/0x04` push; they are pruned by default
   now (`RBX_HYDRATION_FIELDS=1` restores them). Our 0x90 body is 10725 B
   against the native's 10717 B, the difference being per-session values.

**Where it still stops.** With the ladder matching, the RCC sends `0x93` at
ordered index 1 and then only MTU pads — never the `0x9B` challenge, on 1818 and
189707, for both accounts. That is not the join body: replaying *the native's
own* 0x90 (`RBX_JOIN90_RAW`) and its own 0x8A ticket (`RBX_JOIN8A_RAW`) in our
session changes nothing.

**The one transport fact left.** The native's 0x09 is its last subtype-1
datagram; its 0x13 is the first under RUPP subtype 2, carrying the token the
server hands it in **Reply2 TLV1** (`wire_rx_26`:
`01 00 00 17 01 11 02 f13e6ad6118faf14ecd1a99453b2cf6b`), and every later
datagram reuses that token. We now take the token from exactly there, but every
subtype-2 datagram we send is dropped — with the server-issued token, with a
locally generated one, whether the switch happens on the 0x13 or earlier, with
ACKs kept on subtype 1 or not. The session stops after 2–5 datagrams. Until that
is understood the client stays on subtype 1 (`--token-sub 2` forces the switch),
which the RCC still answers with 0x93.

## Solved (2026-10-30): RUPP subtype-2 datagrams were routed to a dead port

The drop was never the token, the subtype ordering, or the 0x13 body. It is the
**TLV2 port, and it has to change with the subtype**.

Method: one live job (`ad2bb1b4-…`, place 1818, `king.txt`), the native captured
on it under the gdb harness, then our client on the *same* job and account with
`RBX_RAKNET_WIRE` / `RBX_RAKNET_TXDUMP`, and both directions decrypted with the
captured KDF keys. The sendto hook was extended to record the destination
sockaddr, which rules out a "rehome to the RCC" step: every native datagram,
subtype 2 included, goes to the same Udmux peer.

Byte-for-byte, the first subtype-2 datagram (0x13) matched except for the 12-byte
shorter body (since fixed: 114 B, native's 36-byte tail) and one header field:

    native  TLV2 = 10.41.0.95:58328   (= ServerConnections[0].Port, the dial port)
    ours    TLV2 = 10.41.0.95:57050   (= NetStackPort from the join script)

The native's two values coincide for *its* script, which is why the byte diff
looked harmless. For ours they differ, and the RCC routes the established
session's datagrams by TLV2:

* `subtype 1` (Req1/Req2/0x09) must carry **NetStackPort**. Advertising the RCC
  port there makes the edge ignore Req1 outright (no Reply1, reproduced on a
  live job seconds apart from a working run).
* `subtype 2` (everything after the 0x09) must carry **ServerConnections[0].Port**.
  With NetStackPort there the RCC ACKs our 0x09 and then drops every datagram
  silently — exactly the "dies in 2–5 datagrams" symptom. Replaying the native's
  own 114-byte 0x13 body changed nothing until this was fixed, which is what
  isolates it from the payload.

Proof: `RBX_TLV2_SUB2=1` alone flipped the session from dead to the RCC ACKing
dgram 0, 1 (our 0x13), 2, 4 and 9, pushing 0x93, issuing 0x9B and streaming 0x97.
Now the default in `sendWrapped()` (with `RBX_TLV2_SUB2=0` to A/B it), and the
0x13 switches the session to subtype 2 by default (`--token-sub 1` pins subtype 1).

Default runs, no flags, 3/3: `0x93` → `0x9B CHALLENGE` solved in 0 ms and
answered → `0x97` (54 211 B) → `live: schema=yes`, spawn 166–3 283, 35–45 events
fired. The unit suite is unchanged (16/17; `unit-engine` `stream_grafted` fails
the same way it did before this work).

### Soak result (20 joins, place 1818, `king.txt`, 12 s each, `--headless`)

| stage | rate |
| --- | --- |
| Reply2 verified | 20/20 |
| 0x93 network settings | 20/20 |
| 0x9B challenge received | 20/20 |
| 0x9B answered | 20/20 |
| 0x97 replication | 20/20 |
| live world applied (`schema=yes`) | 20/20 |

`live_frames` 12–27 (median 17) per 12 s run, 89–104 app messages, 643–900 data
datagrams received, challenge blob 1948 B (one 1944 B). No auto-rejoin, no frame
that failed to open, no dropped datagram, no join-script failure in any run.

Like-for-like control on the same build, same place, `RBX_TLV2_SUB2=0`
(subtype 2 advertising NetStackPort — the old behaviour): **0/5** challenges,
`srv_acks=0` in every run. That is the TLV2 port alone flipping 0/5 to 20/20.

## The placed session is kicked: RakNet 0x15 = ID_DISCONNECTION_NOTIFICATION (2026-10-30)

The friend "leaving" in a two-client test is not a presence bug. Our client is
disconnected by the RCC ~10-12 s after the join snapshot applies:

    raknet: app message 0x15 (5B, rel 0x60, ord 63) 1500000104
    raknet: NO SERVER DATAGRAM for 6s ...

`0x15` is RakNet's `ID_DISCONNECTION_NOTIFICATION` (`sub_446D5C0` labels ids 21
and 22 "disconnect" vs 0x90 "join"). The 4-byte body is `[u16 0][u16 BE code]`,
so `0x0104` = **260 DisconnectReceivePacketError** and `0x0105` = 261
DisconnectReceivePacketStreamError (`sub_33E7C3F` name table, `sub_64FE56E`
friendly-text table). Same code for king and mizg, same on 1818 and 189707:
the RCC says the *server* failed to receive/parse our traffic, and it never
reconnects. A healthy native session receives no 0x15 at all (34 s capture).

Bisection (env knobs added to the input pump / message sink):
`RBX_INPUT_MS=100000`, `RBX_NO_A6`, `RBX_NO_A1`, `RBX_NO_HB`,
`RBX_NO_CLOCK`, `RBX_NO_ACK83`, `RBX_NO_MIRROR`, `RBX_NO_HYDRATION`. With
**every** post-join stream silenced the kick still comes (code flips to 0x0105),
so the offending bytes are in the join ladder or our ACK/retransmit path, not
the steady-state stream. The server also stops ACKing our datagrams a few
seconds before it sends 0x15 (last acked id 321 of 849 in one 24 s run).

Two real defects found and fixed on the way:

* **The 0x83/0x07 opener stole ordering slot 5.** It was sent right after the
  join ladder, so our 0x9B answer landed at 6 and the attributes push at 7,
  where the native is `9b@5, 83/04@6`. That is the same shift the earlier
  "0x15 00 00 01 01" join gate punished. The opener now goes out from the input
  pump after the answer/attributes; a TX dump confirms `9b@5, 83/04@6`.
* **0xA6 counts 2 and 3 carried the count-4 tag.** Native: only the count-4
  steady-state frame has the 11-byte `10 03 00 64 d0 b7 e6 d7 ff 03 e0` tag;
  counts 1-3 are `a6 <c> 01 00 01 <step8> 00 00 (04 04 00)*(c-1)`. Ours were
  10 bytes long each.

Still open: which join-phase message the RCC chokes on. The RUPP subtype-2
token rotates in both directions mid-session (server header token changes at
rx frames 417/437/456/494; the native's TX token changes 9 times in 34 s) while
our client stamps one token at init — a candidate for "receive packet error".

## Same-job byte diff of the join ladder (2026-10-30)

Method: native joined place 1818 pinned to `jobId 19b2444b-31b9-4440-8dd2-03091fc97e78`
via `RKC_PLACE="1818&gameInstanceId=<job>"`, captured under gdb
(`RC_HOOKS=kdf,seal,dgram,wire`, 58 s, /tmp/nat_same); 16 s later our client joined the
same job id with `--job-id` and dumped TX/RX to /tmp/our_same. Our client's log shows
`player joined: ChatGGPT:4656429295`, i.e. both sessions were in one server. The native
kept sending for the whole window; ours got `0x15 00000105` at ~10 s.

Both dumps are plaintext RakNet datagrams (`dgram_*.bin` native, `t*.bin` ours) and were
reassembled with `tools/raknet_join_diff.py`.

### Differences found and fixed

1. **0x90 missing six keys.** Ours: 47 keys, 9242 B JSON. Native: 53 keys, 9361 B.
   Missing exactly `AgeBracket Gender IsOriginalUser OriginalAccountCreationTimestampMs
   Os Platform` — the block the hydration service returns. `build90` pruned them by
   default behind a stale "native has 47 keys" note. Now kept by default
   (`RBX_HYDRATION_FIELDS=0` prunes). Re-verified live: 53/53 identical key set.
2. **0x13 was 114 B, native 102 B.** The tail was a 36-byte extension from an older
   capture; the same-job native body ends with a 24-byte tail
   (`00*5|A3|00*6|B2|00 06 5f|C3|D2`). Now 24 B by default (`RBX_13_EXT=1` restores 36).
3. **0x83/04 carried a 318-byte account JSON.** The native's 0x83/04 at that slot is a
   55-byte binary replication record, and its account JSON appears nowhere in the
   RakNet stream. Push now off by default (`RBX_ATTRS_8304=1` restores).
4. **Every reliable message was retransmitted 5 times regardless of ACKs.** The server
   ACK branch only logged/counted; `rtx` was never pruned. Native sends 0x8f twice at
   most; we sent 0x13/0x92/0x8f/0x9b six times each and 286 clock ticks where the
   native sends 17. ACK ids now cancel matching pending entries, retry cap 5 -> 2, and
   the 0x83/23 clock cadence was cut from 170 ms to 3 s (native: ~3.5 s).

### Differences still open

- Ours never sends the native's pre-join `83 21 03 00 00 00 10 00 00 00 39 30 00 00 00
  00 00 00 66 00 00 00 20 00` (27 B, reliably ordered, before 0x90).
- 0x92 value magnitude: native's zigzag LEB is always 8 bytes (top of range ~2^55);
  ours is 9-10 depending on the draw (~2^63). Same shape, different range.
- The disconnect is still `0x0105` ~10 s in, after the challenge is answered and the
  first snapshot applies (spawn counts in the thousands). It is not in the join ladder:
  with 0x13, 0x90, 0x83/04 and the retransmit amplification all matched to native, the
  RCC still stops ACKing and drops us, so the remaining cause is in the post-join
  stream (our input/clock/replication frames), not the ladder.

### Post-join 0x83 chain diff: 0x1c and 0x0e

`0x83` is a *container*: after the id byte the body is a run of typed items (0x23 clock,
0x02 spawn, 0x07 event, 0x0a, 0x0e, 0x1c, ...), and `body[1]` is simply the first item's
type. Native TX in the same job, first ~30 chains:

```
83/23 24B  -> items 23(0x78) | 07(13B) | 23(0x88)
83/07 76B  -> 07 | 07 | 07 | 23
83/1c 563B -> 1c 00 <u16 0x07d0> <u16 0x7fff> ... item stream
83/1c 693B, 287B, 1329B, 61B, 10B
83/0e 21B  -> 0e 00 | 07(12B) | 23(0x130)
83/0e 744B, 49B, 49B
83/0a 365B, 21B, 47B, 64B
```

Ours in the same window: **no 0x1c and no 0x0e at all**; only 7-byte `83/23` clock
ticks, the captured `83/07` opener (19 B) and the captured `83/05`/`83/06` pair, all of
which carry instance ids from the session they were recorded in (0x0512b6, 0x10dc16,
0x47d0bb) while this job assigns parts in the 0xc5____ range. Those three canned frames
