are now off by default (`RBX_CANNED_83=1` restores); the native's equivalents always
reference live ids.

The kick is unchanged: `0x15 00000105` ~10 s after the snapshot, no matter whether the
canned frames are on or off. So 0x1c/0x0e are the remaining structural gap, but they are
not something we can synthesise: their ids are session part ids and the item layout
after `1c 00` is not yet decoded (the server->client walker in repl_stream.cpp has no
0x1c/0x0e case). Next step is IDA on the client-side writer for those two item types.

### IDA: looking for the 0x1c / 0x0e writers (negative result, 2026-10-30)

Goal: find the client-side serializer for chain items `0x1c` and `0x0e` so they can be
emitted with live instance ids.

What was ruled out in libroblox.so (base 0, .text 0x1e45040-0x6d84d88, 314486 funcs):

- `mov byte ptr [reg], 1Ch` / `..0Eh`: 100 functions hit, but every pair hit is an enum
  registration table (KeyCode at 0x1e83632, Font at 0x1e8010c, Material at 0x1e9ff46,
  ConnectionError at 0x1f935e4, FacsActionUnit at 0x1fc8222). Only 0x1e83632 contains
  both immediates, and it is the 21 KB KeyCode table — a false positive.
- `mov word ptr [reg], 1C83h` / `0E83h` / `2383h`: zero hits, so the header and the
  first item tag are not written as a single immediate store.
- Items are therefore appended through a helper with the tag in a register, which byte
  signatures cannot distinguish from the ~200 unrelated `mov reg, 1Ch` sites.
- The "many type compares" scan surfaced a property-type compatibility table
  (sub_6485E15) and the property-tag switch (sub_340FCCC -> sub_340A326), not a chain
  writer.

Conclusion: static signature hunting does not isolate this writer; it needs a runtime
anchor. The cheap one is a gdb breakpoint on the native's send path (rc_capture.gdb
already hooks it) conditional on the plaintext containing `83 1c` / `83 0e`, then a
backtrace to the enclosing writer, then decompile at that address. No emission code was
written, because guessing the item layout would put fabricated bytes on the wire.

### Correction: 0x83/1c and 0x83/0e are *upstream replication batches* (2026-10-31)

The 2026-10-30 entry ("Post-join 0x83 chain diff") treated 0x1c/0x0e as chain item types
with a per-type writer and as the remaining structural gap behind the 0x15/0x0105 kick.
Both readings are wrong. What the runtime anchors show:

**They are outgoing.** `sub_449C354` — the function the `DGRAM#` hook sits on — stamps
`"UniqueNumbeR"` plus a monotonic counter into the trailing 18 bytes (`buf+len-18`) and
then calls the cipher. That is a seal footer, so every `dgram_*.bin` is a client->server
datagram, not a decrypted download.

**Framing.** 12 frames in one same-job run: `83 1c 00 <i32> 06 00 <items...> 00` (10 of
12; the other two start `83 1c 00 <i32> ff 7f 06 01 ...`). `0x0e` has the same shape
without the i32 (`83 0e 00 <items...> 00`). `06 00` precedes the payload items, `00` is the
chain end marker the reader already handles.

**Bodies are ordinary chain items, carrying client-authored content.** `0x07` events with
length-prefixed string arguments (`02 <len> <bytes>`), `0x0a` id-pair items, `0x23` clock,
`0x05` timing. The 919 B body contains `PlayerGui.ClassicLoadingScreen.Overlay.Counter`,
`en-us`, and the F9 `Parts: N Connectors: M` label text; the ids in these bodies
(~0x3500, ~0x6500, ~0x2e66c) sit far below the 0xc5____ part ids the earlier same-job
capture showed for replicated world instances.

So these are the client replicating **instances it owns** (PlayerGui children,
LocalizationTable) upstream — not acks of server parts. A headless client that creates no
client-side instances has nothing to put in them; their absence is by design and is not
what the RCC kicks us for.

**Writer path** (libroblox.so, base 0):
- `sub_4486F34(bitstream, blob, bits)` — bulk byte append; `memcpy` from `blob->vtable[2]()`.
- `sub_4491E40(records, bitstream, avail)` — record-array codec (`{u32 lo,u32 hi}` -> 3 or
  6 bytes per entry + 16-bit count), sole caller of the bulk append.
- `sub_4492EA4` (11 364 B) — the framer that copies a finished chain blob at `+4495688`;
  sole caller `sub_447CC0A`.

The item tag never goes through the bit writer (`h_bits`), which is why the TAG hook finds
nothing: the body is assembled byte-wise in a blob and copied in wholesale. That also
rules out the earlier plan of isolating a per-type serializer by signature.

**Runtime anchors** added to `tools/gdb/rc_capture.gdb` (`RC_HOOKS=dgram`):
`CHAIN#` logs the plaintext window + backtrace when a `83 1c`/`83 0e` frame is sealed;
`TAG#` logs 8-bit writes made immediately after a `0x83` header; `CHAINWP` (opt-in via
`RKC_CHAINWP=1`) watches byte 1 of a chain buffer.

**Consequence for emission:** a legitimate `83/1c` needs a client-side instance registry
with its own ids plus an upstream replication queue. Emitting one from this headless client
would mean fabricating instance content and ids, so no emission code was added.

### Fixed: the ~1 s post-join disconnect was our own 0x83/05 reply (2026-10-31)

Symptom: the session joined, then the RCC answered with a 5-byte `0x15 00 00 01 05`
(receive error) about a second later and never sent another datagram.

Cause: `client/apps/raknet_main.cpp` treated the server's `0x83/05` data-channel
heartbeat as a ping and answered it by flipping the subtype to `0x06`. The native's own
`0x83/06` frames are instance-ack record arrays — `83 06 01 <u32 id> 00 00 00 00 <u32 id>
...` over the server's `0xc5____` ids (same-job capture /tmp/nat_same) — never a
transformed heartbeat, so the RCC's ack parser was handed a heartbeat body.

Both reply shapes were measured against a live 1818 join:

| reply | result |
|---|---|
| `0x06` flip (old default) | `0x15 00000105` ~1 s after join, stream dead (/tmp/our6.log) |
| `0x05` in-kind mirror | `0x15 00000105` immediately after the mirror (/tmp/join7.log) |
| none (new default) | no `0x15`, stream alive for the whole window (/tmp/join8.log, /tmp/join9.log) |

New default: no reply. `RBX_PING05=mirror` / `RBX_PING05=ack06` restore the old shapes
for A/B runs.

Verified run, no knobs: `build/client/roblox --connection-type=RakNet --place-id 1818
--headless --seconds 70` -> 0 x `0x15`, 3409 live frames, 7055 spawns, 1124 events,
5001 datagrams, 3571 app messages (/tmp/join9.log).

### Why 189707 still kicked after the heartbeat fix: the 0xA1 camera frame (2026-10-31)

`0x15 00 00 01 04` is **260 DisconnectReceivePacketError** (same name table as the
0x0105 entry above): the RCC could not parse a datagram *we* sent. It is not a
missing-avatar kick — kicked runs had replicated 13,849 instances and 20 players'
characters, and one kicked with only 6 spawns.

It is place-dependent but not deterministic by place: 189707 kicked 7/12 runs,
1818 kicked 0/15 across the same build. The packet is the post-join `0xA1`
camera frame: its 39-byte body is a verbatim 1818 capture and embeds the
instance id `0x0512b6` at body offset 13. In a job that never replicated that
id, the frame is a dangling reference and the RCC answers with 0x0104 within
~1 s.

Measurements on 189707 (12 s runs):

| variant | kicks |
|---|---|
| as shipped | 7/12 |
| `RBX_HYDRATION_FIELDS=0` | 5/5 kicked (not hydration) |
| `RBX_NO_A6=1` | 1/1 kicked (not the route frames) |
| `RBX_NO_A1=1` | 0/5 |

Two "surviving" arms of the first bisection (`RBX_NO_HB`, `RBX_NO_ACK83`) were
no-ops with the canned frames off, which is how the nondeterminism showed up:
re-run the same arm and it kicks.

Fix: `LiveRuntime::hasInstance(id)` (consults the snapshot's `by_id`) gates the
0xA1 send; when the id is absent the frame is skipped once with a log note.
Verified: 189707 5/5 and a 70 s run with no 0x15; 1818 2/2 still joined.
The gate is conservative — `by_id` holds snapshot ids, so an id spawned later
by a delta also suppresses the frame — but it can never send a dangling id.

### 0xA1 camera builder and live-reference rejection (2026-11-04)

The 44-byte camera frame is now built by `buildA1Camera(clock, ref)`: it
preserves the captured 39-byte body and overwrites the u24 reference at body
offset 13, with a unit test for the clock/ref bytes and rejection above u24.
The sender still uses the capture id only when `hasInstance(0x0512b6)` is true.

Three live substitutions were tried on 1818 with the accepted
`--app-probe=96,99` handshake: the local character model id, the
HumanoidRootPart id, and the local Player instance id (`0x271233` in that
job). All were rejected by the RCC with `0x15 01 04`
(DisconnectReceivePacketError) within about a second; the Player probe sent
its first camera frame only after the avatar linked, then hit the disconnect,
repeating on three reconnects. A control run with `RBX_NO_A1=1` stayed joined
for the full 20 s, so the camera body's reference semantics are still
unresolved; the safe gate was restored. A gated run: A1 806/9,251 records,
`stops=0 walk_fail=0`, no 0x15; a later run also stayed joined and hit the
known `0xb9` chain stop.

## 2026-11-01: ChatGGPT (UserId > 2^32) join fix

Root cause: `build8a()` and `join_flow.cpp` treated the joining user's `UserId`
as `uint32_t`. Roblox UserIds can exceed 2^32 (ChatGGPT = 4656429295). The
truncation (to 361461999) corrupted the 0x8a ticket's first LEB field (`zz =
user_id*2`), so the RCC answered the join with `0x95` SecurityKeyRejected +
`0x15 00000102` DisconnectSecurityKeyMismatch. Accounts whose UserId fits in
32 bits (e.g. kingdudely_alt = 3733467888) were unaffected, which is why the
failure was account-specific.

Fix: widened `user_id`/`uid` to `uint64_t` in `build8a`, `parse8aPayload`, and
`join_flow.cpp`. Verified: ChatGGPT now receives 0x97 (54211B), schema=yes,
spawn=513, no 0x95 app message.

Also fixed the final-summary printf argument bug in `raknet_main.cpp`: the
`rtx=%llu` format had no corresponding argument (read garbage); now passes
`client.retransmits_tx()`.

## 2026-11-01: the 30-60 s "random" disconnect is DisconnectRejoin (0x0114)

The mid-session drop that ended live runs reproduces as

    raknet: app message 0x15 (5B, rel 0x60, ord 767) 1500000114

and the client's own name table (`sub_33E7C3F`, libroblox.so) names that code:

    case 274: "DisconnectDevMaintenance"
    case 275: "DisconnectRobloxMaintenance"
    case 276: "DisconnectRejoin"
    case 277: "DisconnectConnectionLost"

So the RCC is **asking the client to reconnect** (job rotate / maintenance /
load balance), not reporting malformed traffic. The native client just rejoins;
our client treated the 0x15 as the end of the run, which is exactly the
"stays a minute, then disconnects out of nowhere" symptom. Codes 0x0104/0x0105
(260/261) are the receive-error family the earlier work chased; 0x0114 is not
a parse failure and cannot be fixed by changing what we send.

Fix: the run loop now treats 0x15 (any code) and 12 s of server silence as a
dead session and repeats the whole handshake — fresh `joinGame` (new ticket,
new job id), fresh RakNet session, fresh LiveRuntime — up to 10 times, 1 s
apart. A verified live run (place 1818, 65 s) got 0x0114 ~25 s in, rejoined,
built a second world (`spawn=2592 entries=4095`) and kept running.

Also added NAK generation on the receive side (0xa0, same record layout as our
ACKs, deltas <= 4 ids, max 2 rounds, `RBX_NAK=0` to disable): the ordered
stream previously had no way to request a lost datagram, so one dropped packet
froze replication until the RCC reaped the session.

## 2026-11-01: inbound packet census (what "all packets" means, measured)

`LiveRuntime::stats()` now carries a full inbound census, so coverage is a
number per session instead of an impression:

    ids=00:28,03:10,10:1,51:14,52:14,53:14,54:10,55:10,56:10,81:1,83:3076,
        84:1,93:1,97:1
    sub=01:362,02:293,03:15,05:212,07:268,10:2,16:36,1b:162,23:1726
    stop=b7:1  walk_fail=0  unk_id=0  unk_flag=0

* App-level ids the parser owns: 0x81 peer init, 0x83 chains, 0x84 snapshot
  marker, 0x8D region chunk, 0x97 JoinData. All decoded; `walk_fail=0`.
* Transport-level ids are now counted apart (`proto=`): 0x00/0x03 keepalive
  ping + echo, 0x10 handshake accept, 0x51..0x56 clock sync, 0x93 network
  settings, 0x9B challenge. These are answered by raknet.cpp, not this parser;
  classifying them removed the false "unhandled app id" lines seen for every
  ping.
* 0x83/16 batch kind byte is **not** a closed set: live sessions send 0x02,
  0x03 and 0x04 as well as 0x00 (records), 0x01 (shared strings) and 0x05
  (NetAsset). Gating records on kind==0 stopped the world from building at all
  (spawn=0 in a 60 s run); kinds 2/3/4 parse cleanly with the ordinary record
  grammar and are now accepted.
* NetAsset (kind 0x05) is parsed via parseNetAssetBatch instead of dropped;
  payload stays opaque by design. Shared-string batches are parsed and their
  values kept (keyed by the 16-byte content key) — they cannot be substituted
  into `schema.shared[idx]` (tag 49 indexes the 0x97 order, not a hash) — 183
  entries in a 70 s session.

Resolved (2026-11-03, later): the residual `0xb7`/`0xd9`/`0xe8` stops were
not unknown item types. A live `0x02` spawn's parent ref carries a 4-byte
varint `lo` whose fourth byte has the high bit set; reading it as an unbounded
LEB128 consumed 3 bytes of the following `u32` and desynced the next item.
`read_ref` now caps `lo` at 4 bytes. Script spawns are followed by a typeless
bytecode bulk whose first key byte can collide with a real type (`0x1f`); the
walker parses that bulk after a `LocalScript`/`ModuleScript`/`Script` spawn
when the next byte is not `0x00`/`0x02`. Verified on a 35 s live join:
`stops=0 walk_fail=0 unk_flag=0 unk_id=0 unk_app=0 bulks=1`, local avatar
linked, 1411 A1 messages / 15459 records.

## 2026-11-03 — complete RakNet wire layer, input module, feature flags

**Shipped.** `client/src/raknet/protocol.{h,cpp}` is now the transport's single
wire vocabulary: every packet id this deployment uses (0x09/0x10/0x13/0x15,
Roblox's 0x7b/0x78/0x7d/0x7e handshake, app 0x81..0xa7, ACK 0xc0/0xd0, NAK
0xa0), all five reliability classes, the datagram + message + split headers,
ACK/NAK record encode/decode (both the client's compact form and the RCC's
trailing-u24 form), a bit-exact stream reader/writer, and a per-session
`Census`. `rx_stream.cpp` now parses through the shared codec; the reassembly
policy itself is unchanged.

`client/src/raknet/input.{h,cpp}` holds the captured 0xA6 route frames and the
ControlState block (`[N][A][B][len][presence][values]`) with explicit slot
masks. `--input=control --slots=0,3` is the probe surface for the unrecovered
movement mapping; no "forward" key is invented.

The run summary prints `raknet: census dgrams=… data=… acks=… naks=… msgs=…
malformed=… unk_internal=… unk_app=… internal=… app=… sub=… unknown=…`, so
coverage is a number per session. Unknown ids are named once and counted, not
silently dropped. `0x51..0x56` are now named clock pings instead of falling
into the unknown bucket.

**Feature flags.** `--no-render` (same as `--headless`), `--no-scripts` (a real
gate in `LiveRuntime`: replicated chunks are counted as `scripts_skip` instead
of executed), `--no-physics` (accepted; physics is server-authoritative and
there is no client solver), plus the positive `--render/--scripts/--physics`
forms. This is the RAM-saving switch surface the client was missing.

**Verification.** `raknet_protocol` (new: 50 checks over every layout) and
`raknet_rx_stream` pass; full ctest is 17/18 with only the pre-existing
`unit-engine stream_grafted` failure. A live join was not possible today —
`joinGame` failed before the handshake (the saved cookie no longer yields a
join script), so this work is offline-verified only.

**Avatar gate, unchanged and still open.** The RCC replicates other players'
Characters (`player character: …`) but never spawns ours. The remaining
candidates, in evidence order: the 0xA4 join-progress auth (never
reproduced), upstream client-instance replication (0x83/1c/0x0e), and driven
ControlState content. The 0xA6 framing is now native-exact and switchable, so
a live session can bisect those without fabricated bytes.

## 2026-11-03 (cont.) — the avatar gate is the post-answer 0x83/04 record

**0x97 now always builds.** The two-byte tag after 0x97 is a rolling version,
not a fixed magic: `e5 d3` appeared where older jobs sent other values. The
snapshot parser now validates structure (header `00 00` + u32 size + zstd
magic + decompressed size) instead of rejecting an unknown tag. First live job
that built: `schema=yes`, 1732 frames, 1631 spawns.

**Spawn.** The native's ordered stream after the challenge answer is
`0x9b@5, 0x83/04@6, 0x83/23@7, 0x83/06@8, 0x83/1c@9`. The client sent
everything except the 55-byte 0x83/04 record — its client-owned character
state. Replaying that captured record at slot 6 made the RCC spawn our
Character on both test accounts and on unrelated jobs:

| run | account | 0x83/04 | local Character | inbound 0xA1 |
|-----|---------|---------|-----------------|--------------|
| A   | king    | yes     | kingdudely_alt, 13 parts | 1395 |
| B   | king    | no      | none | 0 |
| A2  | mizg    | yes     | MissGurl_2345, 26 parts | 1584 |
| B2  | mizg    | no      | none | 0 |

The record embeds instance ids from the capture's job (0x0a95/0x0a96/0x0a92),
yet the RCC spawned in jobs whose ids are 0xc5____, so it reads the record as
"the client has a character", not as a reference to those ids. It is sent by
default; `RBX_NO_8304=1` disables it and `RBX_8304_RAW=<file>` overrides the
bytes. `LiveRuntime::local_character()` resolves the avatar by UserId (the CLI
now installs `rbx::local_user_id`), and the viewer reframes when it appears.

**Inbound 0xA1 is not the outbound camera.** Once the avatar exists the RCC
streams ~100 0xA1 messages/s, each ~1 KB, carrying clock/id/CFrame-shaped
records and strings; the outbound 0xA1 camera is 44 B at ~1.4/s. It is counted
as `unk_id` today and `RBX_APP_HEX=a1` dumps the first eight. Parsing this
stream is the next step for character motion/physics, not for spawning.

**Verification.** `raknet_protocol`, `raknet_rx_stream`, `join_msg_raknet` and
`unit-replicate` pass; `unit-engine` still fails only the pre-existing
`stream_grafted` case. Headful capture: `screenshots/avatar_mizg.png`
(`boxes=329 avatar=1`).

### Inbound 0xA1: envelope and records (2026-11-03, later)

The RCC sends 0xA1 only after the local Character exists (A/B above). Full
payload dumps (`RBX_DUMP_APP=<dir> RBX_DUMP_APP_ID=a1`, 800 messages across
three live sessions) give a fixed 17-byte envelope:

    [a1][u32 tick][u32 records][u32 ack][u32 flags][records...]   (all LE)

Records start with a 5-byte ref (`01` + u32 instance id) followed by a u16
**class id**; the class-specific payload follows. First-record class
histogram from a 30 s live run (1606 A1 messages / 26786 header-counted
records): Humanoid 266 (750), Part 360 (583), MeshPart 330 (103), Tool 558
(34), Player 383 (21), Workspace 630 (17), Model 335 (16), Animator 28 (7),
plus Attachment, BodyVelocity, Folder, HumanoidDescription, IntValue,
Lighting, NumberValue, Players, SelectionBox, Status, Weld and AuroraService.
Examples from one 225B message (`records=5`): a Humanoid record, then an
Animator record (`AnimationId5` etc.), then more Humanoid records. Tool
payloads carry a length-prefixed attribute blob; the two Tool samples in
`/tmp/a1dump` end in `HotbarOrder` / `MouseCursor` attributes and the
`rbxasset://textures/Gun*Cursor.png` values.

The complete record frame is now known (2026-11-03, later):

    [ref lo+u32 id][u16 class][u16 member_count]
    [ceil(member_count/64) x u64 LE mask words][u16 body_len][body]

`member_count` is not capped at 64: 68/69/70/82-member classes use two mask
words. Records are sequential. A four-byte marker before the next record can
be `01 00 00 00` **or** `01 00 01 00` (and is sometimes absent), so the parser
uses `body_len` plus a validated following record header rather than a fixed
separator. This parses all 500 frozen corpus frames and the latest live run
(1,166 messages / 14,978 records) with zero A1 failures. `AppStateRecord`
exposes every mask word and the raw body.

Still opaque: the class-specific body values (Humanoid/Animator state and how
Tool attributes are applied to the local DataModel). The stream is no longer
counted as an unknown app id; `LiveRuntime::stats()` reports
`a1=<messages>/<records> a1_first=<class>:<count>` and the first three
messages log their tick/records/class. `parseAppStateHeader` is pinned by
`unit-replicate`. The outbound 44-byte 0xA1 camera frame is a different
message (see above); inbound state is never echoed.

### 0xA9 compressed app packet: LZ4 block (2026-11-04)

The native `Client::OnReceive` recognizes `0xA9` both directly and inside a
`0x1B` timestamp envelope (`*v42 == 0xA9`, or `*v42 == 27` with
`v42[9] == 0xA9`) and calls `sub_55F0544`. That function reads a LEB128
decompressed size (`sub_391768C`), optionally verifies a u16 trailer when
`AddHashToCompressedPayload` is enabled, then calls `sub_253DF1C`. The latter
is the LZ4 block grammar: token literal/match nibbles, 15-extension runs,
little-endian 16-bit offsets, overlapping match copies. The wrapper is

    [A9][LEB128 decompressed size][LZ4 block][optional u16 hash]

with the optional 9-byte `0x1B` timestamp prefix. Our client now decompresses
both forms and feeds the inner application payload through the normal message
sink. The optional hash trailer is accepted but not value-checked yet; the
LZ4 output and exact-close checks still fail closed on malformed input. No
`0xA9` application packet has appeared in the captured live sessions, so this
is native-layout evidence rather than a live capture. `0xA9` is receive-only
for this client (the native direction is server-to-client).

### 0x93 network settings: live payload form (2026-11-03, later)

The live sink passes the payload **after** the app id, while raw captures
include the id. `parseNetworkSettings` now accepts both forms. The live push
is 1,987 bytes, `flags=0` and **51** `[u16 name_len][name][u16 value_len]
[value]` entries; the first is `AnimGraphServerAuthorityPhaseSyncSkip=true`
and the last is `RCCChannelName=production`. `LiveRuntime` logs this as
`0x93 network settings: flags=0 entries=51`. There is no client-side 0x93
builder: it is a server-to-client setup message, not echoed.

### Outbound 0x96 / 0x99 probes (2026-11-04)

The native-shaped client handshake messages now have RakNet builders too:
`0x96` idle `[96 00]`, `0x96` flag form `[96 01 08 00 00 00]`, `0x99`
14-byte capability report, and the `0x04` marker ack (`[04][u32 LE]`).
`--app-probe=96,99` sends the two handshake messages once after the join;
it is opt-in because they are not part of the proven RakNet join ladder on
every job. Live result: both sent (2B / 14B), avatar linked, A1 794/9,395
records, `stops=0 walk_fail=0 unk_flag=0 unk_id=0 unk_app=0`, no 0x15.
The top-level `--packet-manifest` now routes to RakNet directly, so the
coverage table is available without a connection-type flag.

### Remaining 0x83 stops (2026-11-03, later)

The latest 25 s join had `stops=1`, pinned at `0xa3` after a `0x02` spawn.
`0xa3` is the already-documented 24-byte atomic unknown; its first byte is
outside both native dispatch tables, so native also throws/drops there. Other
recent sessions pinned `0xb9`, `0xe3` and `0xe5`; those bytes are likewise
left unassigned rather than guessed. The walker stops loudly at these bytes
and never silently skips them. The rest of that run was clean:
`walk_fail=0 unk_flag=0 unk_id=0 unk_app=0`.

### App-id coverage audit (2026-11-03, later)

The app range is **0x81..0xa9**, not 0x81..0xa7. `0xa8` is a real early-auth
builder (`[a8][version][u8 pre_len][pre][u8 auth_len][auth]`, RbxTransport),
and the native's `Client::OnReceive` checks `0xa9` as the compressed-packet
wrapper (`sub_63DC94E`: `if (*v42 == 0xA9 || ...)`). Both were outside the old
range and would have been counted as internal/unknown forever. `0xa0` is the
transport NAK and is explicitly excluded from `is_app_message`.

`client/src/raknet/protocol.cpp` now has an `AppPacketInfo` entry for every
id 0x81..0xa9: direction (`c2s`, `s2c`, `both`, `transport`), `receive_decoded`,
`send_buildable`, and a layout note. The manifest is printed offline with
`./build/client/roblox --packet-manifest`; unobserved ids are `(unobserved)`,
not invented. This is the audit surface for the remaining payload work.

Census bug fixed: `Census::note_app` used to require
`!packet_info(id) && !is_app_message(id)`, but `is_app_message` is true for
every id in the range, so an unnamed app id could never increment `unk_app`.
It now flags any app id with no named `AppPacketInfo`, and `0xa5` is the unit
case. `note_internal` likewise treats a null name as unknown. The lookup must
be the app table, not `PacketInfo`: `0x87/0x88/0x8b/0x8c` are named chat ids
in `kAppPackets` but have no transport-table entry, so using `packet_info`
reported every received chat packet as `unk_app`.

### Native app-dispatch recheck (2026-11-04)

`sub_6524916` and its copy `sub_6528C54` test `v25 - 135 < 2 ||
 v25 - 139 < 2` and call `sub_63C8D81`; this is direct native proof that the
chat receive ids are `0x87/0x88/0x8b/0x8c`. The same dispatcher categorizes
`0x83`, `0x86`, `0x8d`, `0xa1`, `0xa5` and `0xa6`, so `0xa5` is a real app id,
not an alias for `0xa4`. The four copies also compare `> 0xA4`; `0xa4` there is
only an upper bound, not a handled case. `0x9c` is handled at the top level by
`Client::OnReceive` (`sub_63DC94E`) through `sub_63D75A8`, which extracts a
length-prefixed blob and queues it; no app-level schema has been recovered for
it yet. The manifest keeps `0x9c` and `0xa5` unobserved rather than inventing
payloads.

Observed inbound ids remain covered: 0x00/0x03 keepalive, 0x10 accept,
0x51..0x56 clock, 0x81 peer, 0x83 chains, 0x84 marker, 0x93 settings,
0x97 join data, 0x9B challenge, 0xA1 state. Observed outbound: 0x00 pad,
0x04 marker ack, 0x51..0x53 probe pings, 0x83/04, 0x83/05, 0x83/07, 0x83/23,
0x8A, 0x8F, 0x90, 0x92, 0x96/0x99 (opt-in probes), 0x9B, 0xA1 camera (built,
capture-gated), 0xA6, 0xA7. The audit makes the unobserved ids and the
still-opaque payloads explicit instead of claiming they are decoded.

## 2026-11-04 — 0x86 instance-pair stream (structure pinned, semantics open)

A live 189707 session (`/tmp/join_189707_86hex.log`, 20 s) produced 50 0x86
messages / 74 records. Every payload has the same shape:

```
[86][u8 version = 01]  then  N * 11 bytes
N = (len - 2) / 11
record: [u32 LE ref_a][u8 ref_kind][u32 LE ref_b][u8 state][u8 flag]
```

The observed byte lengths were 13, 24, 35, 46, 57 and 90: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and
8 records. The byte after 0x86 is therefore a version, not a record count
(the 24-byte form has two records but carries 01 there). All 74 records had
`ref_kind = 1`; `state` counts were 0:13, 1:35, 2:4, 3:8, 4:10, 5:4 and `flag`
was 0:50 / 1:24. Example one-record payload:

```
86 01 a7 9a 2e 00 01 12 9f 2e 00 04 00
   version  ref_a     kind ref_b     state flag
```

`parseAppInstancePairs` (`client/src/raknet/protocol.*`) enforces the version,
exact 11-byte record boundary and `ref_kind == 1`, and is exercised by the
captured 13-byte and 24-byte vectors in `raknet_protocol_test`. The message is
wired through `LiveRuntime`, counted as `pairs=<messages>/<records>`, and the
manifest marks it receive-decoded. It is not named by a native handler: the
IDA comparisons found for 0x86 are size/priority and routing checks
(`sub_6412062`, `sub_648F01C`, `sub_6521AAA`, `sub_65244C0`, `sub_6524916`,
`sub_6526A9C`, `sub_6528C54`, `sub_654E58C`), not a semantic decoder. `RBX_PAIR_CLASSES=1`
prints the DataModel classes for both refs if the stream recurs.

The parser landed after the 0x86 capture; six subsequent 20–30 s joins on
189707 did not contain 0x86, so there has been no new live application effect
to measure. The field roles are left at their wire names.

### Join status at the same checkpoint

- 1818, 30 s: `stage=2`, `local avatar linked: ChatGGPT`, `unk_app=0`.
- 189707: two of six post-parser joins (12–30 s) reached `stage=2`, linked
  `ChatGGPT` and reported `unk_app=0`; the other four reached `stage=2` with
  `unk_app=0` but no local Character. None of the six carried 0x86.
- The default 0xA6 route stream keeps the session alive and carries
  `nActions = 0`; the RCC's LoadCharacter trigger is still not deterministic
  on that place. The 0x83/04 55-byte bootstrap is sent in all runs. Do not
  treat "world joined" as "local avatar guaranteed".

## 2026-11-04 — native chat receive (IDA layout, live delivery age-gated)

`sub_63C8D81` / `sub_63CABA0` dispatch 0x87/0x88/0x8B/0x8C into the native
chat path. The receive layout is now implemented in `parseAppChat`:

```
0x87/0x88: [id][ULEB sender_low][u32 LE sender_high][u32 LE text_len]
           [text][optional u8][optional u8]
0x8B:     [id][u32 LE text_len][text][optional u8][optional u8]
0x8C:     [id][ULEB sender_low][u32 LE sender_high]
           [ULEB target_low][u32 LE target_high][u32 LE text_len]
           [text][optional u8][optional u8]
```

The pair low half is ULEB; the high half is fixed u32 LE. The parser rejects a
body over the native 1010-byte limit, a length that overruns the body, and a
third trailing byte. `LiveRuntime` counts every accepted chat message and logs
the id, sender/target pairs, optional flags and text. The manifest marks these
four ids receive-decoded and still marks sending unavailable. Tests cover all
four layouts plus overrun, trailing-byte and non-chat-id failures.

The codec is **native-derived, not live-capture-confirmed**. Two controlled
same-job attempts could not make the server deliver a chat frame to the
receiver:

- Doors, place 6516141723, job `45a15d48-a884-4473-ad8e-a961c3548387`, server
  `128.116.56.33|49622`: receiver `MissGurl_2345` (2477467842) and sender
  `kingdudely_alt` (3733467888) were both in the player list. The sender used
  `TextChatService.TextChannels.RBXGeneral:SendAsync`; it received its own
  `Success` echo and the system message "Only people in similar age groups and
  your trusted friends can chat with you." The receiver's 1722 opened frames /
  419 app messages contained no 0x87/0x88/0x8B/0x8C.
- Baseplate, place 1818, job `823f3026-4517-4b60-8dae-a23811839db8`, server
  `128.116.46.33|49448`: `LegacyChatService` `SayMessageRequest:FireServer`
  produced the same age-group system message; the receiver's 1371 opened frames
  also contained no chat id. `TextChatService:CanUsersChatAsync` is server-only
  from the client, so the gate could not be checked locally.

A 240-second Brookhaven (4924922222, job
`3ec17908-1da4-4c41-822b-d51e5e0293a1`) receiver capture had no chat id in any
of its 2702 opened frames. These runs establish that the absence is
delivery/age gating (or a non-RakNet chat transport), not a parser read of a
malformed 0x87. Do not describe these four codecs as live-confirmed until a
real server chat frame is captured; do not invent an outgoing builder.

## 2026-11-05 — replicated RemoteEvent arguments are no longer dropped

The streaming applier used to call `Signal::fire(L)` with no arguments for a
0x07 invocation. A handler therefore ran, but `OnClientEvent` received
`inst == nil, s == nil, ...` for every argument. `fireStreamEvent` now decodes
each parsed `EventArg` directly onto the Lua stack and dispatches through the
new `Signal::fire_lua(L, nargs)`; positions are preserved, and a shape this
client cannot materialize becomes `nil` at that position rather than shifting
later arguments.

Decoded now: string/BinaryString, bool, signed zigzag ints, unsigned varint,
f32/f64, u32, DateTime, UDim, UDim2, Vector2, Vector3, Color3/Color3uint8,
BrickColor, NumberRange, Rect, Ray, CFrame (tag 26/27/51), entity refs, Enum
items, Content, FontFace, asset strings, Name/shared strings, and the
Tuple/ValueArray/ValueTable/ValueMap family (nested tables, string keys). An
unresolved entity ref is `nil`, matching the absence of a local instance; it
is never a fabricated object. Unknown tags are nil and countable rather than
silently changing the argument list.

`Signal:Wait()` now returns those arguments too: the scheduler carries an
anchored argument array into the resumed thread. A `Wait`-er on
`OnClientEvent` therefore observes the same values as a `Connect` handler,
including nested tables.

Unit coverage: `unit_engine` constructs a RemoteEvent, fires a synthetic 0x07
with a self-reference, string, int, bool, Vector3 and a string-keyed
ValueTable, and checks every value from Luau; a second case drives
`OnClientEvent:Wait()` and checks both returned arguments. Full engine suite:
42 cases / 1002 assertions; `unit_replicate` 69/1055 and `unit_profile` 3/23
stay green.

Live verification: a 20 s place-1818 join (headless + `--gui`) reached
`stage=2`, executed the replicated bulk, reported `scripts_skip=0`, and
delivered `events=85 fired=39` with no script/runtime error. That run also
spawned 1,527 instances and parsed every observed item type
(`walk_fail=0`, `unk_app=0`).

**Still upstream-missing.** `FireServer`, movement/owned writes, and the
native remote-event serializer are not implemented. The client still sends
only join/auth traffic, marker acks, clock/route samples and probe frames.
The IDA binding path is located (`sub_504BD9E` / `sub_504C9CE` ->
`sub_504E694` -> queue enqueue in `sub_504EAAC`); the queue consumer that
emits the 0x83 event body has not yet been resolved, so there is no invented
outgoing builder. Inbound event delivery is now complete for the wire shapes
observed in live sessions; outbound remote events remain the next real gap.

## 2026-11-05 (later) — the classic loading screen runs; game is a DataModel

Four gaps surfaced by running real replicated LocalScripts on place 1818 and
189707:

1. **Font was gated behind --gui.** The 6301 B ClassicLoadingScreen sets
   `TextLabel.FontFace = Font.new("rbxasset://fonts/families/…")` at line 72.
   With `--gui` off, `Font` was nil and the script died with "attempt to index
   nil with 'new'". Font/FontFace are datatypes, not a window: they are now
   registered unconditionally. The GUI *tree* (PlayerGui, CurrentCamera) stays
   behind `--gui`, which is now on by default; `--no-gui` remains the minimal
   opt-out.
2. **FontFace had no property decoder.** Once the property was declared, the
   snapshot's FontFace values were counted as type mismatches. Tag 54 is
   `[u16 weight][u8 style][u32 family len][family][u32 face-source len][source]`;
   decoding it moved three 1818 snapshot props from skipped to applied
   (props_set 6018 -> 6021, typemismatch 0).
3. **Every 0x78 bulk was executed.** NDS 189707 produced 0x78 pseudo-parses
   inside MeshPart collision data (`CSGPHS…`) and server `Script` chunks.
   Execution now requires a client-run class (LocalScript/CoreScript, or
   Script with `RunContext.Client`), never ModuleScript, and a payload whose
   first byte is a Luau bytecode version after container decode. The loading
   screen still runs (scripts=1/0/0); the false positives are counted as
   skipped instead of failed.
4. **`game` was a plain Lua table.** `game:GetService` worked, but
   `game:GetDescendants()`, `game:IsA("DataModel")`, `game:FindFirstChild`
   and `game.ClassName` did not. `engine::attach` now pushes the real
   DataModel instance as `game`, sets JobId/Workspace internally (readonly to
   scripts), and `GetService` is a DataModel method that looks at replicated
   service children first, then the engine service registry. This exposed
   580 scripts on NDS and made `StarterPlayerScripts` reachable once streaming
   delivered the container.

Also: `tostring(Enum.X.Y)` now returns `Enum.X.Y` instead of the userdata
address.

Unit: 46 cases / 1028 assertions green (`unit_engine`). Live: place 1818
loading screen completes with no script failure; place 189707 reaches
`scripts=0/0/N` (no chunk has a source bulk yet) and its DataModel walk works.

**Next real gap:** replicated LocalScript *sources* for StarterPlayerScripts.
The server sends a Source hash on the script and only the 0x78 bulk when the
client's content cache misses; we currently run bulks that arrive but never
request one. That is the next protocol feature, not an API stub.

## 2026-11-05 (final) — Instance.dot-child, DataModel services, script content keys

**`game.Players` threw while `game:FindFirstChild("Players")` worked.** The
Instance `__index` never fell back to children, so a real LocalScript's
`game.Players.LocalPlayer` failed on a perfectly replicated tree. Lookup is now
native order: Methods table -> class methods -> events -> Parent/ClassName ->
declared props -> **child by name** -> error. Measured on MM2: its 299 B
LoadingS2 chunk now runs (`script id=44215 ... ok`); before the fix it died at
`script:1: Players is not a valid member of DataModel`.

**`game.RunService` was nil even though GetService returned it.** Two causes:
(1) engine service singletons were only in the `rbx.Services` table, never in
the DataModel; `adopt_services` now parents the unreplicated ones under the
root and rebinds each replicated service's registry slot to the tree child
(measured: 343 table entries -> 338 adopted, 5 rebound against a root that held
only Players in the unit fixture). (2) The dump declares DataModel `Workspace`,
`workspace`, `lighting` and `RunService` as readonly Instance props whose
stored default is null; those were shadowing the children. `get_prop` now
resolves a null DataModel Instance prop to the matching child (name or class,
case-insensitive, which is what `workspace`/`lighting` need). Probe after the
fix: `game.Players`, `Players.LocalPlayer`, `PlayerGui`, `CurrentCamera`,
`game.RunService` and `GetService("RunService")` all resolve, `GetService`
identity equals dot access, and `game:FindFirstChild("RunService")` is the same
instance.

**Script Source is a content hash, and it is the bulk key.** The snapshot
carries `LocalScript.Source` 220x, `ModuleScript.Source` 173x, `Script.Source`
100x as tag 0x2f, 16 bytes (a per-join content hash). We used to drop all 481
as undeclared. They are now recorded as the script's content key (16-byte map
plus both 8-byte halves), and a 0x78 bulk is attributed through its 8 key bytes
instead of "the last spawn item". Live confirmation: the loading-screen bulk
matched its script's recorded hash (`bulk key=8f4ac1867340c4c7 matches=1
last=44215 owner=44215`) and ran. On 1818 the full-snapshot audit moved
undeclared 2303 -> 2067 with `source_hashes=236` and no other counter changed.

**Native capture: there is no script-content request.** `rc_capture.sh` on
place 142823291 (90 s, 5484 TX frames / 535 KB, `RC_HOOKS=kdf,seal,wire`)
joined (job 4d64c1f5, user 4656429295) and ran a game script
(`Workspace.Lobby.Elite.Screen.Surface.Frame.Frame.Script` at 8.58 s), yet none
of four sampled Source hashes appears in any TX frame in raw, reversed or
half form, and the client makes no assetdelivery/script HTTP request. So the
RCC *pushes* script bodies over replication; missing bodies are a parse
problem, not a missing client message.

**That parse problem is the 0x83 chain walker.** MM2 runs show 25-146
`walk stopped` per 20-35 s (highly variable; 1818 is 2-3 per run), with the
stop byte spread across many values (0x41/0x6e dominated one run, 0x77/0x9c
another). Since a chain is applied only up to its stop, every later item --
including 0x78 script bulks -- is dropped. Attacking the walker needs the item
grammar for those types (the native dispatch tables are the ground truth), not
more stubs in the runtime.

Tests: `unit_engine` 48 cases / 1049 assertions, `unit_replicate` 69 / 1055,
`unit_profile` 3 / 23, `unit_runtime` 10 / 33 — all green. Live: 1818 joins,
world=3438 instances, loading screen runs, walk_fail=0; MM2 joins, world
~17-33k instances, LoadingS2 runs, DataModel/service probe clean.

### Viewer: raylib -> SDL3 + OpenGL 3.3 (2026-11-06)

raylib is gone from the build (`view_raylib.cpp` removed, 568 lines). The
window, input, GL context and PNG path are SDL3; drawing is plain OpenGL 3.3
core with one instanced `glDrawElementsInstanced` per part shape (ball, block,
cylinder, wedge, corner wedge), a 2048² directional shadow map with 3x3 PCF,
CPU frustum culling, an alpha pass sorted back-to-front, sky + fog and 4x MSAA.
No renderer library is linked (`ldd` shows SDL3 + libOpenGL only); `rbx_view`
still falls back to `view_stub.cpp` when SDL3/OpenGL dev files are absent, so
CI and headless-only machines behave exactly as before.

Two real bugs surfaced while verifying against a live 1818 join:
`--shot` counted warmup frames from window open, so it captured 2 s into a
join where the snapshot had not landed (sky-only PNG) — the counter now starts
when the replicated world exists. And the Gribb/Hartmann frustum planes were
extracted by matrix *column* instead of row, which culled the whole world at
third-person distance (overview happened to survive); row indexing fixed
`boxes=0` at close range to 652 boxes / avatar framed.

Checks: `unit-engine` 48 cases green, client tests 3/3 green, live 1818 join
(world 3185-3627 instances) renders framed overview and third-person avatar
shots. Live join needs a fresh cookie: reusing a saved `join_*.json` can point
at a reaped RCC (`no OpenConnectionReply1`), not a client bug.

### Parity snapshot after the vfmt/schema-tag pass (2026-11-06)

Crossroads 1818 (45 s headless): world 3566-3810 instances, `stops=2`,
`walk_fail=0`, `prop3=48`, `prop_miss=3`. MM2 142823291 (45 s headless):
world 6763, 46k spawn items, `prop3=1923`, `prop_miss=28`, `stops=18`,
`walk_fail=3`, `pairs=137/605`. The remaining diagnostics are concrete:

- `prop_miss` on MM2 is `BallSocketConstraint.Attachment0/1` applied before
  their Attachment entities exist (out-of-order refs; `applyStreamProp`
  rejects unknown ids), and prop 10328, which is past the frozen schema's
  10145-member bound (rolling-build member, loud by design).
- Most `stops` are one trailing chain byte with no body (`0x9c`, `0xff`,
  `0xa4`, ...) reached after the last spawn in a message; the applied prefix
  is intact. A few (`0x77`, `0x84`) are content-keyed bulks that
  `parseBulkItem` does not yet accept, and `0x7d` is pinned by the
  `prop03_5018` test.
- `RBX_LOG_PROPMISS=1` names every rejected triple; `RBX_DUMP_FAIL` and
  `RBX_DUMP_STOP` keep replayable bodies for each gap.

Tests: 18/18 green (`unit_replicate` 69 cases / 1070 assertions).

### Correction: `0xa3` was never an item — it is a Tags-keyed spawn bulk (2026-11-06)

Two chat messages captured in separate sessions/accounts (132 B and 137 B)
both pinned the walk at `0xa3` right after a `Folder` spawn. They are now
proven complete with **no unknown item at all**. The bytes after loop B are
the spawn's **inline content bulk keyed by its own `Tags` hash** (16 bytes,
byte-for-byte equal to the `Tags` value the spawn just wrote):

```
[02][ref][Folder cls] [06 07 "Message"] [09 <16B Tags hash>] [0a 02] ff
<16B Tags hash> 00 07 "Message" <anchor ref>      <- inline bulk
[02][ref][StringValue cls] ff [00 <len> "Regenerating ..."] [05 0b "MessageText"] ff
<parent ref = Folder> [23 <clock>] 00
```

Reading the repeated key as a parent ref consumed its first 6 bytes
(`ea 30 fe f5 17 9f`) and left the cursor on the key's 7th byte, `0xa3`, which
is why a "24-byte atomic unknown" appeared to exist: skipping `a3` + 23 bytes
landed exactly on the following `StringValue` spawn, so the old length was
self-consistent. It was an artifact of stopping inside the key.

**Rule now implemented:** a spawn carries its bulk inline when the 16 bytes
after loop B equal *any* 16-byte hashed prop of that same spawn, `parseBulkItem`
succeeds, and the byte after the bulk is `0x00` (end) or a real item type
(≤ `0x41`). `BulkItem::key_tag` records which prop matched. Only tag 47
(`Source`) is script bytecode and surfaces as pseudo-item `0x78`; the tag 46
(`Tags`) payload is opaque content, so it advances the cursor and supplies the
parent from its anchor but is **never** handed to the Luau loader.

Live 1818 Crossroads (main account, 30 s headless): `stops=0`, `walk_fail=0`,
`prop_miss=0`, `spawn=2142`, `events=251`, `bulks=1`, `scripts=1/0/0`,
`a1=1748/19105`. The chat envelope now contributes its `Folder "Message"` +
`StringValue "MessageText"` to the DataModel instead of dropping the tail.
Tests: 18/18 green (`unit_replicate` 73 cases / 1141 assertions), including a
synthetic Tags-keyed bulk case pinned against the frozen schema.

### Tag 60 (NetAssetId) carries a fixed 17-byte value (2026-11-06)

MM2 (`place 142823291`) stopped at `record 641` of a 0x16 spawn batch:
`content form 9 at offset 38`. The record was a **UnionOperation** whose
second prop is `SolidMeshHolder` (tag 60) — the first straight-to-`tag 60`
sighting in a spawn record.

The value is `[varint schemaIdx=1][8B id][varint v=3][7B tail]` = **17 bytes**.
The tail is inferred, not guessed: the 0x16 chain must tile the payload
exactly, and every one of the 641 records before it (plus all 961 after the
fix) writes **strictly increasing member deltas**. The 7-byte tail is the only
length that keeps that record's deltas increasing:

```
L=10 (old shape)  -> Content@0 after SolidMeshHolder@8   (decreasing, then fail)
L=17              -> TriangleCount@9, UsePartColor@10, PhysicalConfigData@14, ...
```

With the tail read, the batch tiles **EOF-exact** (`961` records, all
increasing) and the union's props are exactly the shape a union part needs:
`InitialSize(3,9,0.6) SolidMeshHolder TriangleCount UsePartColor
PhysicalConfigData inertia*4 Anchored CFrame Color3uint8 NetworkOwnerV3
Transparency size`. Both unions in the batch are identical 3x9x0.6 slabs and
carry byte-identical 17-byte handles (they share one mesh asset).

Live MM2 after the fix: `walk_fail=0`, `sub=16:126` (126 spawn batches, zero
record failures), `spawn=19807`, `prop_miss=0`, `stops=0`. `unit_replicate`
tag-60 case updated to the 17-byte value (73 cases / 1141 assertions green).
Diagnostic: `tools/repl_skipsearch.cpp` (`walk` / `scan` / `len` modes) — it
finds the length by requiring the rest of the chain to parse and the deltas to
keep increasing.

## 2026-11-06 — the 0xA1 state body is the mask-driven member run

The 0xA1 state record was parsed but its body was carried opaque. It is the
same grammar the snapshot uses: `member_count` indexes the class's **schema
member list**, the mask words select members, and the selected members' values
follow in ascending index order, each in the normal tag grammar
(`read_tagged_value`). `parseAppStateBody` (replicate.h / repl_stream.cpp)
decodes exactly that and refuses the record unless the body closes on the
selected members, so an unmodelled tail is never applied as a guess.
`LiveRuntime::onState` then feeds every decoded member through
`applyStreamProp`, i.e. an A1 record is applied exactly like a 0x03 update.

Live 1818 (`kingdudely_alt`, 40 s): `a1=2762/36136 applied=14642/31172
a1fail=20491` — 40 % of records close exactly and carry the visible world.
A/B screenshots on the same job (`--shot`) are the proof: with the state
applied the frame shows terrain, trees, buildings and the local avatar; with
`RBX_NO_A1_APPLY=1` the same world is a dark empty baseplate with a handful of
floating parts (positions/colors live in this stream, not in the snapshot).

Remaining tails, per class, each logged once by name: `Tool` (the known
attribute blob after the members), `Part`/`SelectionBox` (extra trailing bytes
after the selected members), and value-shape failures (`truncated 12B/16B
value`) on records whose mask and body disagree. These are per-class tails,
not a framing error: the envelope, refs, masks and body lengths stay exact.

189707 stayed nondeterministic in the same session (no avatar, A1 bodies all
with tails); Adopt Me (920587237) never delivered 0x97 in 35 s, so its world
was not applied at all — neither is A1 evidence.
Evidence frames: `screenshots/a1_state_applied.png` and
`screenshots/a1_state_not_applied.png` (same job, same account, 30 s).

### The 0xA1 tail is the per-instance attribute section

`RBX_A1_TAIL=1` dumps the bytes `parseAppStateBody` leaves after a record's
selected members (one line per class). They are attribute payloads, not
leftover member values:

```
MeshPart       00 52 42 58 52 65 66 ... 00 00 80 3f x3   RBXRefinementScale = Vector3(1,1,1)
Attachment     1d 00 00 00 "RBXOriginalAttachmentRotation" 10 <12B>
Tool           "HotbarOrder" ... 0b 00 00 00 "MouseCursor" 25 rbxasset://...GunWaitCursor.png
SpawnLocation  0c 12 00 00 00 "SpawnLocationIndex"
Animation      15 0e 00 00 00 "LinearVelocity"
Part           00 80 3f 00 00 80 3f 00 00 80 3f           Vector3(1,1,1)
```

Two things follow. First, the section is an attribute list — u32 name length,
name, then a type byte and the variant value (`Tool`'s `HotbarOrder` /
`MouseCursor`, `MeshPart`'s `RBXRefinementScale`, `Attachment`'s
`RBXOriginalAttachmentRotation` are all real attributes). Second, the tail can
begin *inside* that list (`Tool` starts at `HotbarOrder` with its length prefix
already consumed, `Part` starts at a bare Vector3), so the member run and the
attribute run are not simply concatenated: aligning them needs the native
writer's section order. Every record with a tail is refused whole, which is
why `Part`/`SelectionBox`/`Tool` state still does not land; nothing is applied
half-way.

## 2026-11-06 — join watchdog: a half-join is now a rejoin, not a wait

Adopt Me (920587237) reproduced the "can't join" report in this client only:
the RCC answered the join with 0x97 and ~128 shared-string batches, then never
sent the record batch. `finalizeSnapshot` refuses to build from an empty
snapshot, so the session sat on a live transport with zero instances, ran no
scripts, and the old exit line claimed "0x97 never arrived" — which was wrong
and hid the real shape.

`LiveRuntime` now distinguishes the two failure modes and the session aborts a
half-join instead of waiting on it:

* `join_data_seen()` — 0x97 arrived (the RCC answered the placed join).
* `ms_since_progress()` — time since anything that advances the world:
  shared-string batches, NetAsset batches, snapshot record batches, and applied
  0x83 chains. Before the first mark, the 0x97 timestamp is the reference, so a
  big place that is still streaming is never mistaken for a stalled one.
* The run loop (headless and headful) drops the session after
  `RBX_JOIN_STALL_MS` (default 20000) without progress and redoes the whole
  handshake, exactly like the disconnect-rejoin path: fresh join script, fresh
  RakNet session, fresh 0x9B answer.

The exit line is now explicit: "no join data (0x97): the RCC never answered"
versus "join data (0x97) arrived but no record batch followed".

Verified: 5 live Adopt Me joins after the change built a world
(`snapshot batch #1` 1759-1983 records, `spawn=68`..`45046`); no watchdog fired
on any of them even at `RBX_JOIN_STALL_MS=3000`, so progress marks are dense
enough on a healthy join. `unit-engine` pins the contract: 0x97 alone reports
join data + no world, adding the frozen snapshot frames flips it to a world.

### Adopt Me's loader: two real API gaps behind one error

The same job failed its first local script with
`AwaitPersistentModels is not a valid member of LocalScript` (script 96234,
16241 B, a copy of Roblox's InitialClientLoad — its strings include
`initial_client_load`, `create_session`, `LoadTimers`, `NetworkStats`). Two
separate gaps are behind it:

1. `LocalScript:AwaitPersistentModels()` is a CoreScript-only yield missing
   from the public API dump but present in the native client. Added as a real
   method that returns immediately (this client has no model streaming, so
   nothing can be pending). The error moved on, which proves the lookup was
   the actual failure.
2. The loader then failed with `attempt to call a nil value`: it requires the
   replicated `AwaitPersistentModels` **ModuleScript** (id 232156, sibling of
   `LoadTimers`/`LoadingScreen`/`NetworkStats`). This engine has no `require`
   at all — ModuleScripts are skipped and no module loader/cache exists. That
   is the next Luau parity item, and it is now the first thing a real
   experience trips on after the world applies.

### require(): replicated ModuleScripts now load the way the native does

`require` did not exist at all — ModuleScripts were skipped, so the first real
experience that requires one died at the call. Implemented in the engine, not
the client, with native semantics:

* only a ModuleScript is requireable (anything else errors like the native);
* the chunk runs with its **own environment** whose `__index` is the shared
  globals and whose `script` is the module;
* the return value is cached per ModuleScript, so a second require neither
  re-runs nor reloads; a `false` in-flight marker turns a cycle into the
  native's cycle error instead of infinite recursion;
* the module's bytecode is registered by `LiveRuntime` when the 0x78 bulk is
  attributed to a ModuleScript (`set_module_source`), container-decoded and
  wire-standardized exactly like a LocalScript chunk. `stats()` now reports
  `modules=N`; Adopt Me registers 7 on a normal join.

### `script` is per chunk, not a global

The client used to `lua_setglobal("script", ...)` before every execute. A
LocalScript that yields (Adopt Me's loader yields before line 16) then sees
whatever script most recently started, or nil. The engine now installs a
per-chunk environment on the script's **thread** and loads the chunk on that
thread — Luau resolves GETIMPORT against the loading thread's globals and
stores that table as the chunk's environment, so `script` is correct before
the first instruction and survives every yield. `engine::execute` takes the
instance; the engine owns the binding.

`unit-engine` pins both: `require` returns the same table twice, runs the
chunk exactly once, and the module sees `script.Name`.

### Adopt Me's loader: the parent chain, not require, is the remaining block

With require in place the loader gets past `AwaitPersistentModels` as a name
and now fails with `expected an Instance (argument #1)` — i.e. `require(nil)`.
The diagnostic (`RBX_LOG_SCRIPTS`) shows why:

```
run id=96234 'Load' class=LocalScript parent=none children=[]
module id=232156 'AwaitPersistentModels' ... (parent LocalScript 'Load')
```

The chunk is attributed to a LocalScript that is **not in the DataModel** and
has no children, while the seven module chunks are parented to another
`Load` instance (different id, also outside the tree). A detached script owning
a chunk is a snapshot parent-chain gap: the record's parent was never resolved
(and the deferred record was never grafted), so the loader and its modules are
orphans. The in-tree-copy retarget only fires when exactly one same-class,
same-name instance is inside the tree; on this job there is none, so the
chunk still runs on the orphan. Next target: the parent chain of `Load` —
which record was deferred/dropped — not `require` itself.

### The snapshot parent refs are in a different id namespace (biggest world bug)

`RBX_DUMP_JOIN=1` on Adopt Me 920587237, applied snapshot:

```
joinrec id=96284 cls=LocalScript parent=402042 PARENT-MISSING name=MaxZoomDistance
joinrec id=394690 cls=Sound      parent=286719 PARENT-MISSING name=
joinrec id=232253 cls=ModuleScript parent=232255 PARENT-IN-SNAPSHOT ...
world spawned=1983 grafted=1898 deferred=1898 instances=1983 engine=on
```

1898 of 1983 snapshot records have a parent id that is **not in the snapshot**,
so `flushDeferredToRoot` grafts them onto the DataModel root. The result is a
flat world: `Workspace` contains only its `Camera`, every Part/Model/Sound is a
top-level child of `game`. That is why Adopt Me's loader sees
`script.AwaitPersistentModels == nil` and why the tree looks nothing like the
native's. Only the 85 records whose parent ids *are* snapshot ids (module
siblings like 232253 -> 232255) parent correctly.

Two properties of the missing ids rule out the obvious explanations:

* They are not declared in the 0x90 join document: dumping it
  (`RBX_DUMP_JOIN90=<path>`) shows no instance table and no id in the 70k
  range.
* They shift with the session, one for one with the record ids. Two runs:
  parents 76663/76664 and records 96284/96285. So they are allocations from
  the same monotonic session counter, made **before** the snapshot records —
  i.e. instances the RCC created and streamed earlier in the session, in
  messages this client never materializes. The snapshot is a delta against
  that earlier state, not a self-contained tree.

Consequence: the snapshot path cannot be fixed by changing graft policy; the
earlier records (or the id mapping) have to be recovered. The next probe is
the native join-record reader: how it resolves a parent id that is not in the
snapshot (IDA on the 0x97 handler) and which message carried the 76xxx
instances. Live-chain spawns are unaffected: their parent ids are in the live
id space, which is why a streamed part tree still builds and the flat snapshot
alone does not.

### The "error 304 after ~11 minutes" report (2026-11-06)

Public Roblox documentation and community references agree on what 304 means: it
is the client-integrity disconnect — *"Roblox has detected missing or corrupted
files"*. It belongs to the same family as 272 (security key mismatch) and 268
(unexpected client behavior): the server/anti-tamper layer decided the client is
not the official build. It is not a network timeout and not a script error, so
raising RakNet keepalive frequency cannot fix it — but the 11-minute delay says
the check is periodic during a session, not a launch gate.

For this client the observable equivalent is the RakNet `0x15` notification,
which we already decode and log with its numeric code. Nothing in the transport
path currently produces a 304, so a report of "304" from a Boblox user most
likely means the RCC ended the session on an integrity/security-key verdict and
they read the code off the client log. The next time it is reported, the exact
`0x15` line (id, code, hex body) plus the last few `raknet:` lines before it are
what identifies the trigger; without that, changing the handshake is guessing.

### Soak observation: the A1 state stream mostly fails to apply

A Crossroads soak (`--seconds 800`, king account) was cut off externally after
4.5 minutes — the log ends with `raknet: interrupted — closing session`, i.e. the
backgrounded process was signalled, not reaped by the RCC. **It therefore did not
reach the 11-minute window and says nothing about the 304 report.** The numbers
below are from those 4.5 minutes. Final line:

```
live_frames=881 schema=yes stops=0 walk_fail=1 spawn=1898 update=1 entries=1143
a1=1350/16130 applied=6068/14094 a1fail=8535 scripts=1/0/0 modules=0
```

`applied + a1fail` accounts for essentially every A1 record, so **most inbound
state records do not apply**; only ~43 % do. Combined with `scripts=1/0/0`
(one script discovered, none ran successfully) this is a bigger parity gap than
any single missing API: the world streams, but the state channel that carries
Humanoid/Animator/Part updates and the script execution path are both failing on
a place as simple as Crossroads. Triage order: `a1fail` reasons first (they are
per-record and cheap to histogram), then the one discovered script's error.

## 2026-11-06 — the disconnect is 0x15 code 266, and it was our own keepalive cap

A 13-minute Crossroads soak (`--seconds 800`, main account, headless) captured the
exact thing the "disconnects after ~11 minutes" reports describe, and it happened
much sooner. The RCC ended the first session with an explicit notification:

```
raknet: app message 0x15 (5B, rel 0x60, ord 9930) 150000010a
raknet: 0x15 disconnect (code 266 / 0x010a) — session ended by the RCC
```

**266 is Roblox's "your connection timed out"** (the same table lists 264 = same
account on another device, 268 = kicked for unexpected client behavior, 272 =
security key mismatch, 280 = out of date). It is a liveness verdict, not an
anti-tamper one, so no handshake or integrity change is implied by it.

The cause is in `mirrorKeepalive`: every non-pad keepalive id was answered **at
most twice for the whole session** (`if (seen >= 2) return false;`). That cap
was introduced to stop the 1160-byte MTU pad exchange from becoming an echo
loop, but it also applied to the 9-byte `0x00` and 25-byte `0x03`/`0x54..0x56`
pings, which the RCC keeps sending for the lifetime of the session. Measured in
the same soak before the fix: 52 inbound `0x00` against 10 replies, and the
session died minutes later with 266. The second and third sessions of that run
produced **no** `0x15` at all — they simply went silent, which is the same
timeout seen from the client side (and is why an earlier note could honestly say
"nothing in the transport path produces a 304": the visible code here is 266).

Fix: pads stay bounded, small pings are mirrored for the whole session with a
20 ms per-id throttle instead of a lifetime cap.

**It is not the whole story.** The fixed client keeps mirroring (200+ replies
over a run where the old one sent 10), and sessions *still* end with 266 — now at
a repeatable ~5.5 minutes per session, twice in one run, then the third rejoin
never got its `OpenConnectionReply1`. So the ping cap was a real defect but not
the cause of the timeout. What the last seconds of both sessions show is more
specific:

```
data datagram 1093B (flags=0x88 dgram=28341 first=0x70)   <- retransmissions
data datagram  111B (flags=0x88 dgram=28342 first=0x70)
data datagram 1029B (flags=0x88 dgram=28343 first=0x60)
app message 0x83/01 (69B, rel 0x60, ord 12560)
app message 0x15 (5B, rel 0x60, ord 12562) 150000010a     <- 266
```

The RCC is retransmitting datagrams (`flags=0x88`, mid-burst, while ~100 0xA1
state messages/s are arriving) and then declares the connection timed out. That
points at our ACK coverage / receive window under the state firehose rather than
at pings: a datagram we receive but never cover in an ACK is retransmitted until
the server's queue gives up. Next probe: diff the received datagram ids against
the ids we ACK inside one session (`RBX_RAKNET_ACKLOG=1` prints every ACK), and
look for ids that appear in `data datagram` but never in an ACK range.


### Correction: `scripts=a/b/c` is run/failed/skipped

The earlier soak note in this file read `scripts=1/0/0` as "one script
discovered, none ran". That was wrong: the three fields are **run**, **failed**
and **skipped**, so `1/0/0` means one script ran and succeeded. The
Crossroads/1818 numbers in this file are unaffected otherwise, but no conclusion
should be drawn from them about script execution failing.

### The 0xA1 member table is not the property schema

An offline probe over the 500-frame corpus (`tools/a1_tail_probe.cpp`, using a
live `RBX_DUMP_SCHEMA` table) shows the state stream's member table is **not**
the class's property member list. The wire's per-record `member_count` is the
size of the writer's table, and it disagrees with the schema in both directions:

| class | schema members | wire member_count |
|-------|----------------|-------------------|
| Humanoid | 55 | 50 |
| Part | 64 | **82** |
| MeshPart | 81 | 82 |
| SelectionBox | 17 | 5 |
| Tool | 27 | 19 |

Concrete consequence: a Humanoid record with mask bit **46** carries a 4-byte
float. In the property schema index 46 is `Archivable` (tag 9, one byte), so the
decode asks for 12 bytes somewhere else and refuses. Mask bits above the
schema's member count (Part 64..81) cannot be decoded at all. This is a *table*
mismatch, not a framing error: the envelope, refs, masks, body lengths and the
inter-record markers all stay exact, and 5,373 of 6,495 corpus records still
close on their members.

So `parseAppStateBody` no longer discards a record just because it runs past
what the schema models. Mask bits ascend and values follow in index order, so
the members decoded before the first unknown slot are exact; they are returned
with `partial=true`, and `LiveRuntime` applies them and counts `a1part`. On the
corpus this turned 2,585 decodable records into 5,373 with no partial member
applied from unverified bytes. Remaining failures are records whose *first*
selected member is one of the unmodelled slots — their value width is unknown, so
they still fail closed.

Next probe: the 0x97 schema's unparsed tail (`JoinSchema::tail_off`) is the
likeliest home of that second per-class member table. `run/cap97/…bin` keeps a
full 0x97 payload for offline work; it decompresses through the client's own
`parseJoinOuter`, so the tail can be walked without a live session.

### A1 partial application, measured live (2026-11-06)

`a1part` in the stats line is the count of records applied from a decodable
prefix. Crossroads, 1818, 45 s after the change:

```
a1=5070/68432 applied=55365/103600 a1fail=12285 a1part=35680
world spawned=2472 grafted=53 deferred=0 instances=2472 engine=on
```

Before the change the same place at t=60 s reported
`applied=19487/45545 a1fail=28009`, i.e. **58 % of state records refused**;
now **18 %** are, and 35,680 records apply with an exact member prefix. The
remaining `a1fail` are records whose first selected member is one of the
unmodelled replication slots (Humanoid bit 46 and friends), which still fail
closed because the value width is unknown.

### The 11-minute disconnect is the RCC's DisconnectTimeout at exactly 300 s (2026-11-06)

Code 266 is not a report about our traffic and not anti-tamper: `ConnectionError`
in the API dump (`rbx_runtime/src/instance/enums.inc`) maps **266 =
DisconnectTimeout**, and the number is exact, not incidental:

```
raknet: 0x15 DisconnectTimeout (code 266 / 0x010a) at session age 300.138s — session ended by the RCC
```

Same job, same place, measured twice; every later session on that account dies at
the same age, which is why a client left running shows a drop "after a few
minutes" and a rejoin loop. The disconnects do **not** correlate with anything we
send at the time: the RCC is still streaming 0xA1 state and still ACKing our
pings 12 s after the 0x15, so the transport is healthy in both directions when
the verdict lands.

**The transport is not the cause.** `dups_dropped=0` for the whole window: the
RCC never re-sent a datagram we had already received, so its reliable window was
never jammed and our ACK datagrams were accepted. (An earlier note here read the
`flags=0x88` header byte as a retransmission marker; the flag decoder only pins
`0x80` valid / `0x40` ACK / `0x20` continuous-send, so that reading was a guess
and this measurement retires it.)

**The native client does not have this problem, so it is our omission.** Native
`~/Rum/build/roblox` on the same machine, same place (1818), same network:

| | native | this client |
|---|---|---|
| join | 0x97 at 3.4 s | 0x97 at ~3 s |
| survives 300 s | yes | no — `0x15 266` at 300.1 s |
| survived window | 13 min 35 s (killed by hand) | 300 s per session |

That difference is the whole diagnosis: the RCC starts a 300 s timer for a client
that has not sent something, and the native satisfies it.

**What the native sends that we do not** (steady state, from the decrypted 34 s
TX capture plus this session's inbound census): 0xA6 input 13.3/s, 0xA1 camera
1.4/s, 0x83/0x23 clock ~6/s — all of which we also send — plus the **0x83/0x05
pair**: a 45-byte unreliable frame every ~2.5 s and a 50-byte reliable copy every
~5 s. Our default steady state has no 0x83/0x05 at all (`RBX_CANNED_83` gates it
because the captured frames reference another job's instance id).

Those frames are 45/50 bytes and decode as `83 05 <u24 ref> ... <f32 x> <f32 z>
... <f32 clock>` — a per-instance physics/state acknowledgement, i.e. exactly the
kind of message a server would use to decide whether the client that owns a
character's physics is still there.

**That pair is what the timer waits for — measured A/B, same job, same minute:**

| arm | flags | result |
|---|---|---|
| control | none (old default) | `0x15 266` at **300.514 s** |
| pair on | `RBX_CANNED_83=1 RBX_NO_OPENER=1` | no 0x15; live at 361 s, ended by `--seconds` |
| new default | none | no 0x15; live at 362 s (and still going) |

The captured frames are accepted by this job's RCC — the dangling reference is
not fatal the way the 0x83/0x07 opener and the A1 camera frame are (both got
0x0104/0x0105 within a second). So the pair no longer rides on `RBX_CANNED_83`:
`pushInput` sends it on its own, and `RBX_NO_ACK83=1` restores the silent steady
state for A/B runs.

**Known deviation left in the fix:** the two frames are still the captured bytes,
so they acknowledge instance `0x10dc16` from the job they were recorded in, not
the live character. The RCC tolerates it (7 min, two accounts, no 0x0104), but a
native client acks *its own* assemblies. Building the frame from the live
character id and the CFrame the server sent is the next step for exact parity;
the grammar is `83 05 <u24 ref> ... <f32 x> <f32 z> ... <f32 clock>` and the
reliable copy adds an ordered-ack trailer.

### The "11 minutes, 304" report is the Android anti-cheat kick (2026-11-06)

With the 266 timer fixed, a session now reaches the age the users reported, and
the next verdict is exactly the code they quoted:

```
raknet: app message 0x15 (5B, rel 0x60, ord 24816) 1500000130
raknet: 0x15 disconnect (code 304 / 0x0130) at session age 666.788s
```

`ConnectionError` names 304 **AndroidAnticheatKick** (the neighbours are 305
AndroidEmulatorKick / 306 AndroidRootedKick), so this is not a replication or
timing gap like 266 was: it is Roblox's client-integrity verdict on the session
that is playing, delivered ~11 min 7 s after the join, and it lands while the RCC
is still streaming 0xA1 and rotating the session token normally (the log shows
both sides healthy right up to the 5-byte message).

Measured on place 1818, one clean 700 s window:
`live_frames=26112 spawn=25085 events=4379 fired=3511 a1=29170/398303
applied=321625/671064 a1fail=75846 a1part=139238` — the world is fully built and
the Luau path is live when the kick lands, which is why it looks like a random
timeout from the outside.

**Withdrawn (2026-11-07): the "deliberately not pursued" reading above was not a
measurement.** `AndroidAnticheatKick` is the enum's *name* for the code; it does
not establish that the verdict is an attestation check we cannot satisfy, and
nothing here measured a trigger. What is measured: 304 was seen **once**, in a
session 666.788 s old, while the RCC kept streaming 0xA1 and rotating the RUPP
token; a later 700 s round on the same place produced no 0x15 at all, and a
400 s control window none either. So 304 is a single observation with no
reproduction — not a policy position. The 2026-11-07 section at the end of this
file records what the native's traffic does (and does not do) at that age.

### The 0x97 tail is a member-id table, not a prealloc list (2026-11-06)

The earlier note here read the tail as `[u32 630][u32 0][u32 7][u32 0]` + u32 arrays
1..10144 and guessed "prealloc/id lists". A *complete* inner settles the shape, and
it is a member-id table:

```
RBX_DUMP_APP=<dir> ./build/client/roblox ... --seconds 30
  -> 00033_97_54254.bin   54,254 B frame, 358,137 B inner
  -> classes=638 props=10328 tail_off=327417 tail=30720  (= 7,680 u32s, no remainder)
```

* The tail opens `[u32 638]` — the class count again.
* The body is u32 values that climb to **10,327 = prop_bound - 1** and never past it,
  i.e. a table over the global member-id space, with 1,268 ascending runs and
  repeated ids (12 appears twice, 4 several times) because inherited members are
  listed once per class that carries them.
* The last two u32s are the only large values (`67175170`, `132865`), so the tables
  are integer, not content hashes.

So the "extra slots" A1 needs are in here somewhere: the writer's per-class list is
a subset/reordering of the 10,328 schema members, which is exactly why `Part`
claims 82 slots against 64 schema members. Not yet decoded: the per-class
delimiters. A `[u32 count][count ids]` walk from the first entry overshoots the
buffer (consumes 14,551 of 7,680 entries), so the counts are either a separate
array or encoded differently — the next test is to try the count walk at candidate
offsets and accept only a parse that consumes the tail exactly and gives some class
82 slots.

`tools/schema_tail.cpp` is the tool for this: it dumps the tail (hex + u16/u32
views) and can write it raw for offline analysis. It decompresses by ignoring the
outer `u32`, which is the decompressed size on live frames but the compressed size
in gdb harness dumps — a parser that trusts it fails on captures.

## 2026-11-06 — A1 slot table wired in; the remaining A1 gap is record framing

**The 0x97 tail is the A1 slot table.** `[u32 class_count]`, then per class
`[u32 class_id][u32 count][count x u32 global member id]`, then an 8-byte
trailer (verified exact on a live 0x97: 638 lists, 30,712 of 30,720 bytes,
6,401 ids over the 10,328-member space). `parseJoinSchema` now keeps it, and
`parseAppStateBody` resolves a mask bit through `slots[cls][idx]` →
`member_by_gid` → the owning class + member, so a mask selects the writer's
replicated-member list instead of indexing a class's raw member table.

**Measured effect (Crossroads 1818, 70 s):** `a1part` (records that parsed but
stopped at an unrunnable slot) fell **35,680 → 1,639** — the slot lookup is
what resolves those indexes, so it is the right table. The world applies:
`spawned=2812 grafted=73 instances=2812`, 9 snapshot batches, `unk_flag=0
unk_id=0 walk_fail=1`, and `a1=4783/61380` records fully applied with
`applied=46876/89308` members. Tests: `ctest` 3/3 (`unit_replicate` incl. the
new slot cases).

**What is left is not tag 57's shape.** Every remaining A1 failure is counted
under `tag 57 (Part.ReplicationPV)` (13,721 of 13,727), which made 57 look like
the whole gap. It is not:

* `sel=` (raw mask index → class member) and the slot-resolved name disagree:
  index 36 on Part is `PhysicsRepRootRef tag 69` (schema dump, `m 36`), while
  the slot table resolves the same index to `ReplicationPV tag 57` (`m 39`).
  One of the two is wrong for that index, and 57 was never proven to be the
  member actually selected.
* The undecoded bodies are **not one shape**: 14, 19, 21, 30, 31 and 425 bytes
  for the same class, all with `body == tail.size()` (nothing decoded before
  the failure). A property value is not variable in size like that.
* The 19-byte bodies carry a u32 that increments once per frame
  (`b6 ed 60 81`, `62 ec 60 81`, `da eb 60 81`, …) — a clock, not a property.
* The 425-byte body contains ASCII attribute names (`previousHumanoidSpeed`,
  `currentAnimTime`) inside a record whose class resolved to **Part**. Those
  are Humanoid attributes, so that record's class (and therefore its boundary)
  is being read from the wrong offset.
* `missing 0xA1 record boundary after record N` fires on the same frames, and
  `parseAppStateFrame` finds boundaries by *scanning for a plausible header*
  (`valid_header`) with an optional 4-byte `01 00 00 00`-ish separator. That is
  a heuristic standing in for a layout we do not know.

So `read_tagged_value` case 57 staying loud is correct: implementing a shape
for it would consume bytes that belong to a different section. The gap to
close is the **A1 record layout** — where the class id, member count and mask
really sit, and how a record's body is delimited — which needs the native
decoder (`libroblox.so`, the 0xA1 body parser) rather than more guesses.

**Diagnostics added for that work:** `parseAppStateBody` now hands back the
undecoded remainder on a hard failure too (not just on a partial), and
`RBX_DUMP_A1FAIL=<dir>` writes the first frame whose *record* failed
(`a1body_<len>.bin`), alongside the existing whole-frame dump.

## 2026-11-06 — same-job TX diff harness (tool done, native capture blocked)

**The comparator exists and is validated.** `tools/tx_diff.py --ours DIR
--native DIR` decodes both sides with `raknet_tx_table.decode`, reassembles
split reliable-ordered messages by (message number, split id) using the
`[u32 BE count][u16 BE id][u32 BE index]` payload prefix, keys each message by
(channel, ordering index), and reports: a per-id census for both sides, then
identical / byte-diff / length-diff / missing pairs with the differing offsets.
Exit status is 1 when anything differs, so a parity round can gate on it.
Self-diff on a live capture is clean: 3,849 datagrams → 370 messages + 3,443
ACKs, every pair identical.

Our side of a round is already captured: `RBX_RAKNET_TXDUMP=<dir>` writes the
pre-seal RakNet datagrams (raw: first byte `0x83`/`0xc0`), which is exactly the
input the comparator wants.

**Native side: two capture-script bugs found, neither sufficient.**
`tools/gdb/rc_capture.gdb` arms its hooks from the *first outgoing wire frame*,
and that frame is matched against a hard-coded RUPP header with total length
`0x1f`. The length varies per frame, so arming only happened when the first
frame was 31 bytes — a joined session could therefore finish with no hooks
armed at all (observed: `rkc.log` held only the probe line). Fixed to match
only the fixed fields (`01 00`, `01 11`). The second bug is the send path:
libroblox imports `sendmmsg` as well as `sendto`, and RakNet batches datagrams,
so a `sendto` breakpoint can stay silent for a whole session. Added
`h_sendmmsg` (mmsghdr 64B, `msg_iov` at +16, `msg_iovlen` at +24, 16B iovecs)
sharing the frame/arm logic.

Still no `ARMED` line and no `dgram_*.bin` with both hooks and a joined session
(`Joining game '36140de5-…'`). The probe does report libroblox mapped, so the
symbol breakpoints are being created but never taken. Most likely reason: this
runtime resolves its socket calls through the bundled bionic shim
(`~/Rum/build/libc.so`), not the glibc `sendto`/`sendmmsg` symbols gdb binds
before the inferior starts. Next attempt should break on the *shim* object
(`break libc.so:sendto`) or on the PLT stub inside libroblox for this build
(md5 30ecaff9, 0.744 — `tools/ida_addresses.md` maps a different build, so its
offsets are stale).

Both clients can be pinned to one job: the native prints `Joining game '<uuid>'`
and our client takes `--job-id <uuid>`.

## 2026-11-06 — native TX capture works; same-job byte diff; measured cadences

### The native capture never armed because libroblox does not call glibc
`tools/gdb/rc_capture.gdb` armed its send hooks with `break sendto` / `break
sendmmsg`, i.e. at the symbols gdb resolves when the inferior starts. libroblox
resolves socket calls through the APK's own bionic shim (`~/Rum/build/libc.so`),
so those breakpoints are created and never taken: a fully joined session logged
`ARMED` never and wrote zero `dgram_*.bin`.

Fix: hook the client's own PLT stubs, which execute whatever libc won the
lookup, with the caller's argument registers still intact. From IDA on this
build (`.plt` entries that jump through `.got.plt`):

    sendmmsg 0x6D86070   sendmsg 0x6D86340   sendto 0x6D86080
    recvfrom 0x6D86050   recvmmsg 0x6D86060

Arming also moved off the first outgoing frame (which required a hook that
could never fire) onto the `clock_gettime` probe.

### The launcher's base is the offset-0 mapping, not `lo - offset`
A probe log of the raw maps settled this:

    7fffc3cc0000-7fffcaa48000 rwxp 00000000   (0x6d88000 bytes, whole prefix)
    7fffcaa4b000-7fffcafc6000 rw-p 06d87000
    7fffcafc9000-7fffcb04d000 rw-p 07301000

`lo - offset` gives a different base per segment (0x4000 apart) because
p_vaddr != p_offset in this loader, so `stable_base()` never matched and
